## 2026-10-24 — Batch 121: Q4 derivation must pair facts by vintage — a restated annual pairs with an unrestated 9M only

**What:** `_derive_q4_income` (and the cash-flow un-cumulation in `_cash_flow_series_for_tag`) now derive Q4 from the contemporaneous pair when the year's annual fact was restated by a later filing but the 9M fact was not: the FY leg is replaced by the year's own-10-K value (earliest-filed 10-K annual fact for that start, `_own_tenk_annual_value`) while the 9M leg stays. When the 9M was itself restated (earliest-filed 9M value for that end, `_own_ytd_value`, differs from the latest-wins value), no substitution happens — the latest legs already pair consistently. The NI annual in the EPS share-implied path gets the same per-pair treatment. `_values_differ` fences split-rounding noise by comparing at the coarser filing precision.

**Why:** Latest-wins dedup pairs a restated FY with an unrestated 9M whenever a later 10-K restates a prior-year annual — and prior-year restatements turn out to be common, not exceptional. OXY is the demonstrated case (FY2024 Revenues $26,725M → $22,019M ex-OxyChem; 9M $19,965M untouched; old derivation $2,054M vs as-reported $6,760M), and the DB-wide pass found 757 such value diffs across 220 of 292 companies, including AAPL Q4 FY2008 (old NI $2,421M vs as-reported $1,136M; old diluted EPS $2.68 vs as-reported $1.26). The first, unrefined version of the rule substituted unconditionally — and the AAPL trace caught it over-firing: FY2013's annual AND 9M were both restated onto the post-split basis, so substituting the own-10-K annual mixed bases the other way and failed the coherence check, deleting a good derived row. The refined gate (annual-restated AND 9M-not-restated) is the correct pairing rule: it asks whether each leg is on the old or the new basis and only repairs the mixed pairs. Split-rounding tolerance is needed because the split rescale leaves the own-10-K value a rounding step from the filed latest value (NFLX 7.19/10 = 0.719 vs filed 0.72); the blind spot is bounded at half the coarser precision unit (≤5¢ for EPS, $0 for integer dollars) and the latest value is the corrected one inside it. The break years are untouched by design: the batch-85/120 guard runs first and abstains there, because the break year's own 10-K is already on the new basis, so no contemporaneous pair exists.

**Blast-radius method:** full DB-wide old-vs-new comparison (new rule vs `_own_tenk_annual_value` patched to None) over all 292 companies × 5 metrics, with fp-label changes separated from value changes (a derived-row gain/loss relabels a filed Q4 stub FY↔Q4 at an identical value — 25 such labels, not value changes). 757 value diffs, 10 added/removed derived rows, 0 fetch errors. Repair scope decided by joining diff ends against stored rows: only 8 tickers had in-window diffs (AEP, AJG, BAC, COIN, CRWD, LLY, NOW, OXY), all re-seeded through the production path; the other 749 diffs sit outside stored windows and self-correct on each ticker's next seed. 8 new regression tests (OXY pair, cash-flow pair, both-restated keeps-latest ×2, annual-restated-only EPS, split-rounding ×2, no-own-10-K unchanged); 1005-test suite green; `git diff --check` clean; live routes green with OXY detail rendering 6760000000.0 and no 2054 remnant.

**Related:** Closes the batch-120 OXY mixed-basis follow-up. Still open: STZ table-row wart; CB "Core" 5-line wraps; ABNB heading-less metric table; CINF subsidiary-list prefix + trailing quote fragment in highlights (cosmetic); MTB address-line prefix in highlights; pending-filing cohort at 26 + VRSK Q2 2026; partial residuals incl. PEG 6/8; Wikipedia 403s; table-header sections winning when a release has no bullets at all. New: the live pipeline applies only the 2020 4:1 split rescale to AAPL's pre-2014 per-share facts (single detected event; pre-2014 quarters sit 7× above the current basis) — a multi-split detection batch is open, and batch 121 deliberately keeps each series internally consistent on the split machinery's basis rather than fixing that here. Accepted limitation: a 9M restated in a later 10-Q during the window before the next 10-K restates the annual leaves a temporarily mixed pair (no substitution when the FY is unrestated); it self-heals on the next re-seed. Accepted residual risk of the refined guard: a component acquired and discontinued within a single fiscal year leaves no prior-year comparative to restate, so the restatement proof cannot catch it — that shape requires a same-year acquire-and-dispose, and every demonstrated reclassification does restate comparatives.

## 2026-10-24 — Batch 122: the 7-for-1 split ratio joins the detector; split corroboration must agree on one ratio

**What:** (1) `_SPLIT_RATIOS` in `data/xbrl.py` gains 7 (AAPL's 7-for-1 split, 2014-06-09). (2) `_detect_split_events` now filters corroborating votes to those within `_SPLIT_RATIO_TOLERANCE` of the median scale and requires at least `_SPLIT_MIN_CORROBORATING_GROUPS` agreeing votes; the event's scale is the median of the agreeing cluster and its `period_ends` cover only agreeing periods.

**Why:** (1) The batch-121 multi-split note was exactly this gap: AAPL's diluted-EPS facts carry 10 restatement pairs at 1/7 (0.1427–0.1432, filed 2014-07-23 through 2015-04-28) that the detector could never see, so pre-2014 facts rescaled by the 2020 4:1 event alone — 4x instead of 28x. NFLX's July-2015 7:1 split was invisible for the same reason. (2) The naive fix alone shipped a false positive: CRM's Aug-2018 10-Q restated Q2 quarterly basic EPS 0.02 → 0.06 (3x) and 6M basic EPS 0.01 → 0.07 (7x) — tiny rounded values, no split — and the old median-of-all-votes rule fired a 7.0 event on those two disagreeing votes, which would have inflated CRM's 2008–2011 basic EPS 7x. A real split restates every comparative at one ratio, so disagreement is disproof, not corroboration. The median-cluster form (rather than all-votes-must-agree) keeps genuine events robust to one stray rounding vote: a stray ratio outside tolerance cannot kill a 5-vote cluster, and cannot join it either.

**Blast-radius method:** DB-wide old-vs-new EPS basic/diluted series over all 292 companies (same script shape as batch 121): with both fixes, 82 value diffs — AAPL 36 (all pre-2014 ends, now ÷28, e.g. Q3 FY2008 basic 1.21 → 0.043) and NFLX 46 (2015 7:1 at 0.1449 + 2025 10:1 at 0.1001, both verified as clean single-ratio clusters) — CRM clean, no other ticker changed. The pre-fix blast (ratio only) showed the CRM damage explicitly (15 diffs, all ×7 upward) and is what forced the second fix. All 82 diffs sit outside stored 8-quarter windows, so no re-seed was needed. 3 new regression tests (AAPL 7:1 end-to-end, CRM mixed-ratio rejection, agreeing-cluster-with-stray control); 1008-test suite green; `git diff --check` clean; live routes green.

**Related:** Closes the batch-121 multi-split open item. Still open: STZ table-row wart; CB "Core" 5-line wraps; ABNB heading-less metric table; CINF/MTB cosmetic prefixes; pending-filing cohort at 26 + VRSK Q2 2026; partial residuals incl. PEG 6/8; Wikipedia 403s; table-header sections winning when a release has no bullets at all; the batch-121 transient-window limitation.

## 2026-10-25 — Batch 123: a flattened two-column contact table is not a headline — skip it by its role-label row

**What:** New `_skip_contact_table` in `data/filings.py`, called from the headline pass just before the final pick, plus a doubled-role-label alternative in `_CONTACT_HEADER_RE`. The skip fires when: the next line is a role-label row (one or more of investor/media/corporate-communications/press/public relations, optional colon); the candidate line is short (≤6 words), has no terminal punctuation, and contains no headline word (reports/announces/results/quarter/fiscal/earnings/revenue); and a headline-shaped line appears within 8 lines past table-continuation rows (repeated phrase via new `_is_repeated_phrase`, company-name line, admin/contact line, or short label line). Otherwise it returns None and the original behavior holds exactly.

**Why:** Akamai's Q4 releases are the demonstrated case: the full-year release puts a two-column contact table before the title (names row / roles row / company row / wrapped title), while Q1–Q3 put the title first — so only the two Q4 quarters broke, both storing contact names as headlines. Every existing guard misses the names row for a different reason: it is 4 words and headline-shaped (so the bare-masthead skip's ≤3-word, non-headline-shaped requirements fail), and there is no header line for the contact-header branch (the roles row is "Media Relations Investor Relations", a doubled label the old header regex could not match). The three guards are each load-bearing: the role-row lookahead is the table's signature (a real headline is never followed by a role-label row); the no-headline-word rule keeps a genuine verbless headline safe even if a role line somehow follows it; and the must-find-a-headline-ahead rule means a table whose headline never arrives swallows nothing. The doubled-label header alternative is added to `_CONTACT_HEADER_RE` itself (not just the new skip) so the lede fallback and every other header-branch caller also read the shape as a contact header.

**Blast-radius method:** DB-wide scan for the defect shape (stored highlights body opening with a role-label row) over all 2370 quarters with highlights: exactly 2 rows, both AKAM Q4 — the defect is fully contained, no silent cohort. Direct re-extraction from exhibit HTML (the backfill path skips filled rows, so it cannot measure an extractor change) over AKAM + AAPL/ROK/MTB/WDC/FSLR: exactly 2 attributable diffs, both AKAM Q4 improvements; ROK/MTB/WDC/FSLR byte-identical; AAPL's 8 diffs are pre-existing 1-char whitespace drift (stored len 1396 vs both pre- and post-123 extractor len 1395, proven via a stashed pre-123 extraction — the stored rows predate current `_html_to_text` spacing), deliberately left untouched rather than churning stored rows for invisible whitespace. Live repair overwrote only the 2 AKAM rows (new differs and non-None guard, batch-118 pattern); the other 6 AKAM quarters re-extracted identical. 7 new regression tests (AKAM Q4 end-to-end, doubled-role header, repeated-phrase unit, skip index, and three negatives: verb headline, no headline ahead, no role row); 1015-test suite green; `git diff --check` clean; live quarter-specific detail/data renders show both repaired headlines with bullet blocks and zero contact-name remnants.

**Related:** Closes the AKAM contact-name headline defect found by seed round 42 (LULU, WDC, FSLR, ENPH, TTD, AKAM — DB at 298). Still open: STZ table-row wart; CB "Core" 5-line wraps; ABNB heading-less metric table; CINF/MTB cosmetic prefixes; LULU Q3 FY2024/FY2025 section-header headline ("EARNINGS RELEASE & STOCK REPURCHASE PROGRAM" — needs raw-document investigation); pending-filing cohort at 26 + VRSK Q2 2026; partial residuals incl. PEG 6/8; Wikipedia 403s; table-header sections winning when a release has no bullets at all; the batch-121 transient-window limitation.

## 2026-10-25 — Batch 124: a compound earnings-release document title is not the headline — skip it by its exhibit marker

**What:** New `_is_compound_earnings_title` in `data/filings.py`, called from the headline pass just before the bare-label branch, plus a dedicated `_EXHIBIT_MARKER_RE`. The skip fires when: the line carries "earnings release" followed by "&" or "and"; an exhibit-marker line ("Exhibit 99.1" / "EXHIBIT 99.1" / "EX-99.1") appears within the next 2 lines; and the line immediately after that marker is headline-shaped and carries a headline verb (announces/reports).

**Why:** lululemon's Q3 releases are the demonstrated case: the combined earnings-release + buyback 8-K titles its exhibit "EARNINGS RELEASE & STOCK REPURCHASE PROGRAM", and that title sits before "Exhibit 99.1" while the real headline ("LULULEMON ATHLETICA INC. ANNOUNCES THIRD QUARTER FISCAL 2024/2025 RESULTS") follows it — so both Q3 quarters stored the document title. Q4 was immune because its title is the bare "EARNINGS RELEASE", which the label branch already skips. The three guards are each load-bearing: the "&"/"and" requirement separates compound titles from bare labels and results headlines; the marker lookahead means a compound title with no marker after it keeps old behavior; and the verb-headline requirement is what protects JPMorgan — its "EARNINGS RELEASE - THIRD QUARTER 2025 RESULTS" title is also followed by an exhibit marker, but the line after the marker is the company name/address block, not a verb headline, so JPM's 8 stored headlines are untouched (proven by re-extraction: JPM headlines identical pre/post-124, and its body drift is pre-existing, proven via a stashed pre-124 extraction). A regex bug in the first version (`\b` after `&`, which can never match since "&" followed by a space has no word boundary) was caught by the new tests and fixed to `(&|and\b)`.

**Blast-radius method:** DB-wide scan for the defect shape (stored headline containing "earnings release" with "&"/"and") over all 2370 quarters with highlights: exactly 2 rows, both LULU Q3 — fully contained, no silent cohort. Direct re-extraction from exhibit HTML over LULU + JPM/XOM/AKAM/AAPL/ROK/MTB/WDC/FSLR: exactly 2 attributable diffs, both LULU Q3 headline improvements (bodies otherwise identical); XOM/AKAM/ROK/MTB/WDC/FSLR byte-identical; JPM body diffs and AAPL 1–2-char diffs proven pre-existing (stash comparison for JPM, batch-123 proof for AAPL) and deliberately left untouched. Live repair overwrote only the 2 LULU rows (new differs and non-None guard, batch-118 pattern); the other 6 LULU quarters re-extracted identical. 4 new regression tests (LULU Q3 end-to-end, "&"/"and" forms, helper guard negatives, JPM-shape preservation); 1019-test suite green; `git diff --check` clean; live quarter-specific detail/data renders show both repaired headlines with zero doc-title remnants.

**Related:** Closes the LULU Q3 headline defect carried from seed round 42 (batch 123). Still open: STZ table-row wart; CB "Core" 5-line wraps; ABNB heading-less metric table; CINF/MTB cosmetic prefixes; XOM doc-title headlines ("3Q 2025 Earnings Release" with the real "ExxonMobil Announces..." headline unpicked — title-after-marker shape, needs its own rule); pending-filing cohort at 26 + VRSK Q2 2026; partial residuals incl. PEG 6/8; Wikipedia 403s; table-header sections winning when a release has no bullets at all; the batch-121 transient-window limitation.

## 2026-10-25 — Batch 125: slide-deck bullet-run mega-lines split into pseudo-lines; deck titles are never headlines

**What:** New `_split_bullet_runs` in `data/filings.py`, applied at the top of `extract_press_release_highlights` before any headline/section/lede logic, plus a slide-deck-title branch on `_NEVER_HEADLINE_RE`. A line longer than the 300-char headline cap with at least two " • " separators splits into one pseudo-line per bullet; continuation pieces keep a "• " prefix so downstream bullet logic treats them as bullets. The never-headline branch matches a line starting with a page number + quarter token + "Financial Highlights".

**Why:** Bank of America's releases are slide decks: `_html_to_text` flattens a whole highlights slide into one ~7,000-char line with bullets joined by " • ". The headline loop saw a single over-cap line, picked no headline, and the batch-105 page-line guard discarded the release — BAC was blank on all 8 quarters, the only whole-company blank left. Splitting restores the bullets the rest of the pipeline already knows how to handle. The deck-title branch is load-bearing: without it the split's first piece ("1 2Q26 Financial Highlights… Business Segment Highlights… Consumer Banking") became the headline. The "• " prefix on continuations is load-bearing in the other direction: the batch-115 bullet-headline strip is what turns the lead bullet into a clean headline, and the lede's bullet handling is what keeps the body as bullets rather than table fragments.

**Deliberately not done:** exact recovery of BAC's true "Bank of America Reports…" headline. It is glued to sub-heads and endnotes inside the final bullet piece with no sentence boundary ("…Up 34% YoY 2Q26 Revenue Up 15% YoY…"), so extracting it exactly would need BAC-specific surgery; the lead-bullet headline (Palantir precedent) plus genuine segment bullets is honest and informative. Also not added: a bullet skip in the headline loop — bullet-as-headline is tested Palantir/Skydance behavior, and skipping bullets would regress those cases. EOG's 7 blank quarters were investigated in the same audit and are a different defect class (combined release + supplement packet whose TOC entries escape the SPG TOC rule, landing extraction on footnote fragments) — left for its own batch rather than stretched into this one.

**Blast-radius method:** the split only fires on >300-char lines with ≥2 " • " separators, so prose releases are byte-identical by construction; the never-headline branch only matches page-number + token + "Financial Highlights" lines. 3 new regression tests (split unit behavior, BAC-shaped end-to-end extraction, deck-title narrowness); 1021-test suite green; `git diff --check` clean. Live repair backfilled all 8 BAC quarters (blank cohort 25 → 17); /company/BAC overview and detail/data both 200 with the backfilled highlights rendering.

**Related:** Closes the BAC whole-company blank found by the batch-125 blank-highlights audit. Still open: EOG 7/8 blank (TOC + supplement packet); SKYD 4 blank (structurally unextractable per batch 48); PEG 2 + AFRM/CCI/LIN/PCAR 1 blank each; STZ table-row wart; CB "Core" 5-line wraps; ABNB heading-less metric table; CINF/MTB cosmetic prefixes; XOM doc-title headlines (title-after-marker shape); pending-filing cohort at 28; Wikipedia 403s; table-header sections winning when a release has no bullets at all; the batch-121 transient-window limitation.

## 2026-10-25 — Batch 126: a footnote definition or a table of contents is never press-release highlights — result-level guards, not headline-loop skips

**What:** Two result-level guards in `extract_press_release_highlights` (after headline cleanup, before truncation): (1) a headline matching `_FOOTNOTE_HEADLINE_RE` (`^\([A-Za-z0-9]\)` — a single letter/digit in parentheses) returns None; (2) a headline whose body consists entirely (≥2 lines) of title + trailing page-number lines (`_TOC_ENTRY_RE`, `^.+ \d{1,3}$`) returns None.

**Why:** EOG Resources' exhibits are combined release + supplement packets whose narrative text lives in full-page images; the text layer is only the supplement TOC and tables. Six exhibits land on a footnote definition as the headline (the SPG TOC rule over-consumes tables and stops at the first >140-char line, a footnote legend); Q3 FY2024 lands on the TOC subtitle with TOC entries as the body (only one standalone page line, so the TOC rule never fires). The guards are result-level deliberately: skipping footnote lines inside the headline loop would not fix anything — a 700-line table document always yields some pick (a table heading, a units line, a financial row), so the loop would just move the garbage. Only a document-level "this result is not highlights" decision yields the honest blank. The single-character footnote bound is load-bearing: "(NYSE: ACME)" and "(2026)" parentheticals are not footnote markers (the character after the first is not ")"), so exchange- and year-prefixed headlines are untouched; the all-lines TOC bound is load-bearing in the other direction, since a real lede never consists entirely of title + page-number lines.

**Blast-radius method:** DB-wide scans over all 2378 stored highlight rows for both guard shapes: exactly 1 footnote-headline row (EOG Q1 FY2026 — cleared to NULL in the live repair) and 0 TOC-entry bodies, so no other stored row changes. Post-fix live re-extraction over all 8 EOG exhibits returns None on every quarter. 5 new regression tests (both EOG shapes end-to-end; NYSE- and year-prefixed headline preservation; normal release with numbered lede lines preserved); 1026-test suite green; `git diff --check` clean; /company/EOG overview + detail 200 with the Q1 FY2026 garbage gone.

**Related:** Closes the EOG follow-up carried from batch 125 (with a corrected mechanism: TOC over-consumption + under-firing, not simply "not skipped"). EOG is now honestly 8/8 blank — its image-page narrative is structurally unextractable short of OCR, same class as SKYD per batch 48. Still open: STZ table-row wart; CB "Core" 5-line wraps; ABNB heading-less metric table; CINF/MTB cosmetic prefixes; XOM doc-title headlines (title-after-marker shape); SKYD 4 + PEG 2 + AFRM/CCI/LIN/PCAR 1 blank each; pending-filing cohort at 28 + VRSK Q2 2026; Wikipedia 403s; table-header sections winning when a release has no bullets at all; the batch-121 transient-window limitation; a bounded `_skip_toc` fix (its over-consumption is now harmless given these guards, so it stays open rather than risking SPG).

## 2026-10-25 — Batch 128: a 99.1 exhibit marker followed directly by a quarter-end date run is the release

**What:** `_release_name_rank`'s "991" infix lookahead gains a date-run alternative (`\d{6,8}` followed by end-of-stem or a separator) in `services/ir_download.py`.

**Why:** Cognizant furnishes its earnings release as `exhibit9916302026.htm` — 99.1 plus the quarter-end date with no separator — and no existing rule matched it: the tier-0 ex99 regex's digit guard exists to keep 99.2+ supplements out, and it rejects any 99.1 marker whose "1" is followed by a digit, which is exactly what a date run does. All 8 CTSH quarters therefore discovered their 2.02 8-Ks but found no exhibit, landing 0/8 despite clean, extractable releases. The date-run bound is what keeps the rule honest: a 6–8-digit run is a date (MDDYYYY/MMDDYYYY), while the `doc9912final` shape (1 digit) and 5-digit runs stay unmatched, and 992/993 supplements never contain 991 at all.

**Blast-radius method:** All 84 ir_download tests green (every prior exhibit convention unchanged); the new alternative fires only on "991" + 6–8-digit-date stems. Live repair backfilled CTSH 8/8 with real headlines; the detail/data route renders the repaired highlights.

## 2026-10-26 — Batch 129: a dated press-release document label is never the headline

**What:** New `_is_dated_press_release_label` in `data/filings.py`, called from the headline pass just after the batch-127 quarter-title branch, plus `_DATED_PRESS_RELEASE_LABEL_RE`. The skip fires when the line matches "<prefix> (EARNINGS) PRESS RELEASE,? DATED <full month date>" at end of line and stays within 140 chars.

**Why:** Altria's exhibits put a document label between the exhibit header and the "Exhibit 99.1" marker — "ALTRIA GROUP, INC. EARNINGS PRESS RELEASE, DATED OCTOBER 31, 2024" — so all 8 MO quarters stored the label instead of the real headline. No earlier rule caught it: the masthead branch requires a corporate-suffix ending (Inc./Corp./Co.), and the label ends with a date; the batch-124 compound-title rule needs an "&/and" continuation, and batch 127's quarter-title rule needs an exact "<digit>Q <year>" shape. The date anchor is the whole discriminator and needs no marker lookbehind/lookahead, unlike 124/127: a genuine headline never ends with "DATED <full date>", and the label precedes its marker in both MO variants (with and without "EARNINGS"/comma), so a marker condition would only narrow the rule against its own evidence. The 140-char bound is load-bearing in the other direction: a prose sentence that happens to end "... press release, dated <date>" is not swallowed.

**Blast-radius method:** DB-wide scan for a dated-label first paragraph over all 2424 stored highlight rows: exactly the 8 MO rows — fully contained, no silent cohort. ## 2026-10-26 — Batch 130: a deck-title page is never the headline; the highlights heading after it is

**What:** A headline-loop branch in `data/filings.py` before the never-headline check, plus `_DECK_KEY_FINANCIAL_DATA_RE` (`^[1234]Q\d{2} Key Financial Data$`) added to `_NEVER_HEADLINE_RE` as a backstop. When the title line is followed by a highlights heading (≤120 chars, `_HIGHLIGHTS_HEADING_RE`), the heading becomes the headline and `body_start` is the heading's own index.

**Why:** U.S. Bancorp's exhibits are slide decks: the title page "<NQ>YY Key Financial Data" sits immediately before the highlights section heading, and the loop picked the title page because no skip matched it — batch 125's deck rule covers only BAC's page-number-prefixed shape. The two-part design is load-bearing in both directions. The branch's body_start-at-heading is what keeps the repair headline-only: the section extractor finds the heading at body_lines[0] and returns its content, so the body is byte-identical to the stored one (proven per row on all 8 quarters, not assumed). A naive skip — headline = heading, body after it — was traced and rejected: without the heading in body_lines, the section anchor is lost, the leading-bullet block finds a table line instead of bullets, and the body falls back to the later "Business and Other Highlights" section, losing the financial bullets. The never-headline backstop covers decks where no highlights heading follows the title, so the title is never a headline in any case; the full-line anchor keeps Lam Research's "Key Financial Data for the Quarters Ended ..." prose heading untouched, and TFC's body mention (headline found first) is unaffected.

**Blast-radius method:** DB-wide scan for a deck-title first paragraph over all 2424 stored highlight rows: exactly the 8 USB rows — fully contained, no silent cohort. Direct re-extraction over all 8 USB exhibits: exactly 8 attributable diffs, all headline repairs with bodies byte-identical. Controls TFC/LRCX/BAC 8/8 identical. JPM's 8 diffs were investigated, not assumed attributable: the pre-change code (via stash comparison) produces the identical live output, so JPM's stored rows are stale relative to the current extractor for a pre-existing reason — a re-extraction sweep of its own, deliberately not folded into this batch. 4 new regression tests (USB Q3 end-to-end, USB 2Q26 heading shape, no-following-heading backstop, regex guards); 1039-test suite green; `git diff --check` clean; live repair overwrote only the 8 USB rows (DB is git-ignored, so the repair lives in the local DB and re-seeds reproduce it via the fixed extractor); /company/USB overview + detail + detail/data all 200 with zero title remnants.

## 2026-10-26 — Batch 132: a pending-filing recheck must test only the missing end — a hit on an already-stored end is not a landing, and a zero-landing recheck writes nothing

**What:** No code change. Rechecked all 29 pending-filing-note companies against live companyfacts using `_revenue_duration_facts` (revenue-chain duration facts only, the note logic's own evidence), and wrote nothing: zero landings.

**Why:** Two method points, both learned the hard way inside this batch. First, the watch set must contain only missing ends: the first script also watched 2026-03-31, which "hit" on 27 tickers — but 2026-03-31 is the stored Q1 quarter on those rows, so those hits are the status quo, not landings. Only the missing end (2026-06-30 for the cohort) counts, and it hit nowhere. A future recheck that reuses a stored end as a watch end will manufacture 27 false landings. Second, fiscal calendars break the calendar assumption: GD's Q2 10-Q (filed Jul 29, 2026) carries report date 2026-07-05, so a 2026-06-30-only watch set silently skips GD — the targeted GD probe (revenue ends in 2026: only 2026-04-05) is what proves GD still lagged, not the cohort-wide pass. The zero-landing outcome is itself the discipline: the recheck's job is to find landings, and when it finds none, the correct repair is no repair — writing "refreshed" rows would churn the DB for no user-visible change, and the notes are proven current (they still describe the live state: ABT's note renders on its overview today).

**Blast-radius method:** Read-only companyfacts recheck over all 29 cohort tickers (0 fetch failures); GD targeted probe for its fiscal end; live overview checks on ABT/GD/DOW (200, ABT note rendering). No DB writes, no code diff. 1039-test suite green; `git diff --check` clean.

## 2026-10-26 — Batch 134: a " n "-joined company list is a masthead, and the proof is the suffix on every segment — no lookahead, no following-line check

The suffix-every-segment rule is a self-sufficient discriminator: a flattened table row is exactly a list of company names, a prose line containing " n " fails the suffix rule on at least one segment, and a real headline never consists solely of " n "-joined company names — so the rule needs no marker lookbehind, no following-line check, and no company-name match, the mirror of batch 133's ZIP+date rule, which also stands on its ending anchor alone. ## 2026-10-27 — Batch 135: a headline ending on a modifier word is a wrap fragment, and the proof is that the word cannot complete a noun phrase — but a fragment tail is the weakest trigger, so it yields to a fresh headline

**What:** Three additions to `_join_wrapped_headline` in `data/filings.py`: `_HEADLINE_FRAGMENT_TAIL_RE` (headline ends on Core/Record/Consolidated/Life/Net/Operating/Combined/Prior/P&C/Up); `_HEADLINE_PCT_LEAD_RE` (a percentage-led continuation bypasses the digit-led bad-start guard only after a fragment or wrap tail); the join cap rises from 3 to 12; and a fresh-headline block (a fragment-tail join is refused when the continuation is itself a "Name Verbs …" headline).

**Why:** Chubb's headline paragraph wraps at arbitrary points, and the wrap lands on words the old tail set never covered because the old set was built from punctuation and verbs, not from phrase structure. The fragment set is principled rather than CB-shaped: each word is a modifier or preposition that cannot end a complete headline noun phrase — "Reports Record" is a fragment of "Record … Income", "Reports P&C" of "P&C Combined Ratio", "… and Life" of "Life Insurance", "… Up" of "Up 34.6%". The percentage bypass is equally narrow: a digit-led line is a metric-table row in general, but a percentage-led line after a fragment or wrap tail is the figure completing the fragment — the tail is what distinguishes the two, the mirror of batch 108's year-bypass, which also keyed the bypass to the tail rather than the line. The cap rise follows from the same paragraph shape: CB's longest paragraph needs 10 joins, and capping at 3 would have left every quarter half-repaired; the cap is safe because every join still requires a trigger plus all continuation guards, so a runaway needs 12 consecutive fragment-shaped lines, and the 144-quarter old-vs-new run shows exactly 12 diffs, all CB.

**Deliberately not done:** applying the fresh-headline block to all triggers — Merck's "Merck & Co., Inc., Rahway, N.J.," / "USA Announces …" wrap must still join, and there the verb-led continuation is the second half of the same headline, not a new one; the block is fragment-tail-only because the fragment tail is the weakest signal and the only one a cover/slogan line can trip (Danaher's "Innovation at the speed of life" ends on "life" — a slogan, not a wrap — and the pre-guard run proved the failure mode on all 12 DHR quarters before the guard removed it). Also not done: an "Insurance"-tail rule for CB Q4 2024's last wrap ("…and Life Insurance" / "Up 15.7%…") — "Insurance" at the end of a headline is far more plausibly complete ("Reports Life Insurance") than "Core" or "Up", so it stays a follow-up rather than a rule added on one quarter's evidence; likewise no "Both"-lead rule for Q3 2024 ("…Net Investment Income" / "Both Up Double-Digit…"), where the continuation is a sentence start, not a fragment. No template change: the defect is extractor-side, so no screenshot gate applies.

**Blast-radius method:** DB-wide scan for fragment-tail headlines over all 2425 stored highlight rows: exactly 20 — CB 8 (repaired), DHR 8 (slogan, unchanged by design), ABNB 4 (shareholder-letter table fragments, a path the joiner never runs in). Old-vs-new extraction via `git stash` over 144 quarters (CB, DHR, ABNB, AAPL, MSFT, JPM, MTB, XOM, CHD, CINF, LULU, AKAM): exactly 12 diffs, all CB headline improvements, 0 elsewhere. 4 new regression tests (CB Q2 2024 end-to-end; "Up" + percentage chain; guard negatives; DHR slogan block + Merck exemption); 1047-test suite green; `git diff --check` clean; live repair gated per row (old headline must end on a fragment tail, new headline must start with the old headline and be longer) — all 8 stored CB rows passed, 0 skipped-with-row, no inserts for the 4 quarters without stored rows; /company/CB overview + detail + detail/data all 200 with the repaired Q1 2026 headline rendering.

**Related:** Closes the CB "Core" follow-up carried from batch 120. Still open: CB Q3 2024 "Both" wrap and Q4 2024 "Insurance" wrap (with its dropped tail clause); STZ table-row wart; ABNB heading-less metric table; EOG/SKYD honest blanks; PEG/AFRM/CCI/LIN/PCAR blanks; pending-filing cohort at 29; Wikipedia 403s; table-header sections winning on bullet-less releases; the batch-121 transient-window limitation; a bounded `_skip_toc` fix.

## 2026-10-27 — Batch 136: a "for"-led continuation is a headline fragment only when "for" is followed by a period designator — a bare "for" lead is never a trigger

**What:** A headline-loop trigger in `data/filings.py` — `_HEADLINE_FOR_LEAD_RE` — for continuation lines matching `^for\s+(?!the\b)(?:ordinal-quarter|bare-year-$)`. Moody's ("FOR THIRD QUARTER 2025"), FICO ("for Fourth Quarter Fiscal 2025"), Kroger ("for 2026"), and Ross ("FOR FOURTH QUARTER AND FISCAL 2024") headlines now join their period phrase; the stored rows (13 FOR + 24 stale AND rows) were repaired, 23 by full re-extraction and 14 by headline-splice.

**Why:** The conjunction-lead rule from batch 107 covered the mirror shape (conjunction leading the second line) but "for" is different from "and" in one load-bearing way: "and" never starts a lede sentence, while "For ..." starts ledes all the time — so the trigger cannot be the word alone, it must be the word plus the period designator that never starts a lede sentence. The three guard shapes in the stored DB prove the boundary empirically: Realty Income's and Intuit's "For the three months/first quarter ..." lines (colon-terminated labels), Lowe's "For 6:00 a.m. ET Release" (admin), and the hypothetical "For 2026, the company expects ..." outlook sentence — the first two are excluded by the `(?!the\b)` guard and the colon guard, the third by the $-anchored bare year. The blast scan over 216 quarters shows exactly the 13 FOR diffs and nothing else, which is the same standard batch 135's fragment rule met (12 diffs, all intended).

**Deliberately not done:** adding "for" to `_HEADLINE_CONT_LEAD_RE` outright (that would have joined Lowe's admin line and any "For the ..." lede that lost its colon); a standalone "fiscal"/"full-year" alternative in the regex (no stored evidence — every fiscal mention rides on an ordinal-quarter line, and "For fiscal 2026, ..." is a live lede-sentence shape, so it stays out until a real wrapped instance appears); forcing the 14 body-divergent rows through full re-extraction (UNH Q2 would have lost its CEO-quote tail, UPS would have gained a re-spaced "*", ARCC/MCO would have swapped section picks — the splice fixes exactly the headline and preserves exactly the body, and the body drift is recorded as its own follow-up rather than laundered through a headline repair). No template change: the defect is extractor-side, so no screenshot gate applies.

**Blast-radius method:** DB-wide scan for for-led body-first lines over all 2425 stored highlight rows: 37 lines — 13 genuine continuations (repaired), 24 colon/admin "For the ..."/"For 6:00 ..." lines (guarded out, untouched). Old-vs-new extraction via `git stash` over 216 quarters (29 tickers): exactly 13 diffs, all FOR headline improvements, 0 elsewhere. 5 new regression tests (MCO end-to-end; FICO, ROST, KR joins; 5 guard negatives); 1053-test suite green; `git diff --check` clean; live repair gated per row (23 FULL: live body content-lines prefix-match stored body; 14 SPLICE: new headline + stored body byte-preserved, asserted in-script) — 37 rows written, post-repair scan finds exactly 1 cohort row left (CART, live None + genuine sentence, skipped by design); /company/MCO, /FICO, /UNH, /NKE overview + detail/data all 200 with joined headlines rendering.

## 2026-10-27 — Batch 137: "Income" is not a bare headline-fragment word — it joins only onto a "Both"-led continuation; "Insurance" joins the bare list

**What:** Two Chubb headline-wrap fixes in `data/filings.py`. "Insurance" joins `_HEADLINE_FRAGMENT_TAIL_RE` (Q4 2024: "Life Insurance" / "Up 15.7%; P&C Combined Ratio of 86.6%"). Carnival's "NET INCOME" headlines are followed by subhead lines ("Accelerates shareholder returns, surpassing $450 million in stock repurchases", "Record bookings provide a strong foundation for 2027") — fragment-shaped (no terminal punctuation, under 140 chars) but not continuations — and PSEG's "$3.54 PER SHARE NET INCOME" is followed by a sibling metric line ("$3.68 PER SHARE NON-GAAP OPERATING EARNINGS"), which is a parallel headline, not a wrap. The difference between those and Chubb's Q3 line is the continuation itself: "Both" is a pronoun with an antecedent in the headline (the two income figures), so "Both Up Double-Digit" cannot stand alone as a subhead or a sibling metric — while "Accelerates ..." and "$3.68 PER SHARE ..." can. The conjunctive rule is the narrowest statement of the actual defect shape, and it is what the blast verifies: broad-"Income" gives 6 diffs (2 intended + 4 over-joins), conjunctive gives exactly 2. "Insurance" needed no such narrowing — across 264 blast quarters including 8 insurers, no genuine "Insurance"-ending headline is followed by a fragment-shaped non-continuation, and a complete headline ending on "Insurance" with a following subhead does not appear in the corpus.

## 2026-10-27 — Batch 138: a lone all-caps word in the lede is a heading tail, and a leading lone bullet marker is not content — both must not consume lede budget

**What:** Two skips in the lede fallback of `extract_press_release_highlights` in `data/filings.py` — `_is_lede_heading_fragment` (single all-caps word with a letter) and `_is_lone_bullet_marker` (a line that is only ●/•/-/∗), the latter applied only while no lede line has been collected yet.

**Why:** Chubb's Q4 2024 exhibit flattens its bullet list as a table-split section heading ("QUARTER", the tail of "Fourth Quarter") followed by lone "●" markers, one per bullet. The lede fallback spent two of its six lines on that junk, so the live body showed bullet 1 plus a dangling "●" and cut bullet 2 mid-item — while the stored row held both bullets complete. The fix's evidence is unusually clean: the fixed live extraction for Q4 2024 is byte-identical to the stored row, which proves both that the stored row was right and that the fix reproduces it. The two rules are deliberately asymmetric: the heading-fragment skip applies anywhere in the lede (a mid-lede "YEAR" tail from the full-year section is the same junk), while the marker skip applies only at the lead — a mid-lede marker is the only separator between bullets in the rendered paragraphs, and dropping it would merge bullets. The single-word bound on the fragment rule is load-bearing: a two-word all-caps line could be a company name or a short label with content, while a single all-caps word can never be a sentence; digit-only lines ("2024") are excluded by the letter guard, and "Q4" counts as a fragment because a bare quarter label is a section label, never prose.

**Deliberately not done:** raising the 6-line lede cap (the cap is fine once junk stops consuming it — the Q4 body now fits exactly); applying the skips to the section path or `_leading_bullet_block` (the defect lives in the lede fallback, and widening the fix would change paths with their own guard logic); taking CB Q3 2024's 2 extra trailing bullets through this repair (the batch-137 splice deliberately preserved Q3's body; the trailing-bullet drift is a body-coverage question, not a junk-line question, and stays carried). No template change: the defect is extractor-side, so no screenshot gate applies.

**Blast-radius method:** Old-vs-new extraction via `git stash` over 360 quarters (46 tickers): exactly 7 diffs, all CB, zero content lines lost (removed lines are only blanks, "QUARTER", "●"; added lines are bullet content or a repositioned mid marker). Per-row repair gates before every write: stored headline identical to live headline; every stored content paragraph present in the live body — all 6 rows passed, 0 skipped. ## 2026-10-27 — Batch 139: a lone bullet marker is a separator, not lede budget — and the finish-the-item rule only applies where items are delimited

**What:** Three changes to the lede fallback in `data/filings.py`. (1) `_is_lone_bullet_marker` gains "·" (middle dot) and "○" (white circle) — Chubb's Q3 2024 flattened markers, previously unrecognized, so the lead "·" was kept as a junk paragraph and every marker consumed a budget line. (2) Lone markers never consume the 6-content-line budget: at the lead they are skipped (batch 138's rule, extended), mid-lede they are appended as separator paragraphs. (3) In a marker-delimited lede, reaching the budget finishes the current item instead of cutting it mid-item: up to 4 continuation lines, and a marker at the boundary ends collection without being appended.

**Why:** The batch-138 follow-up said the Q3 trailing bullets were "body-coverage drift" and the cap "still truncates after 6 content lines, now junk-free" — but counting showed the truncation was never about the cap size. The Q3 lede spends its budget as lead-"·" (kept, because unrecognized) + 3 bullet-1 lines + "·" + bullet-2 line: 6 entries, 4 of them content. Removing the markers from the count without a finish rule would have ended the lede mid-bullet-4 ("...with"), so the finish rule and the marker rule are one fix, not two. The gate is the load-bearing part: the first version applied the finish rule to every lede, and the blast showed 109/232 diffs — prose ledes extending to the 4000-char cap, ARCC pulling table rows into highlights — for a defect that only exists in marker-delimited ledes. A prose lede has no item delimiters, so "finish the item" is undefined there, and the 6-line cap stays the bound. The gated version's 14 diffs are the defect's true footprint: CB (its lists flatten this way every quarter), GIS, and SPG.

**Deliberately not done:** raising the 6-line cap (the cap is fine once separators stop consuming it — Q3 now fits bullets 1–4, and the cap still bounds prose); applying the finish rule to prose ledes (see above — 109 diffs rejected on blast evidence, not on principle); repairing stored rows beyond the 14 blast diffs (a stored row that differs from live for another reason, e.g. older extraction or re-seed drift, is a separate question and must not be rewritten by this repair — the script restricts itself to diff keys); taking CB 2026-06-30 through the repair (no stored row exists yet; it extracts clean on next seed). No template change: the defect is extractor-side, so no screenshot gate applies.

**Blast-radius method:** Old-vs-new extraction over 232 quarters (29 tickers: CB, cohort, guards, insurers, banks, breadth) from cached exhibit HTML, so both runs see identical input. Ungated version: 109 diffs — rejected. Gated version: exactly 14 diffs, all with identical headlines; 13 are pure extensions ending on terminal punctuation (GIS's period-less tail matches its filer's house style — its bullets never end with periods); CB Q3 is a paragraph shift, not a loss: the only removed paragraph anywhere is the lead "·" itself, and every stored content paragraph appears in the new body in order. 6 new regression tests (CB Q3 end-to-end; boundary marker; 4-line finishing cap; prose hard cap; lead "·"/"○" skip; predicate negatives); 1074-test suite green; `git diff --check` clean; live repair gated per row (identical headline + stored content paragraphs in order) — 13 rows written, 0 skipped, 1 diff key (CB 2026-06-30) with no stored row; /company/CB overview + detail + detail/data all 200 with the repaired Q3 FY2024 body rendering bullets 1–4 complete.

**Related:** Closes the CB Q3 live trailing bullets (carried from batch 137 via batch 138). Still open: STZ table-row wart; ABNB heading-less metric table; CART genuine "And ..." sentence; ARCC table-section tail + MCO section-pick body divergence; EOG/SKYD honest blanks; PEG/AFRM/CCI/LIN/PCAR blanks; pending-filing cohort at 29; Wikipedia 403s; table-header sections winning on bullet-less releases; the batch-121 transient-window limitation; a bounded `_skip_toc` fix.

## 2026-10-27 — Batch 140: lone bullet markers are never stored, in any extraction path

**What:** Three changes to `data/filings.py`. (1) The lede loop no longer appends lone bullet markers — a marker still sets marker_seen, so the batch-139 finish rule and budget accounting are unchanged, but no marker paragraph is ever stored. (2) `_extract_highlights_sections` and `_leading_bullet_block` drop lone-marker lines AFTER qualification, so their caps (12 lines / 150 chars) and item-close logic see the same lines as before. (3) `_is_lone_bullet_marker` gains "▪" (black small square, PNC's lists) and "–" (en dash, Simon's lists) — the hyphen-minus was covered, the en dash was not.

**Why:** Batch 139's keep-as-separator choice rested on the theory that mid-lede markers separate bullets in the rendered output. The DB-wide audit falsified the premise: paragraphs are already blank-line separated, so a lone marker paragraph renders as a junk glyph line (42 lede-path rows) and a lone marker line inside a "\n"-joined section paragraph renders as a junk glyph line (50 section/leading-block rows). Dropping markers loses no separation anywhere — items stay separate paragraphs/lines — and the blast proves it: 73/144 diffs, every one a pure marker-line removal except SPG 2025-12-31, where recognizing "–" frees a budget line and admits one genuine FFO bullet (the batch-139 mechanism working as designed). This also supersedes batch 138's deliberately-not-done of applying the skips to the section path and leading block: that decision predated the audit showing those paths hold the majority of the junk (50 rows vs 42 lede rows).

**Deliberately not done:** importing live content through the repair (the repair only removes junk paragraphs/lines from stored rows — SPG 2025-12-31's budget-freed FFO line stays out of the stored row until a full re-extraction, same precedent as batch 137's untaken trailing bullets); rewriting the 27 + 3 rows the gate refused (PNC's stored bodies are table fragments the current extractor rejects; MET's page paragraph rides along with body drift; the rest differ from live by headline or content drift — a row that differs from live for another reason is a separate question, and the gate exists to refuse exactly that); touching UNH's inline "Page N of M <headline>" mega-lines (page-image exhibit shape — a headline-pick question, not a paragraph-drop). No template change: the defect is extractor-side, so no screenshot gate applies.

**Blast-radius method:** Old-vs-new extraction over 144 quarters (18 tickers: all marker/page cohort companies + AAPL/MSFT/JPM/XOM controls) from cached exhibit HTML, so both runs see identical input. Round 1 (pre-glyph-extension): 73 diffs, all pure marker removals, new text a subsequence of old everywhere, controls zero diffs. Round 2 (predicate + "▪"/"–"): same 73 keys, with SPG 2025-12-31 gaining exactly one genuine content line (the FFO bullet) and nothing else added anywhere. Repair gates per row (cleaned headline equals live headline + cleaned content paragraphs in order in live paragraphs): every gated row written across two rounds (round 2 wrote the final 2 after the glyph extension); 16 gate-failed + 14 uncovered rows left untouched and named in the status row. 6 new regression tests + 4 updated batch-138/139 expectations (the old keep-as-separator assertions now assert the opposite); 1080-test suite green; `git diff --check` clean; /company/SPG, CB, NUE, ON, NDAQ, VRSK, GIS overviews all 200 with zero glyph paragraphs rendered on the SPG overview.

**Related:** Supersedes batch 138's section/leading-block exclusion and batch 139's keep-as-separator choice. ## 2026-10-28 — Batch 141: a page marker glued to the headline is stripped; masthead paragraphs are dropped from stored rows

**What:** Two changes. (1) In `data/filings.py`, the headline loop strips a leading "Page N of M" / "Page N" prefix (`_PAGE_PREFIX_RE`) from a candidate line and runs the remainder through the wrap joiner; the strip is accepted only when the joined result is headline-shaped. (2) A gated live repair over the masthead-headline cohort: SHIFT drops 1–3 junk prefix paragraphs (bare page marker, street address, city+ZIP, "News" kicker, company website) when the remaining stored lines equal a prefix of the live lines modulo spacing normalization; SPLICE replaces a junk headline with the live headline when the stored body is byte-identical to the live body. 17 rows written (MET 3, EL 6, SPGI 8); UNH's 3 rows deliberately deferred.

**Why:** Batch 140 left UNH's "Page 1 of 16 <headline>" mega-lines out of scope as "a headline-pick question, not a paragraph-drop" — and the audit for this batch showed the same page-image flattening also produced the MET/EL/SPGI masthead headlines (address blocks that became the headline when older extractions ran). The headline fix itself is narrow by construction: the bare-page admin guard is `$`-anchored, so it cannot fire when text follows the marker, and the only place a glued marker can reach the headline is the loop's final pick — hence a branch there, not a preprocessing pass over all lines (which would change section/lede inputs too). The joined-result shape check (not a remainder shape check) is load-bearing: UNH's wrapped shape ("Page 1 of 21 UnitedHealth Group Reports" / "2025 Results and Issues 2026 Outlook") has a 3-word remainder that fails the shape test alone but joins into a genuine headline — checking the remainder would have left the prefix on exactly the wrapped case, and checking nothing would have stored 3-word fragment headlines.

**Deliberately not done:** stripping page prefixes from body lines in this batch (the "Page 2 of 16 business, with ..." continuation lines keep their prefix in live bodies — same regex, different path, needs its own blast evidence); truncating URL-glued bullet lines (the outlook bullet dropped by the contact-line guard's `www.` match is a separate guard-ordering question, not a headline question); repairing UNH's 3 stored rows with the current live body (it drops the genuine outlook bullet — storing it would bake in a known-missing bullet, so the repair waits for the body fixes); importing live body content through the repair (stored bodies are preserved byte-for-byte everywhere, including MET's truncated bodies and SPGI's missing last bullets — those land on next full re-extraction, same precedent as batch 137's untaken trailing bullets). No template change: the defect is extractor-side, so no screenshot gate applies.

**Blast-radius method:** Old-vs-new extraction via `git stash` over 168 quarters (21 tickers: UNH/MET/EL cohort + AAPL/MSFT/JPM/XOM controls + BAC/WMT/PG/JNJ/V/HD/MA/CVX/LLY/AVGO/COST/MRK/ABBV/PEP breadth) from cached exhibit HTML, so both runs see identical input. Exactly 3 diffs, all UNH headlines, all headline-only with byte-identical bodies; controls and breadth zero diffs. Repair gates per row (SHIFT: dropped paragraphs all junk-shaped per the loop's own admin/contact predicates + stored lines a normalized prefix of live lines; SPLICE: stored body byte-identical to live body + clean live headline): 17 rows written across two rounds (v1: MET 3 + EL 2; v2 generalized SHIFT/SPLICE: EL 4 + SPGI 8, with the 5 v1 rows re-verified already-clean); 3 UNH rows deferred by design, 0 gate-failed. 7 new regression tests; 1087-test suite green; `git diff --check` clean; /company/UNH, MET, EL, SPGI overviews + /company/UNH/detail/data all 200.

**Related:** Closes batch 140's UNH headline follow-up (headline half only) and its MET page-paragraph rows. ## 2026-10-28 — Batch 142: body-line page markers stripped; view-online trailers cut; UNH's 3 stored rows re-extracted

**What:** Two changes to `data/filings.py`, both in a new `_clean_body_line` applied to body lines only, after headline selection. (1) A leading full-form "Page N of M" marker is stripped when a remainder exists (`_BODY_PAGE_PREFIX_RE`). (2) A line is cut at a "To view this information in a different format ..." trailer (`_VIEW_ONLINE_TRAILER_RE`) when a prefix exists. Then a gated live repair replaces UNH's 3 stored rows (2026-03-31, 2026-06-30, 2025-12-31) with the post-fix live extraction from the same cached exhibits.

**Why:** Batch 141 fixed the headline half and explicitly deferred the body half: the outlook bullet was dropped by the contact-line guard's `www.` match on the URL glued into the bullet line, and page-2 continuation lines kept their prefix. Cleaning on body lines only (not a preprocessing pass over all lines) is load-bearing: batch 141's headline guard exists because a bare fragment remainder must not become the headline, and a global pre-strip would bypass exactly that guard. The "of M" restriction is the same kind of narrowness for prose: "Page 2 of the report ..." is a sentence, not a marker, and only the full "Page N of M" shape is ever a page-image artifact. The trailer cut drops the glued prose after the URL deliberately: that prose was dropped with the whole line before (so nothing previously kept is lost), and keeping it as a separate line would consume lede budget or push two metric bullets past the 14-line block cap to preserve one quote paragraph in a highlights feature — the wrong trade.

**Deliberately not done:** a body-preserving repair in batch 141's SHIFT/SPLICE shapes (the stored UNH rows are old-format mega-paragraphs with no preservable body — Q4 lacks its headline and opening bullets entirely — so the repair imports the new live body under explicit gates instead, with the old-vs-new diff reviewed by hand; the dropped CEO-quote/dateline prose is named here as the one content loss, and it is narrative, not highlights); raising the 14-line leading-block cap to keep the displaced metric bullets (a cap change would move bodies DB-wide; the outlook bullet is worth more than the 14th metric bullet); recovering the glued post-URL prose as its own line (see above); touching templates (extractor-side defect, so no screenshot gate applies).

**Blast-radius method:** Old-vs-new extraction via `git stash` over 168 quarters (21 tickers: UNH/MET/EL cohort + AAPL/MSFT/JPM/XOM controls + 10 breadth large caps) from cached exhibit HTML, so both runs see identical input. Exactly 3 diffs, all UNH, all improvements (outlook bullet gained + page prefixes stripped in each; Q2's block additionally runs to the cap into the reform-actions bullets because the cleaned Page-2 line is now a bullet rather than a terminal-punctuation continuation that broke the block early); controls and breadth zero diffs; COST's 8 fetch errors identical in both runs. Repair gates per row (same-exhibit live text + clean headline + outlook present + no page prefix/trailer anywhere + stored source): 3 rows written, 0 gate-failed. 6 new regression tests; 1093-test suite green; `git diff --check` clean; /company/UNH, MET, EL, SPGI overviews + /company/UNH/detail/data all 200, detail page verified serving the repaired Q2 highlights.

## 2026-10-28 — Batch 143: flattened-cell tables are tables — word-units, split cells, and count rows reject the section; 17 stored rows re-extracted

**What:** `_looks_like_financial_table` in `data/filings.py` gains three signal families, all evaluated on non-bullet lines only: (1) strong signals — a line-start "(In millions|thousands|billions" units parenthetical (`_TABLE_WORD_UNITS_RE`) and a full-line "Percent Change" header (`_PERCENT_CHANGE_WORDS_RE`); (2) flattened cells (`_looks_like_table_cell`) — lone "$", bare numbers including parenthesized negatives (`_BARE_CELL_RE`), period headers including doubled forms (`_PERIOD_HEADER_RE`), standalone "(Unaudited)"-style annotations (`_STANDALONE_PAREN_RE`), "Change"/"Percent" header fragments and capitalized trailing " Percent" (`_HEADER_WORD_RE` + endswith check), and label + two-number count rows (`_COUNT_ROW_RE`, month-guarded); 3+ cells reject the section. Then a gated live repair replaces 17 stored rows (FDX 8, SPG Q1 2025 + Q1 2026, AZO 7) with the post-fix live extraction from the same cached exhibits.

**Why:** The batch-97 guard's mental model was "a table line carries $ and % together, or a (Dollars ...) unit line". Exhibit flattening breaks both halves of that model in three distinct ways, and each batch-143 signal maps to one observed break: FedEx spells units and percent headers in words and splits years onto their own lines; Simon splits every cell (currency symbol, value, currency symbol, value) so no line has any signal at all except bare numbers; AutoZone's count table has neither symbol anywhere, only labels with trailing numbers. Counting cells instead of demanding a single smoking-gun line is the right generalization because a flattened table is exactly a run of cells — and the 3-cell threshold plus the non-bullet restriction is what keeps it from firing on prose: a narrative section would need three cell-shaped lines, and the unit tests pin the two realistic near-misses (a doubled date header is month-guarded out of count rows; one stray "(Unaudited)" line among prose is below threshold). The case-sensitivity split on "Percent" is the same narrowness principle: capitalized trailing "Percent" is a split column header, lowercase trailing "percent" is a wrapped prose line.

**Deliberately not done:** widening the count-row rule to catch date-doubled headers (they are headers, not rows, and the month guard keeps datelines out of the count); treating "(Unaudited)" alone as a strong signal (one annotation line is not a table — the stub needed five cells, and a single-line rule would condemn prose sections that quote an unaudited figure); repairing MPLX/ELV stored rows in this batch (their live extraction is already clean, so the refresh is data-only and belongs to a repair batch, not a code batch — named as a follow-up, not silently folded in); backfilling AZO Q4 FY2026 highlights without a quarterly row (there is no row to attach them to; the quarter's financials seeding is the actual gap); touching templates (extractor-side defect, so no screenshot gate applies).

**Blast-radius method:** Old-vs-new extraction via `git stash` over 208 quarters (table cohort SPG/MPLX/FDX/AZO/ELV + UNH/MET/EL + AAPL/MSFT/JPM/XOM controls + 14 breadth large caps) from cached exhibit HTML, so both runs see identical input. Exactly 18 diffs, all improvements, all in the target cohort (FDX 8, SPG 2, AZO 8 — each table body replaced by subheads + dateline lede); controls and breadth zero diffs; COST's 8 fetch errors identical in both runs. The first blast pass caught two FDX FY2026 quarters surviving as six-line header-only sections, which the second-pass stub signals (doubled period headers, "(Unaudited)", trailing "Percent", lone "Change") then rejected — the re-blast confirms all 18 diffs are clean ledes. Repair gates per row (same-exhibit cached HTML, headline-shaped, no table residue in the new body, a long prose paragraph present, stored headline equal or a prefix of the new one): 17 rows written, 1 gate-skipped (AZO 2026-08-29, no quarterly row). 11 new regression tests; 1104-test suite green; `git diff --check` clean; /company/FDX, SPG, AZO overviews + /company/FDX/detail + /company/FDX/detail/data + /company/AZO/detail all 200, detail/data verified serving the repaired FDX Q4 FY2026 highlights with zero table residue on AZO/SPG pages.

**Related:** Closes the batch-142 follow-up "table-header sections winning on bullet-less releases" in full (guard + repair). Still open: the batch-140 marker rows minus MET 3; SPG 2025-12-31's live FFO line; STZ table-row wart; ABNB heading-less metric table; CART genuine "And ..." sentence; ARCC table-section tail + MCO section-pick body divergence; EOG/SKYD honest blanks; PEG/AFRM/CCI/LIN/PCAR blanks; pending-filing cohort at 29; Wikipedia 403s; the batch-121 transient-window limitation; a bounded `_skip_toc` fix. New: MPLX/ELV stale table rows (repair-only refresh); AZO Q4 FY2026 missing quarterly row (seeding gap, not extraction).

## 2026-10-28 — Batch 144: a repair-only batch writes no code, and a return-shape bug in the repair script must fail closed, not be worked around

**What:** No change to `data/filings.py`. Scripts left untracked in `scripts/` (`batch144_verify.py`, `batch144_repair.py`).

The gate design carries two precedents: batch 141's SPLICE/SHIFT principle (never import live content except through a gate that proves same-release), and batch 140's refusal to rewrite rows that differ for another reason. The headline-prefix acceptance is the same judgment batch 143 made by hand (old extraction truncated the headline; the fuller live headline is the same release), now encoded as a gate rule rather than a hand exception.

**Deliberately not done:** taking SPG 2025-12-31's live FFO line in this repair (batch 143 assigned it to the next full re-extraction, and a repair that imports one new bullet line past a gate designed for whole-body replacement would be scope creep with a different risk shape); adding regression tests for unchanged code (the suite re-run at 1104 green is the validation; a test for a script that lives in /tmp and untracked scripts/ would test the wrong artifact); committing the repair scripts (batch-105–110 precedent leaves them untracked — the durable record is this entry plus the status row, not the script); touching templates (data-only batch, so no screenshot gate applies).

**Blast-radius method:** The dry run is the blast: first draft failed all 9 rows, which exposed the real defect in the batch — `_fetch_press_release_disclosures` returns the full highlights string first, not a headline, so the draft was gating on guidance text. The fix (split full highlights into headline + body) was verified by eyeballing two complete new bodies before the write, and the post-write check re-read all 9 rows from the DB plus live HTTP verification on both tickers' overview, detail, and detail/data routes. A repair script that fails all rows is working as designed — the failure mode to fear is a script that passes rows it should not, which is why the gates check the body shape, not just the headline.

## 2026-10-28 — Batch 145: a strict-prefix gate is the safe shape for importing an appended paragraph, and it fits narrative bodies that bullet gates do not

**What:** No change to `data/filings.py`. SPG 2025-12-31's stored highlights lacked the release's Real Estate FFO line; the live extraction is the stored string verbatim plus one appended FFO paragraph. The repair (`scripts/batch145_repair.py`, dry-run then `--write`, left untracked per the batch-105–110 precedent) replaces the stored string with the live string only if: live source equals stored source, headlines are equal, stored is a strict prefix of live, and the appended tail mentions FFO with zero table-residue matches. 1 row written (1462 → 1662 chars).

**Why:** Batch 144 deliberately did not take this line because its bullet-body gates were designed for whole-body replacement of table-shaped rows and did not fit SPG's narrative body — importing one line past those gates would have been scope creep. The prefix gate resolves that tension without weakening the standard: when live == stored + tail, nothing previously served is removed or altered, so the only risk is the tail itself, and the tail is eyeballed plus residue-checked. This gate generalizes to any future "live adds a paragraph" case, whereas batch 144's bullet gates remain the shape for "live replaces a table body" cases.

**Deliberately not done:** adding regression tests for unchanged code (the suite re-run at 1104 green is the validation; a test for an untracked script would test the wrong artifact); committing the repair scripts (batch-105–110 precedent); touching templates (data-only batch, so no screenshot gate applies); claiming the AZO Q4 FY2026 row as a batch-145 repair — the row was verified present with full financials and highlights but this batch did not create it, so it is recorded as verified-closed via the app's own refresh, not as batch work.

**Blast-radius method:** The probe printed stored and live bodies in full before any gate was written, so the "verbatim + one paragraph" shape was eyeballed, not inferred from a gate pass. The dry run then confirmed exactly 1 appended paragraph (200 chars) before the write, and the post-write check re-read the row from the DB plus live HTTP verification on the SPG overview, detail, and Q4 FY2025 detail/data routes.

**Related:** Closes the batch-143/144 SPG FFO-line follow-up in full. ## 2026-10-28 — Batch 146: a strict change-phrase grammar is what makes a flattened-table parser safe, and its failure mode is the feature

**What:** New shareholder-letter extraction mode in `data/filings.py` (`_parse_flat_summary_table`, tried after the batch-94 metric mode): Airbnb's heading-less "Key Financial/Business Measures" summary table, flattened by text extraction into run-on lines, parses into bullets — label value [change …] with change phrases restricted to "%" + "Y/Y" ["(ex-FX)"] or "%" + words ending in "margin", consecutive values merged as multi-column values, and a dual-column header line naming the value order. 5 regression tests; full suite 1109 green. Live repair: 6 stored ABNB rows (Q1 2025–Q2 2026) under gates (stored blob holds a Key Financial/Business fragment; live output starts with "Shareholder Letter" with ≥5 bullets; every bullet value token appears verbatim in the stored blob).

**Why:** The batch-94 mode's "Highlights" heading requirement was the wrong gate for this shape — the table never has that heading, so every ABNB quarter served table gibberish as its headline. The value-delimited grammar works because the flattened stream preserves one invariant the prose does not: between two values there are only change phrases and label words, and change phrases have exactly two forms in this table. The grammar's strictness is load-bearing, not incidental: the Q3/Q4 2024 letters interleave the same table with narrative sentences, and the first prose change phrase ("10% compared to Q3 2023…") fails the parse rather than producing a label like "Revenue Q3 revenue of $3.7 billion increased" — the digit guard on continuations is a second tripwire for the same shape. A parser that tried to be lenient here would trade 6 clean quarters for 2 junk ones.

**Deliberately not done:** parsing the Q3/Q4 2024 prose-interleaved tables (needs sentence-aware parsing, a different mode, not a loosened grammar); emitting group-header lines for single-column quarters (bullets are self-explanatory; only the dual-column case gets a header, because there the value order is otherwise ambiguous); stripping the "TTM" duplication in Q2 2025's "Trailing Twelve Month TTM ("TTM") FCF" label (it is what the table says — de-duplication would be inventing cleanliness); committing the probe/blast/repair scripts (batch-105–110 precedent); touching templates (data-only batch, so no screenshot gate applies).

**Blast-radius method:** Old-vs-new extraction via `git stash` over 24 live quarters (ABNB 8 + DUOL/AFRM/COIN/CART 4 each): exactly 6 diffs, all intended ABNB shape-A quarters; ABNB shape-B quarters and all 16 other-filer quarters byte-identical. The two new bullets were eyeballed in full against the source tables (Q2 2026, Q4 2025) before the repair write, and the post-write check re-read all 8 ABNB rows from the DB plus live HTTP verification on the ABNB overview, detail, and both repaired quarters' detail/data routes.

**Related:** Closes the batch-143 ABNB heading-less metric table follow-up for the 6 parseable quarters. ## 2026-10-29 — Batch 147: let the app's own refresh land a cohort, don't hand-repair what the freshness check already handles

**What:** Pending-filing cohort recheck (same evidence as the note logic — revenue-chain duration facts only): 6 of 28 tickers landed (BIIB, CB, CDNS, ENPH, PYPL, WELL with 2026-06-30 revenue facts now ingested); 22 still lagged. Each landed ticker's overview was visited once and the app's own freshness check — middle-gap refire, `fetch_and_persist`, highlights enrichment, note recompute — landed the Q2 FY2026 quarter (plus Q3 FY2026) and cleared the note. No code changed; full suite re-run 1109 green.

**Why:** Batches 144–146 hand-repaired stored rows because the live extraction differed from what was stored — the stored data was wrong. Here the stored data was not wrong, it was absent, and the absence had a built-in remedy: the middle-gap check exists precisely for "filing older than the marker whose quarter never made it in." Hand-writing rows would have duplicated that path, skipped the highlights enrichment and the Q3 pull, and risked diverging from what the app itself computes. A page visit is the honest trigger — it is exactly what a user opening the page would do — and the post-visit DB read (figures, highlights length, note NULL) is the verification, not the visit's 200.

**Deliberately not done:** writing a repair script for the 6 quarters (the app's path already covers it, including Q3); visiting the 22 still-lagged tickers' pages to "force" anything (their facts are absent, so the check correctly does nothing — a visit cannot manufacture EDGAR data); adding regression tests for unchanged code (the suite re-run at 1109 green is the validation); committing the recheck/verify scripts (batch-105–110 precedent); touching templates (data-only batch, so no screenshot gate applies).

**Blast-radius method:** The recheck ran read-only against live EDGAR before any page visit, so landings were established independently of the refresh; the verify script then re-read all 6 rows plus notes from the DB after the visits, and the 6 Q2 detail/data routes were HTTP-checked. The 22 still-lagged tickers were not visited for refresh purposes, so their stored state is untouched — the cohort count (28 → 22) is a DB-counted fact, not an inference.

**Related:** Closes 6 of the pending-filing cohort; cohort now at 22. ## 2026-10-29 — Batch 148: bound the TOC tail by headline shape, not by line count

**What:** `_skip_toc` in `data/filings.py` now rolls back when the unanchored tail after the last page-number line contains a headline-shaped line — a headline verb (reports/announces/delivers/posts/declares/names/elects) plus a results topic (results/quarter/earnings/year/revenue/guidance/outlook), with no terminal sentence punctuation (`_NARRATIVE_TAIL_RE`). The rollback target is the first such line, so a leading footnote marker is skipped with the tail rather than landed on. A tail with no headline-shaped line keeps the old return. 4 regression tests; full suite 1113 green.

**Why:** The old loop consumed title-shaped lines without limit after the last page number, because only a page number vouches for a TOC entry — everything past it is unanchored, and "title-shaped" (≤140 chars) describes headlines and ledes just as well as section labels. The demonstrated loss: a packet whose narrative follows the TOC directly stored its first medium-length prose sentence as the headline. The signal is deliberately headline-shaped, not prose-shaped: a footnote sentence ("Includes reconciliation of consolidated net income to FFO ...") carries no headline verb, and a prose lede is excluded by its terminal period — so SPG's "(1)" + footnote + mini-TOC tail, which ends at Contacts:, returns exactly what it returned before. The verb+topic pairing is what separates a headline ("Acme Reports Fourth Quarter Results") from a section title ("Results of Operations", no verb; "Report of Independent Auditors", no topic).

**Deliberately not done:** capping the tail at N lines (a cap still swallows a headline that fits inside it, and a cap of zero would land on SPG's "(1)"); removing the mini-TOC second loop even though it is dead code in all reachable paths (the main loop stops only at a marker, a long line, or EOF, so the second loop always returns the same index — minimal diff beats cleanup here); a stored-row repair (the blast radius shows zero live quarters change, so there is nothing to repair); touching templates (data-only batch, so no screenshot gate applies).

**Blast-radius method:** Old-vs-new extraction via `git stash` over 36 live quarters (SPG 8 + EOG 8 + ABNB/DUOL/AFRM/COIN/CART 4 each): exactly 0 diffs. The self-healing lede fallback (headline=None + body from 0, TOC-fragment dropper) is why several overshoot shapes show no diff — the fix changes only the shape where the fallback cannot recover, i.e. where the overshoot lands on a pickable medium line. The probe/blast scripts are left untracked in `scripts/` per the batch-105–110 precedent. Live: /company/SPG, /company/SPG/detail, /company/EOG, /company/ABNB all 200.

**Related:** Closes the `_skip_toc` over-consumption follow-up carried since batch 126. Still open: ABNB Q3/Q4 2024 prose-interleaved tables; STZ table-row wart; CART genuine "And ..." sentence; ARCC table-section tail + MCO section-pick body divergence; EOG/SKYD honest blanks; PEG/AFRM/CCI/LIN/PCAR blanks; pending-filing cohort at 22; Wikipedia 403s; the batch-121 transient-window limitation.

## 2026-10-29 — Batch 149: blank means missing in disclosure enrichment; repair the blanks the live extractor already recovers

**What:** `_is_blank_disclosure` (None or whitespace-only) now gates the disclosure-enrichment missing filter, the three fill conditions, and the detail-page lazy trigger. DB-wide audit found exactly one empty-string highlights row (PCAR Q1 FY2026) among 10 NULLs; the probe showed the live extractor recovers genuine highlights for PEG Q3/Q4 2025, CCI Q4 2024, and PCAR Q1 2026, so all 4 stored rows were repaired under gates. AFRM Q4 FY2026 and LIN Q3 2024 extract None because their exhibits are image-only, so they stay honestly blank. 1 regression test; full suite 1114 green.

**Why:** The old `is None` checks encoded "a stored value is a good value," but an empty string is not a value — it is the absence of one, produced by some older path (the current extractor returns None or ≥200 chars, never ''). The failure was silent and permanent: PCAR's row kept tripping the lazy trigger on its NULL guidance, yet every enrichment pass skipped its highlights precisely because '' is not None. Treating blank as missing restores the invariant the whole lazy-backfill design assumes (missing → refilled on next visit; good → never overwritten) without touching the never-overwrite guarantee for real content. The same blank logic covers guidance/transcript even though no '' rows exist there today, so the class cannot recur in another field. Repairs, not a code change, were right for PEG/CCI/PCAR because the live extraction was already correct — the stored data was stale, not the extractor. CCI's 2-line table-header tail was accepted rather than trimmed in the repair because the repair must be the verbatim live extraction (a hand-trimmed variant would diverge from what the app computes and would be reintroduced on any re-extraction); the tail-trim rule, if built, belongs in the extractor with its own blast radius.

**Deliberately not done:** a lede tail-trim rule for CCI's table-header tail (a lookahead rule — short lines ahead of a units parenthetical + $ data rows — is plausible but needs its own blast-radius batch; the wart is real content plus 2 junk lines, strictly better than NULL, and matches the accepted STZ-wart precedent); OCR for the AFRM/LIN image-only exhibits (out of scope for a highlights extractor; the honest-blank treatment is the product's designed fallback); clearing EOG Q3 FY2024's stored TOC-junk body (verified live extraction is None under the batch-126 guard, so the stored 200 chars predate the guard — clearing it is a separate repair decision, carried as a follow-up); touching templates (data + service-logic batch, so no screenshot gate applies); committing the probe/diagnose/repair scripts (batch-105–110 precedent) or the untracked DB (tickerlens.db is not tracked — repairs live in the local DB only).

**Blast-radius method:** The blank-as-missing change touches only the enrichment path, and the full suite (1114 green, including the pre-existing never-overwrite tests) is the regression evidence — no live-quarter extraction changed, since the extractor itself is untouched. The repair gates were checked per row against fresh live fetches (not probe output): stored NULL/'', live source exactly "Earnings release <label>", live body ≥200 chars, eyeballed in full. Post-write DB read confirmed all 4 rows plus the cohort counts (NULL 10 → 7, '' 1 → 0); the 4 repaired quarters' detail/data routes plus 3 overviews and 1 detail route returned 200, with the PCAR data route serving the repaired bullets verbatim. The dev server died mid-verification and was restarted via the documented launcher before the final check — the death itself is uninvestigated and noted, not explained away.

**Related:** Closes the stored-blank highlights cohort except the honest blanks. Still open: AFRM Q4 FY2026 + LIN Q3 2024 image-only blanks (OCR-only); EOG Q3 FY2024 stored TOC junk vs live None (clear-or-keep decision); CCI Q4 2024 tail-trim rule candidate; ABNB Q3/Q4 2024 prose-interleaved tables; STZ table-row wart; CART genuine "And ..." sentence; ARCC table-section tail + MCO section-pick body divergence; EOG honestly blank (image-page narrative); SKYD 4 pre-merger blanks; pending-filing cohort at 22; Wikipedia 403s; the batch-121 transient-window limitation.

**What:** EOG's 7 non-blank highlights rows (Q3 FY2024 TOC page; Q1 FY2025–Q2 FY2026 footnote + Balance Sheets fragments) were cleared to NULL/NULL under per-row gates, after a DB-wide sweep confirmed no other ticker matches the junk shapes. No code changed, no new tests; full suite re-run 1114 green.
