## 2026-10-29 — Batch 151: an admin line must not consume a contact-lookahead slot

**What:** `_skip_contact_block`'s contact-evidence lookahead now gathers the next two non-admin lines instead of the next two raw lines, so Crown Castle's contact block (name/title line, then "FOR IMMEDIATE RELEASE", company name, phone) is skipped in full and the real headline is picked. Two new tests; full suite 1116 green. One stored row repaired under gates (CCI Q1 FY2026, contact junk removed, all stored prose preserved verbatim).

**Why:** The batch-105 lookahead was written for blocks where the contact evidence directly follows the name line, and it silently assumed the intervening lines are all detail lines. Crown Castle breaks that assumption with an admin line ("FOR IMMEDIATE RELEASE") sitting between the name and the phone — a line the rest of the extractor already treats as ignorable, but which the lookahead counted as a slot. The failure mode is bad out of proportion to its size: a person's name and title become the page headline, which is the first thing a reader sees. Making the lookahead admin-transparent is the minimal repair because it reuses the existing "contact evidence within two lines" rule and the existing 8-line bound — it only stops counting lines the extractor already ignores everywhere else, rather than widening the window (which would risk swallowing real headlines after misfired headers).

**Deliberately not done:** widening the lookahead from 2 to 3 raw lines (would also fix CCI but would let a misfired header reach one line further into real content on every other filer); repairing CCI Q3/Q4 2025 stored rows with the new live output (their stored bullets are better than the new lede, which carries table headers instead — replacing them would regress, so the bullet-vs-lede gap stays a separate follow-up); touching templates (data-only batch, so no screenshot gate applies); committing the probe/blast/repair scripts (batch-105–110 precedent) or the untracked DB (tickerlens.db is not tracked — repairs live in the local DB only).

**Blast-radius method:** Old-vs-new live extraction via `git stash` over 32 quarters across 8 filers (CCI/SPG/EOG/ABNB/DUOL/COIN/CART/AFRM, 4 each) shows exactly 3 diffs, all the intended CCI headline fixes; the other 29 quarters are byte-identical, including all shareholder-letter and image-only filers. The repair script re-fetched live extraction inside itself and gated on verbatim preservation of the stored dateline and quote paragraphs, so no gate rested on probe output alone. Post-write DB read and page visits confirm the repaired row holds and serves without contact residue.

**Related:** Closes the CCI contact-headline defect (surfaced while investigating the batch-149 CCI tail-trim candidate, which remains open as its own follow-up). Still open: ABNB Q3/Q4 2024 prose-interleaved tables; STZ table-row wart; CART genuine "And …" sentence; ARCC table-section tail + MCO section-pick body divergence; AFRM/LIN image-only blanks; CCI bullet-vs-lede gap and lede tail-trim candidate; SKYD 4 pre-merger blanks; pending-filing cohort at 22; Wikipedia 403s; the batch-121 transient-window limitation.

## 2026-10-30 — Batch 153: skip table residue in the lede instead of letting it fill the budget

**What:** Table lines (unit lines, outlook/guidance-table headings, period/date headers, figure-dense data rows) are now skipped in the lede loop — they neither consume the 6-line budget nor get stored — a trailing table run is trimmed after collection, and the leading-bullet block and the Highlights-section collector both end at a table heading instead of swallowing it. 5 new tests; full suite 1122 green. 11 stored rows repaired under gates (CCI 6, APP 4, COIN 1); CART Q2 2026 deliberately not repaired (live picks a different section than stored).

**Why:** Crown Castle opens its releases with the outlook table directly after the dateline, so the lede filled with the table and the real bullets after it starved — the stored rows were the better half of the defect (real bullet + wart tail) and the live extraction was worse (table headers as the whole body). The fix had to be a skip, not a post-trim: trimming the tail would have removed the wart but not recovered the starved bullets, and the budget was the scarce resource. The section collector gets the same break because AppLovin's guidance-summary heading was being swallowed as section content, and the same break reclassifies CCI's single-bullet Highlights sections (previously rejected as financial tables because the table followed the bullet) as valid sections.

**Deliberately not done:** a post-trim-only fix (removes the wart, never recovers the bullets); extending the footnote-tail rule past 6 words for CCI's 9–12-word table-label lines (a prose fragment can end in a footnote marker too — a noun-phrase test is needed first, so those two headings stand as known warts); a date-header rule inside sections (the lede rule covers the class; APP's period headers only reach the output via the section path, where a unit/heading line already cuts collection); replacing CART Q2 2026 wholesale (live picks the business section, stored holds the financial bullets — replacing would destroy good content); touching templates (data-only batch, so no screenshot gate applies); committing the probe/blast/repair scripts (batch-105–110 precedent) or the untracked DB (tickerlens.db is not tracked — repairs live in the local DB only).

**Blast-radius method:** Old-vs-new live extraction via `git stash` over 36 quarters across 9 filers, with exhibit HTML cached to disk so both runs see byte-identical input; exactly 9 quarters move, all intended, the other 27 byte-identical. The wrapped-prose hazard was caught by the suite itself: the first skip version ate Oracle/Chubb/USB wrapped fragments (5 failures), which forced the strict predicates (figure density, header vocabulary, short labels, terminated/bullet guards) and a figure-regex fix (lone commas counted as figures). The repair script re-fetched live extraction per row inside itself and gated on source match, line-level core preservation (100-char prefix, to tolerate old-code truncation), no residue markers, and a 200-char floor; post-write DB read confirms all 11 rows and the routes serve them with zero residue.

**Related:** Closes the CCI lede tail-trim candidate and bullet-vs-lede gap carried since batch 149, and the APP guidance-heading tail from batch 152. OCR for the image-only blanks; touching templates (data-only batch, so no screenshot gate applies); committing the probe/blast/repair scripts (batch-105–110 precedent) or the untracked DB (tickerlens.db is not tracked — repairs live in the local DB only).

## 2026-10-30 — Batch 156: no-bullet table-headed "Highlights" sections no longer pre-empt the lede

**What:** `_extract_highlights_sections` in `data/filings.py` now rejects a section that hits a table break with no bullet lines, however long its intro prose is ("·"-led bullets count as bullets). The lede loop skips "Highlights" headings, `_is_lede_table_line` treats a "following table / table below summarizes" sentence as residue, `_LEDE_DATE_TOKEN_RE` gains full/year/years, and `_LEDE_DATA_ROW_RE` allows a space-separated $. 7 new tests; full suite 1140 green; blast 0 diffs over 103 quarters, so no stored-row repair applies.

**Why:** The batch-155 GOOGL Q4 hazard was masked for GOOGL by the dateline fix, but the section-path rule itself was unchanged: a table-headed section with no bullets and >=150 chars of intro prose (which `_looks_like_financial_table` never flags) won as the only candidate and served a table stub instead of the lede's real bullets. Rejecting the section alone was not enough — the first fix pass left the lede starved by the section's residue (heading, intro sentence, doubled period header, space-separated-$ data rows consumed the 6-line budget), so the four lede skips ship in the same batch to make the rejection actually deliver the bullets.

**Deliberately not done:** generalizing the table-intro verb beyond "summarizes" — the blast caught "sets forth" eating a genuine Crown Castle prose sentence, so the verb list stays at the one attested word; a finish-the-quote rule for multi-line CEO quotes; extending the footnote-tail rule past 6 words; a gated repair pass over the stale-stored-highlights cohort found by this batch's probe (ADBE 4, META 3+, MTB 2, APP 1, MCO 2, EMR 1 rows whose live extraction is already clean but whose stored rows predate the fixes — a separate repair item, since the batch-156 fix itself changes zero live quarters); OCR for the image-only blanks; touching templates (data-only batch, so no screenshot gate applies); committing the probe/blast scripts (batch-105–110 precedent) or the untracked DB (tickerlens.db is not tracked — repairs live in the local DB only).

**Blast-radius method:** Old-vs-new live extraction via `git stash` over 103 quarters across 22 filers, with exhibit HTML cached to disk so both runs see byte-identical input: exactly 0 diffs. The first blast (before narrowing the intro verb) showed exactly 1 diff, CCI Q1 2026, which was a regression — the lede lost a genuine outlook sentence and gained a table row — and the narrowing fixed it; the re-blast is clean. 5 of the 7 new tests fail with the src change stashed, so the regression is pinned.

**Related:** Closes the no-bullet table-section rule carried since batch 155. ## 2026-10-30 — Batch 158: multi-line quotes finish in the leading-bullet block; closing quotes count as terminal punctuation

**What:** `_leading_bullet_block` in `data/filings.py` no longer closes an item while its quotation is open, and the terminal-punctuation check sees through a closing quote (`.”` / `."`).

**Why:** Alphabet's multi-line CEO quotes open on a line that ends with a period inside the still-open quote, so the old item-close rule truncated GOOGL Q2 FY2026's highlights at the quote's first sentence. Separately, the old terminal regex never matched a line ending in `”`, so a quote that opened and closed on one line never closed the item itself — JNJ's dateline paragraph ends `...technology.”` and the old code leaked the following table's "Overall financial results" / "Q4" header lines into the highlights body before the table-units break stopped it.

**Deliberately not done:** extending the finish-the-quote rule to the lede path (the lede's 6-line budget with its batch-139 finish rule is deliberate; the attested defect is the leading block only); generalizing the quote-state scan past the current item (an earlier item's quote must not keep a later item open); OCR for the image-only blanks; touching templates (data-only batch, so no screenshot gate applies); committing the probe/blast/repair scripts (batch-105–110 precedent) or the untracked DB (tickerlens.db is not tracked — repairs live in the local DB only).

**Blast-radius method:** Old-vs-new live extraction via `git stash` over 117 quarters across 22 filers, with exhibit HTML cached to disk so both runs see byte-identical input: exactly 8 diffs, all intended (GOOGL 5 quote completions; JNJ 3 table-heading removals). The other 109 quarters are byte-identical. The new tests fail with the src change stashed (old code truncates the shaped block to 3 lines ending mid-quote), so the regression is pinned. The repair script re-fetched live extraction per row inside itself and gated on source match, headline match, every stored bullet core + terminated stored sentence preserved, no residue markers, and a 200-char floor; post-write DB read confirms all 8 rows, and the 2 spot-checked rows are byte-identical to the blast's new extraction.

**Related:** Closes the multi-line-quote truncation carried since batch 155 and the JNJ table-heading leak. **What:** `_is_lede_data_row` in `data/filings.py` gains a $-less short-row branch (≥2 figures, ≥25% density, ends in a figure, no `$`, ≤80 chars) for rows whose 3–4-word labels dilute density below the 40% bar, with a comparison-phrase exemption (versus / greater than / less than / higher than / lower than / compared with|to). New `_is_lede_table_title` (unterminated, figure-less, period phrase + table vocabulary, bullet-led lines excluded) breaks the lede loop once content exists, or skips the title when the lede is still empty so bullets after a leading table are still collected. 6 new tests; full suite 1161 green; blast 27 intended/neutral diffs over 259 quarters; 48 stored rows repaired under gates (DELL 8, DUK 8, KMI 8, SO 7, WFC 6, SRE 4, AON 7) and SHOP Q3 2024 cleared to NULL/NULL; 3 skipped (ALL ×2, META Q3 2025).

The section path already rejects these tables; the lede had no equivalent for diluted rows or for a table title.

**Blast-radius method:** Old-vs-new live extraction via `git stash` over 259 quarters across 32 filers (batch-157 cohort + batch-159 probe cohort), with exhibit HTML cached to disk so both runs see byte-identical input: 27 diffs, all intended or neutral (SO 8 table-tail removals; AON 7 diluted-row removals with quotes/prose gained; WFC 6 table-row removals with ◦ bullets gained; EMR 3 + SRE 2 neutral tail reshuffles in the guidance-footnote zone). The other 232 quarters are byte-identical. The first blast had 31 diffs; review caught a content loss in the 4 KMI diffs — the EPS summary line's full-year figures appear nowhere else, and "Q2"/year tokens inflate its density past the new branch — so the comparison-phrase exemption was added and the re-blast has zero KMI diffs. The new tests fail with the src change stashed (the title helper doesn't exist), so the regression is pinned. ## 2026-10-31 — Batch 160: Allstate's long table rows + label lines + symbol legend no longer leak into the lede

**What:** `_is_lede_data_row` in `data/filings.py` raises both $-less length caps 80 → 120 chars and the 25% branch accepts a trailing "NM" as a figure-like ending. New `_is_lede_table_label(ln, next_ln)` in the lede loop: a figure-less, unterminated, ≤12-word line directly above a data row is that row's label. `_is_lede_table_line` treats a short all-caps symbol legend (`^[A-Z]{1,5}\s*=\s`, "NM = not meaningful") and a spaced footnote-glyph legend line (`^[*†‡]\s+\S`, "* Measures used in this release ...") as table residue. 6 new tests; full suite 1167 green; blast 5 intended/neutral diffs over 259 quarters; 2 stored rows repaired under gates (ALL 2024-09-30, 2024-12-31).

The label rule is look-ahead because a label line alone is indistinguishable from a prose fragment — only the values row below it proves it is a label.

The other 254 quarters are byte-identical. These gains were" above a $-figure continuation line — so the label rule now refuses any line containing a mid-line sentence boundary (`[.!?]\s`), and the re-blast has zero COIN diffs. The new tests fail with the src change stashed (the label helper doesn't exist), so the regression is pinned. The repair script re-fetched live extraction per row inside itself and gated on source match, headline match, every stored bullet core + terminated stored sentence preserved, and no live table residue; post-write DB read confirms both rows, and the repair's live text is byte-identical to the blast's new extraction for both quarters.

## 2026-10-31 — Batch 161: Sempra's single-figure reconciliation rows no longer leak into the lede

**What:** New `_is_lede_recon_row` in `data/filings.py` with two branches, both guarded by a ≥4-word label (the tokens before the first figure or dash token): (1) dash-cell — a standalone "—"/"–"/"-" token adjacent to a figure token, the line ending in a figure or a dash, ≤300 chars; (2) bare two-figure — ≥2 figures, ends in a figure, no $ or % anywhere, ≥10% density, ≤150 chars, no mid-line sentence boundary, no comparison phrase. A line ending in a bare year (`(19|20)\d{2}`) is prose, never a row. The predicate plugs into `_is_lede_table_line` (lede-loop skip + tail-trim start) and into `_is_lede_table_label` (a recon row below a figure-less label counts as its values row). 5 new tests; full suite 1172 green; blast 6 intended diffs over 275 quarters; 5 stored rows repaired under gates (SRE 2024-09-30, 2025-03-31, 2025-06-30, 2026-03-31; WFC 2025-06-30).

**Why:** The batch-160 follow-up: Sempra's GAAP-to-adjusted reconciliation table flattens rows no density rule catches — one trailing figure after dash placeholders ("... — — — 44"), rows ending in a dash cell ("... 9 —"), and bare two-figure rows at 13–22% density ("(8) 41", "(19) (8)", "11 9", "71 97 52 89"). The dash-adjacency rule is the discriminator for the first two shapes (a prose em-dash sits between words); the bare branch needs the label, density, ending, and $-/%-free guards together because a wrapped prose fragment can carry two bare figures. The year guard came from the first DB-wide scan, which caught real false positives: headlines and sub-headlines ending in years ("Simon® Reports Third Quarter 2024 Results, Affirms Full Year 2024", Verizon's "... in 1Q 2025") and wrapped fragments ("of common stock at the close of business on August 17, 2026") — a reconciliation value is a small bare number, never a year. The Q2 2026 "71 97 52 89" row is 22% dense, so it is the new bare branch, not batch 160's 25% branch, that catches it.

**Deliberately not done:** a numbered-footnote rule for "1) See Table A for information regarding non-GAAP financial measures." — the SRE Q3 2024 live body now carries one (it was beyond the old 6-line budget until the recon rows were skipped), alongside the stored SRE Q1 2025 row and one other stored row; that shape needs its own predicate with its own blast, and the repair gates tolerate the line; touching templates (data-only batch, so no screenshot gate applies); OCR for the image-only blanks; committing the probe/blast/repair scripts (batch-105–110 precedent) or the untracked DB (tickerlens.db is not tracked — repairs live in the local DB only).

**Blast-radius method:** Old-vs-new live extraction via `git stash` over 275 quarters across 34 filers (batch-157 cohort + batch-159 probe cohort + CRM/DGX, the DB-scan headline/period-header shapes), with exhibit HTML cached to disk so both runs see byte-identical input: 6 diffs, all intended (SRE 5 — recon rows out, real prose gained per quarter: the CPUC decision paragraph, Oncor capital-plan paragraphs, the value-creation updates; WFC 1 — the segment row out, the starved $253M merchant-services bullet gained). The other 269 quarters are byte-identical, including all shareholder-letter, image-only, and Tesla quarters and both CRM/DGX shapes. The new tests fail with the src change stashed (the helper doesn't exist), so the regression is pinned. The repair script re-fetched live extraction per row inside itself and gated on source match, headline match, every stored bullet core + terminated stored sentence preserved, and no live table residue (a recon row blocks outright); post-write DB read confirms all 5 rows, and the repair's live text is byte-identical to the blast's new extraction for the spot-checked SRE Q1 2025 and WFC Q2 2025 rows.

## 2026-10-31 — Batch 162: Bare numbered footnotes ("1) See Table A ...") no longer leak into the lede or a Highlights section

**What:** New `_LEDE_FOOTNOTE_NUM_RE` (`^\d{1,3}\)\s`) in `data/filings.py`, plugged into the lede-loop skip, `_is_lede_table_line` (before the termination gate, batch-156 precedent), and the headline skip; new `_is_section_footnote_line` (all three footnote forms: parenthesized, definition-verb, bare numbered) drops footnote lines from a collected Highlights section after the 150-char qualification (batch-140 pattern). 8 new tests; full suite 1180 green; blast 9 intended diffs over 283 quarters; 8 stored rows repaired under gates (SRE 2024-09-30, 2025-03-31, 2025-06-30; DDOG 2025-06-30; META 2025-03-31, 2025-06-30, 2026-03-31, 2026-09-30).

**Why:** The batch-161 follow-up: Sempra's "1) See Table A ..." and Datadog's "1) The three months ended ... are adjusted for M&A transaction costs ..." match neither the parenthesized footnote rule ("(1) ...", batch 97) nor the definition-verb rule ("1 Defined as ...", batch 104), so the line was collected as prose — mid-lede in SRE (consuming a budget slot ahead of real paragraphs) and between section bullets in DDOG. The section collector was the one collection path with no footnote handling at all, which the first blast exposed: it showed only the 3 SRE diffs, and DDOG's footnote survived because its release is section-served. The section filter covers all three footnote forms because they are one hazard class — a footnote definition is never a highlight — and the re-blast's 5 META diffs (the trailing "(1) For more information on our free cash flow ..." line out of section-served bodies) are the same fix paying off in the parenthesized form. The repair script's narrative-preservation gate needed a matching fix: it exempted digit-led footnote lines but not parenthesized ones, so it refused all 4 META rows as "stored prose line lost" — the gate now exempts all three footnote forms, since their absence in live is the repair, not a loss.

**Deliberately not done:** a footnote skip in `_leading_bullet_block` (a footnote line after a bullet there is collected as a continuation — same hazard class, no attested case; the DDOG shape was section-served, SRE lede-served); a vocabulary guard on the numbered rule (e.g. requiring "See Table"/"adjusted for") — the plain `^\d+\)\s` rule is what batch 161 named, and the 283-quarter blast shows zero genuine numbered-list losses; changing `_FOOTNOTE_DEF_RE` (the spaced-glyph pin stays, per batch 160); OCR for the image-only blanks; touching templates (data-only batch, so no screenshot gate applies); committing the probe/blast/repair scripts (batch-105–110 precedent) or the untracked DB (tickerlens.db is not tracked — repairs live in the local DB only).

**Blast-radius method:** Old-vs-new live extraction via `git stash` over 283 quarters across 35 filers (batch-157 cohort + batch-159 probe cohort + CRM/DGX + DDOG), with exhibit HTML cached to disk so both runs see byte-identical input: 9 diffs, all intended (SRE 3 — footnote out, real prose gained per quarter; DDOG 1 — M&A footnote out of the section, all bullets kept; META 5 — trailing free-cash-flow footnote out, headcount bullet kept). The other 274 quarters are byte-identical. The first blast had 3 diffs (SRE only); review of the zero-diff DDOG row caught the section-path gap, and the re-blast added the DDOG + META diffs. One pre-existing test needed a deliberate update: the batch-157 Meta test asserted the "(1) For more information ..." footnote sentence survived — that pinned incidental behavior (the section path never dropped footnote lines), not content, so it now expects the drop. The new tests fail with the src change stashed (the helper doesn't exist), so the regression is pinned. The repair script re-fetched live extraction per row inside itself and gated on source match, headline match, every stored bullet core + terminated stored sentence preserved, and no live table residue (a numbered-footnote line blocks outright); post-write DB read confirms all 8 rows, and the repair's live text is byte-identical to the blast's new extraction for the spot-checked SRE Q3 2024, DDOG Q2 2025, and all 4 META quarters. A DB-wide re-scan finds zero stored rows with a numbered-footnote line and zero with the Meta free-cash-flow footnote line.

**What:** New `_salvage_page_line_lede` (+ `_split_page_line_sentences`, `_PAGE_DATELINE_RE`) in `data/filings.py`, called only inside the batch-105 page-line guard. Shopify's Q3-2024 exhibit `a3pr.htm` flattens each page into one mega-line (dateline lede + CEO/CFO quotes + headline + metric table, no line breaks), so no headline line exists and the guard returned None — SHOP's only blank quarter. The salvage qualifies a paragraph only when it opens with a dateline, splits it into sentences (abbreviation-guarded), keeps up to 6 narrative sentences, drops footnote/table-residue sentences, and rescues a title at the head of a table fragment as the headline. Live backfill: SHOP Q3 2024 now stores the true headline + quote lede, SHOP 8/8, NULL-highlight rows 16 → 15.

**Why:** The batch-105 guard is correct for slide decks (a page-line is not a highlight) but wrong for Workiva narrative pages, which carry the quarter's real lede in exactly the shape the guard discards. The salvage is gated to the guard branch, so documents that currently extract can never change — the blast (20 tickers, cached HTML, old-vs-new) shows 0 diffs, which is the design working, not a weak test: the only document that flips is the one attested SHOP exhibit.

**Deliberately not done:** a pre-pass sentence split of all mega-lines (rejected — it would re-budget every long lede paragraph in the DB and need a full-corpus blast for a one-row gain); scanning ahead from the dateline branch in the headline loop (rejected — same gain, wider blast radius, Berkshire's headline-less pattern already covers lede-only results); OCR for the image-only AFRM/LIN blanks (out of scope, still structurally unextractable).

**Blast-radius method:** Old-vs-new live extraction via `git stash` over cached exhibit HTML for 20 tickers (NULL cohort + 14 filled, latest exhibits): 0 diffs. The 2 end-to-end tests fail with the salvage stubbed out, so the regression is pinned.

**Related:** Closes the SHOP 6-K follow-up in full (discovery batch 103 + extraction batch 163). Still open: ABNB prose-interleaved tables; CART "And …" sentence; ARCC table-section tail + MCO section-pick body divergence; AFRM/LIN image-only blanks; EOG supplement-only blanks; SKYD pre-merger blanks; COIN Q3 2026 unfilled; pending-filing cohort at 22; Wikipedia 403s; the batch-121 transient-window limitation. **New:** SKYD's latest shareholder letter picks a bare numbered footnote ("1 Non-GAAP measures ...") as its headline — `_FOOTNOTE_HEADLINE_RE` only matches the parenthesized form, so the batch-162 bare-numbered class escapes the batch-126 headline guard; WFC's "Selected Income Statement Data" scaffolding is still a separate table-heading-zone wart; a footnote line inside `_leading_bullet_block` is the same hazard class in the one collection path not yet covered, with no attested case.

## 2026-10-31 — Batch 164: Copula-pointer footnote definitions are never headline or lede material (SKYD)

**What:** `_FOOTNOTE_DEF_RE` gains a third alternative — numeric key + defined term + copula + definition pointer (`(are|is)\s+(detailed|defined|described|calculated|reconciled|set forth)`) — plus a 300-char line bound on that alternative. The same batch adds a footnote-definition skip to `_leading_bullet_block` (a footnote line after a bullet is skipped, not swallowed as a continuation). Live repair: SKYD Q2 FY2026 (580 → 637, footnote headline out, first bullet becomes the headline) and SKYD Q1 FY2026 (2083 → 2693, footnote tail out, letter-opening paragraph in).

**Why:** Skydance's Q2 FY2026 shareholder letter opens with "1 Non-GAAP measures are detailed in the Supplemental Disclosures at the end of this letter." — no closing paren (batch-162 rule misses) and no definition verb (batch-104 rule misses) — and the headline loop picked it as the headline; the same line trailed the Q1 lede. The participle list is definition-pointer only on purpose: "are included / provided / presented / discussed" are prose verbs (a wrapped bullet continuation can read "1 million shares are included in ..."), so they stay out, pinned by negatives. The 300-char bound is the lesson of this batch's own blast: the first (unbounded) version matched SHOP Q3 2024's page-1 mega-line — it opens "1 Internet, Everywhere ..." and contains "are reconciled ..." a thousand chars in — dropping the whole page from the lede, killing the batch-163 salvage, and serving outlook + balance sheet. A footnote definition is one sentence, always far under 300 chars; a mega-line never is, so the bound is the right shape for the guard, not a magic number to tune per filer.

**Deliberately not done:** a "See"-led bare-numbered pointer ("1 See the Supplemental Disclosures ...") — no attested case, and "see" as a verb would widen the guard toward real prose; watch for it in the next shareholder-letter cohort instead. Fixing the SKYD mid-bullet truncation (the section/leading-block line budget cuts the last bullet's continuation) — a budget question, not a footnote defect, and the stored row had the same shape. OCR for the image-only AFRM/LIN blanks (out of scope, still structurally unextractable).

**Blast-radius method:** Old-vs-new live extraction via `git stash` over cached exhibit HTML for 34 tickers, 268 quarters (batch-160 cohort + SKYD + GEV): 2 diffs, both intended (SKYD Q1 + Q2 FY2026). The first blast showed a third diff — the SHOP regression — which is what produced the 300-char bound; the re-blast shows only the 2 intended diffs. 3 of the 6 new tests fail with the src change stashed, so the regression is pinned.

**Related:** Closes the batch-163 SKYD footnote-headline follow-up and the batch-163 `_leading_bullet_block` follow-up in full — the footnote-def hazard class is now covered in all four collection paths (headline loop, lede, section, leading-bullet block). Still open: ABNB prose-interleaved tables; CART "And …" sentence; ARCC table-section tail + MCO section-pick body divergence; AFRM/LIN image-only blanks; EOG supplement-only blanks; SKYD pre-merger blanks; COIN Q3 2026 unfilled; pending-filing cohort at 22; Wikipedia 403s; the batch-121 transient-window limitation. **New:** SKYD Q2 FY2026's live extraction still truncates mid-bullet-3 (line-budget cut); a "See"-led bare-numbered footnote pointer would escape all three footnote shapes (no attested case).

## 2026-11-01 — Batch 165: the lede finish rule fires on a wrap tail, not on any bullet in the lede (SKYD/EMR)

**What:** The lede finish rule (batch 139) gains a second trigger: `_LEDE_WRAP_TAIL_RE` — a lede line ending grammatically unfinished (trailing comma/dash, or a trailing conjunction/preposition/article/copula, including "across"). The decision is snapshotted at the first line past the budget, and for the wrap-tail path collection stops once a continuation line terminates the sentence. A new bullet starting past the budget ends collection at the boundary (`_is_inline_bullet_start`). Live repair: SKYD 2026-06-30 (637 → 746, bullet 3 completes) and EMR 2024-09-30 (634 → 1159, CEO quote completes).

**Why:** Skydance's "Summary Points" list is cut mid-bullet-3 by the lede's 6-line budget — the section path never engages because the heading sits before the headline. The first fix attempt generalized batch 139's gate (inline bullet starts set `marker_seen`); the blast showed 34 diffs, only 1 intended — the gate fired whenever ANY bullet appeared in the first 6 lines, extending finished prose (22 extensions, one pulling a page footer) and complete figure bullets. The wrap tail is the right gate because it tests the item AT the budget, not the lede's contents: a line ending mid-sentence must continue; a complete statement must not. The snapshot matters because a continuation line's own ending is an arbitrary wrap point ("third-party" is mid-phrase, not an ending). The sentence-termination stop matters because the unfinished item is a sentence — without it, the prose case would append the NEXT sentence too. The ";" exclusion is the same lesson at a smaller scale: a semicolon separates clauses, and Coinbase's letter ends a paragraph with one right before its footnote block.

**Deliberately not done:** Fixing the COIN footnote shape ("1 Coinbase Total Trading Volume" + "comprises ..." on the next line) — it needs a multi-line footnote skipper, its own design + blast, like batches 162/164 did for the other footnote shapes. Removing EMR's pre-existing "in millions, except per share)" fragment — it predates this batch in the stored row and is a table-units filter gap, not a finish-rule product. Serving all 7 SKYD bullets — the 6-line budget is a design choice; this batch fixes the truncation, not the budget. A multi-sentence bullet whose opener ends with terminal punctuation still cuts at the budget (sentence-termination stop trades this for prose safety; no attested case in the 268-quarter blast).

**Blast-radius method:** Old-vs-new live extraction via `git stash` over cached exhibit HTML for 34 tickers, 268 quarters (batch-160 cohort + SKYD + GEV): the first blast showed 34 diffs (1 intended), which is what produced the wrap-tail gate; the second showed 3 (the COIN ";" diff is what removed the semicolon); the third shows only the 2 intended diffs. 7 new tests; the SKYD end-to-end case is pinned behaviorally (old code ends mid-item).

**Related:** Closes the batch-164 SKYD mid-bullet-3 follow-up (with the attribution corrected: lede budget, not section/leading-block budget). Still open: the COIN fourth footnote shape; the EMR paren-less units fragment; AON 2026-09-30's table-header lede line; the "See"-led bare-numbered footnote pointer (no attested case).

## 2026-11-01 — Batch 166: footnote-definition blocks skip as blocks, not just their key lines (COIN/EMR)

**What:** A multi-line footnote skipper, `_footnote_def_block_len`, wired into all four collection paths (headline loop, lede, leading-bullet block, Highlights sections) plus the salvage path's sentence check. It closes the batch-165 follow-up (Coinbase's fourth shape: `1 Coinbase Total Trading Volume / comprises Spot Trading Volume`) and, by construction, the wrapped continuations of every earlier shape plus the bracketed shape (`[1] Adjusted EBITDA is a non-GAAP financial measure.`, which starved Coinbase's Q2 FY2025 lede) and the same-line term+verb variant (`3 Trading Volume represents the`, `1 Spot Trading Volume is` — "presented" is the participle shape 2 deliberately excludes). The lede also drops investor-conference-call notices. Live repair: 4 EMR rows (1816 → 1710, 2382 → 2339, 1535 → 1377, 1449 → 1359 — footnote tails out, guidance prose kept).

**Why:** The single-line regexes only ever dropped the key line. A definition wraps — `3 Trading Volume represents the / total US dollar equivalent value of / ... / period of measurement.` — and the wrapped lines were served as lede/section content whenever a path reached them; the fourth shape never matched at all, so the batch-165 finish rule could pull the whole block in as a "continuation". The block scanner consumes the key line, its wrapped continuations, and chained term-definitions, so a footnote definition is never served whole or in part. Only sentence termination ends a definition — there is deliberately no completeness gate, because a wrapped line can look complete (`[3] $USD resources is defined as cash and cash` has a 5-word object yet wraps); the next line's shape is the guard, not this line's. A bare `is`/`are` counts only at line end or before a definition participle, and the term must start Title-Case, which is what keeps `1 Total Revenue is expected to grow` and shape 2's `1 million shares are included in ...` out. The headline loop takes `include_paren=False` because batch 126 deliberately keeps paren lines headline-eligible (the EOG supplement guard depends on it).

**Deliberately not done:** A completeness gate on definition lines (tried — it stopped the `[3] $USD resources ...` definition mid-wrap and left `equivalents and USDC ...` served; the next-line-shape test subsumes it). Table predicates in the block boundary (tried — figure-dense continuations like `million and $687 million in pre-tax gains ...` score as table lines and stopped every block at its key line). Chaining onto a definition's second sentence (`Both measurements represent the product ...`) or a wrapped-term key (`4 Coinbase Crypto Trading Volume / Market Share is defined as ...`) — a capital-led sentence after a terminated definition is indistinguishable from new prose, and a Title-Case fragment is indistinguishable from a section heading; both are documented limitations with no path serving the lines today. A rounding-note paragraph skip (`Figures have been rounded ...`) — Coinbase's live extraction now serves the rounding paragraph instead of the footnotes, and that skip plus a spaced-glyph asterisk-note skip plus a quarter-title headline fallback are the three small follow-ups that unblock COIN's quarters; they are separate rules, not part of this batch. Serving all 7 SKYD bullets, the EMR paren-less units fragment, the AON table-header lede line — pre-existing, out of scope. OCR for the image-only AFRM/LIN blanks (out of scope).

**Blast-radius method:** Old-vs-new live extraction via `git stash` over cached exhibit HTML for 34 tickers, 268 quarters (batch-160 cohort + SKYD + GEV): the first blast showed 12 diffs — 6 EOG (the headline loop was skipping paren lines, defeating the batch-126 supplement guard) — which produced the `include_paren` guard; the second showed 12 again (the COIN tails still served: the boundary bug and the completeness gate; plus the EMR call-notice backfill) — which produced the boundary/completeness fixes, the call-notice exclusion, and the `US`-lead/`excludes?` refinements; the third confirmed the APP `Webcast and Conference Calls` heading false positive was gone (singular-`call` word boundary). Final: 7 diffs, all intended (EMR ×4 footnote tails only; COIN ×3 footnote text out). 15 new tests; the COIN Q4 FY2025 verbatim-block case and the GEV headline case pin the regression behaviorally.

**Related:** Closes the batch-165 COIN fourth-footnote-shape follow-up in full and the batch-164 leading-bullet-block footnote follow-up (now block-aware). Still open: ABNB prose-interleaved tables; CART "And …" sentence; ARCC table-section tail + MCO section-pick body divergence; AFRM/LIN image-only blanks; EOG supplement-only blanks; SKYD pre-merger blanks; COIN Q3 2026 unfilled; pending-filing cohort at 22; Wikipedia 403s; the batch-121 transient-window limitation. **New:** COIN's quarters need a rounding-note skip, a spaced-glyph asterisk-note skip, and a quarter-title headline fallback (Q1/Q2 2025 lose their headline once the `[1] ...` fallback is gone); the scanner's wrapped-term / second-sentence / mixed-case-term limitations (all attested, none served today); the EMR paren-less units fragment; AON 2026-09-30's table-header lede line; a "See"-led bare-numbered footnote pointer (no attested case).

## 2026-11-01 — Batch 167: rounding notes and asterisk notes skip as blocks; quarter titles are headlines, not masthead residue (COIN)

**What:** Three rules closing the batch-166 COIN follow-ups. (1) `_rounding_note_block_len`: the rounding boilerplate ("Figures have been rounded for presentation purposes only. For additional financial information ... Form 10-K/10-Q filed with the SEC on <date>." / "Note: Figures presented [above] may not sum precisely due to rounding.") skips as a block at all four collection paths plus a sentence check in the salvage path; the long form's second sentence may also stand alone as an opener (Q3 FY2024 carries the short note plus the referral sentence). (2) `_asterisk_note_block_len`: chart asterisk notes ("* Based on the closing price of ...", "*Below reporting threshold of 10%.", "*Net of USDC loaned ...", "*As of December 31, 2024 ...", "* Measures used in this release ...") skip as blocks at the same four paths; the lead list is note-shaped only. (3) Quarter titles ("First/Second/Third/Fourth Quarter [and Full Year] YYYY") are excluded from `_looks_like_masthead_residue` and from `_skip_bare_masthead_name`, so they are picked as headlines. Live repair: 3 COIN rows (Q4 FY2024 322 → 776, Q1 FY2025 457 → 1119, Q2 FY2025 246 → 678); Q3 FY2024 correctly refused by the repair's first-paragraph gate.

**Why:** The rounding note filled the lede whenever nothing else preceded the prose (Q4 FY2024's whole stored body was the long form) and became the headline when the title was skipped (Q1/Q2 FY2025). The asterisk note's opener was already dropped by the lede's table-line rule, but its wrapped continuations were served — and in the leading-bullet block the opener starts a bullet item. The quarter title was skipped twice over: the residue predicate's digit rule (≤4 words + digit) reads a 3-word title as a street / city-postal line, and the bare-masthead lookahead reads the date + page number as residue and the unterminated "[1] Adjusted EBITDA is a non-GAAP financial" as the real headline — Q1 survived only because its "[1] This is a non-GAAP financial measure." ends with a period. The fallback design ("use the skipped title when no other headline is found") was rejected: with the title skipped, the loop picks the letter's first prose sentence as a Franken-headline via the wrap joiner, so the fallback would have to fire on prose-shaped picks — stopping the skip is cleaner, and the blast proves no title steals a real verb headline in the cohort. The note-block consumption takes any non-boundary line as a continuation (unlike the footnote block's plausibility test, which would stop mid-note on "GAAP results table ..." / "Coinbase's Class A common stock"); the opener is the guard because both regexes match boilerplate only. The repair uses the batch-164 defect-body branch (stored body IS the defect) with a first-paragraph opener gate — an `opener in live` check would have passed Q3 FY2024, whose live text contains the prose opener after two footnote fragments; the first-paragraph check refuses it.

**Deliberately not done:** Q3 FY2024's repair — its live text serves the [1] footnote's second sentence ("These losses were $92 million ...", the batch-166 limitation, now served because the footnote sits at the lede head) and the [3] definition's "USDC (" wrap tail (the 4-cap lead fails the 2–3-cap plausibility rule); both need their own design + blast. The asterisk note's "During" form and quoted-term form stay uncovered (neither served today). COIN Q1/Q2 FY2026's disclaimer-stored rows (deck handling is a separate design question). COIN Q3 FY2025's "Fellow Shareholders," greeting lines (live drops them; the row is substantively fine).

**Blast-radius method:** Old-vs-new live extraction via `git stash` over cached exhibit HTML for 34 tickers, 268 quarters (batch-160 cohort + SKYD + GEV): 4 diffs, all intended (COIN Q4 FY2024, Q1/Q2/Q3 FY2025). The orphaned-sentence opener changes nothing in the cohort (new-vs-new2 blast empty). 17 new tests; the behavioral pin (`scripts/batch167_pin.py`) fails 8/10 checks on stashed old code. The repair's live text is byte-identical to the blast's new extraction for all 3 repaired rows.

**Related:** Closes the batch-166 COIN follow-ups in full (rounding-note skip, asterisk-note skip, quarter-title headline) for Q4 FY2024 + Q1/Q2 FY2025. Still open: ABNB prose-interleaved tables; CART "And …" sentence; ARCC table-section tail + MCO section-pick body divergence; AFRM/LIN image-only blanks; EOG supplement-only blanks; SKYD pre-merger blanks; COIN Q3 2026 unfilled; COIN Q3 FY2024 (second-sentence + USDC-wrap shapes); COIN Q1/Q2 FY2026 deck rows; pending-filing cohort at 22; Wikipedia 403s; the batch-121 transient-window limitation. **New:** the repair's first-paragraph opener gate (a live body must OPEN with the letter prose, not merely contain it) — Q3 FY2024 is the attested case for it.

## 2026-11-01 — Batch 168: footnote second sentences + paren wrap tails (COIN Q3 FY2024)

**What:** Two rules closing the batch-167 COIN Q3 FY2024 follow-up. (1) An anaphoric second-sentence chain in `_footnote_def_block_len`: after a definition terminates, the next line is consumed as part of the block when it opens with an anaphoric determiner (These/This/Those/That/Both/Such) whose noun appears in the definition just consumed. (2) A parenthetical abbreviation branch in `_is_plausible_continuation`: a line starting with 2+ all-caps letters opening a lowercase parenthetical ("USDC (net of USDC lent or") is a plausible wrap continuation. Plus the stored COIN Q3 FY2024 row repaired (210 → 493) under the batch-157 gates with the defect-body branch.

**Why:** The stored Q3 FY2024 row was the title + rounding note + the [1] footnote itself, and its live text served two footnote residues: the [1] footnote's second sentence ("These losses were $92 million / after reflecting the tax impact.") — the flattening puts the sentence break on a line boundary, so the block stopped at the first sentence's termination — and the [3] definition's "USDC (" wrap tail, whose 4-cap lead failed the 2–3-cap abbreviation branch. A capital-led sentence after a terminated definition is otherwise indistinguishable from new prose, so the anaphora guard is the noun-in-definition test, exact not stemmed; the definition's words accumulate across its wraps because the noun sits on a wrap line. The paren branch drops the termination guard (a tail terminating on its own line is still the tail) but keeps the no-digits guard, and the line is only reachable as the wrap of an unterminated definition.

**Deliberately not done:** A plural-tolerant anaphora test — the Q4 FY2025 "Both measurements represent ..." second sentence stays uncovered because its definition says "period of measurement". It needs its own design: consuming the second sentence would then consume the following "4 Coinbase Crypto Trading Volume" key line via the digit-led plausibility rule, so the interaction with the wrapped-term definition has to be settled first. No path serves that line today. Also not done: the "See"-led bare-numbered footnote pointer watch item, the asterisk-note "During"/quoted-term forms, and the COIN Q1/Q2 FY2026 deck rows.

**Blast-radius method:** Old-vs-new live extraction via `git stash` over cached exhibit HTML for 34 tickers, 269 quarters (batch-160 cohort + SKYD + GEV + the newly cached COIN Q3 FY2024 exhibit): 1 diff, intended (COIN Q3 FY2024 — the two residues out, lede opens with the letter prose). The other 268 quarters are byte-identical. 4 new tests; the behavioral pin (3 of 4 fail on stashed old code) is the test file itself — no separate pin script this batch, since the stash check covers it.

**Related:** Closes the batch-167 COIN Q3 FY2024 follow-up in full (second-sentence + USDC-wrap shapes). Still open: ABNB prose-interleaved tables; CART "And …" sentence; ARCC table-section tail + MCO section-pick body divergence; AFRM/LIN image-only blanks; EOG supplement-only blanks; SKYD pre-merger blanks; COIN Q3 2026 NULL; COIN Q1/Q2 FY2026 deck rows; pending-filing cohort at 22; Wikipedia 403s; the batch-121 transient-window limitation. **New:** the plural-mismatch anaphora limitation ("Both measurements") is the attested case for a plural-tolerant test.

## 2026-11-01 — Batch 169: shareholder-letter salutations skip as blocks; page-line guard bounds the longest line (NFLX/AXON/SKYD/COIN)

**What:** Two rules. (1) `_salutation_block_len`: a shareholder-letter salutation ("Fellow shareholders,", "Dear Shareholders,", "Fellow Affirm Shareholders,", or the split "Fellow" / "Shareholders,") skips as a block at all four collection paths (headline loop, lede, leading-bullet block, Highlights sections). In the headline loop a salutation means the letter has no headline at all (NFLX: date / salutation / bullets), so the lede starts after it, like the dateline branch. (2) The page-line guard's 1000-char bound is on the longest LINE in the first paragraph, not the paragraph: the section path joins a bullet list with "\n", so a 1385-char bullet list read as one paragraph although no line exceeds 350 chars. Plus 5 stored rows repaired (NFLX 2026-09-30 329 → 308, AXON 2024-12-31 888 → 862, AXON 2025-12-31 1028 → 1002, SKYD 2026-03-31 2693 → 2672, COIN 2025-09-30 355 → 812).

**Why:** The salutation was served three ways: as the headline (NFLX, 8 quarters — the headline loop picks the first short line it reaches), as lede content (COIN Q3 FY2025's split form, SKYD mid-body, twice in Q1 FY2026), and as a leading-bullet continuation (AXON, 8 quarters — the block appends it as a wrap of the last bullet). The pattern is salutation-shaped only: an optional single capitalized word between the opener and "shareholders", an optional trailing comma/period, and the line must END at the salutation — prose mentioning shareholders never matches, and an inline salutation ("Dear Shareholders, This is my first letter ...") never matches, because dropping that line would eat the prose with it. The guard refinement was forced by the salutation fix itself: once NFLX letters are headline-less, Q3 FY2024's 5-bullet list (1385 chars, no line over 350) was discarded as a "page-line" and live went None — a live regression for a quarter that used to extract (with the salutation as its headline). A single line over 1000 chars still trips the guard exactly as before.

**Deliberately not done:** The "◦" sub-bullet truncation — the leading-bullet block's item-close rule checks `nxt.startswith(("•", "-", "*"))` and "◦" is not in the tuple, so NFLX live stops at the first "◦" sub-bullet and loses the remaining bullets. It is a separate defect class with its own blast review (including "◦"/"·" as bullet starts), and 6 NFLX rows were refused by the repair's new preservation gate for exactly this. Also not done: the NFLX 2024-09-30 stored source-label correction ("Earnings release Q1 FY2024" for a Q3 FY2024 period) — the label is demonstrably wrong and live is correct, but the source-mismatch gate is load-bearing for exhibit identity and the correction belongs with a deliberate decision, not smuggled into a highlights repair. The plural-tolerant anaphora test stays undone — a DB scan finds zero stored rows serving "Both measurements", so there is no repair pending, only defense-in-depth.

**Blast-radius method:** Old-vs-new live extraction via `git stash` over cached exhibit HTML for 36 tickers, 284 quarters (batch-160 cohort + SKYD + GEV + NFLX + AXON): 19 diffs, all intended (AXON ×8, NFLX ×8, SKYD ×2 — salutation lines only). The other 265 quarters are byte-identical. The guard refinement changes nothing in the cohort (new-vs-new2 blast empty) — it only fires on NFLX Q3 FY2024, outside the blast's 8-quarter window; that quarter was verified separately by live re-fetch (1385 chars, 5 bullets, no salutation). 6 new tests; all 4 end-to-end shapes fail on stashed old code. The repair adds a stored-core-preservation gate (every stored paragraph except date/salutation lines must survive in live) — the first gate that refused rows for dropping content rather than for carrying residue.

**Related:** Closes the batch-167 COIN Q3 FY2025 greeting wart (repaired this batch — batch 168's entry said it was not repaired) and the NFLX salutation-as-headline / AXON bullet-block salutation shapes. Still open: ABNB prose-interleaved tables; CART "And …" sentence; ARCC table-section tail + MCO section-pick body divergence; AFRM/LIN image-only blanks; EOG supplement-only blanks; SKYD pre-merger blanks; COIN Q3 2026 NULL; COIN Q1/Q2 FY2026 deck rows; pending-filing cohort at 22; Wikipedia 403s; the batch-121 transient-window limitation. **New:** the "◦" sub-bullet truncation is the attested next item (6 NFLX repairs blocked on it); the NFLX 2024-09-30 source-label mislabel is the attested case for a source-label correction decision.

## 2026-11-01 — Batch 170: "◦" sub-bullets are bullet starts in the leading-bullet block (NFLX)

**What:** `_LEADING_BULLET_STARTS = ("•", "-", "*", "◦")` — "◦" (white bullet, U+25E6) joins the leading-bullet block's bullet-start set, in the item-start check, the item-close next-line check, and `_current_leading_item_text`. Plus 7 stored NFLX rows repaired (2024-12-31 801 → 1494, 2025-03-31 1134 → 1456, 2025-09-30 929 → 1442, 2025-12-31 636 → 1340, 2026-03-31 1332 → 1606, 2026-06-30 767 → 1644, 2026-09-30 308 → 1714).

**Why:** Netflix nests "◦" sub-bullets under "•" items. The block appended a "◦" line as a wrap continuation of its "•" parent; the line ends with terminal punctuation and the next line is another "◦" line, so the item-close rule fired and the block broke — NFLX Q4 FY2024 live was 204 chars (one "•" item + one "◦" line) against an 801-char stored row, and the batch-169 preservation gate refused 6 rows for exactly this. One shared tuple for the three sites so they cannot drift again. The "◦" glyph is attested as a bullet marker in 606 stored lines across 39 tickers, so the fix is not NFLX-only in principle — but the blast proves it only changes NFLX in this cohort, because the other filers' "◦" lines are served by the lede path, not the block.

**Deliberately not done:** "·"/"▪"/"●" were NOT added — they are top-level bullets served fine by the lede path with no attested leading-block truncation; adding them would need their own attestation + blast review. The lone-marker rule was NOT extended — a lone "◦" line never stands alone (0 of 606 stored "◦" lines), so there is nothing to drop. `_looks_like_headline` was NOT touched — no attested "◦"-as-headline case. The block's append-then-break close rule was NOT changed: it includes the first prose line after the bullets ("Our summary results and forecast for Q1 are below.") in the new live text — pre-existing, quarter-dependent behavior (a table/heading after the bullets breaks before appending), accepted as honest content; reordering it would touch every leading-block extraction. The NFLX 2024-09-30 source-label correction was NOT smuggled in — the source-mismatch gate refused the row again, and the label fix is a separate deliberate decision.

**Blast-radius method:** Old-vs-new live extraction via `git stash` over cached exhibit HTML for 36 tickers, 284 quarters: 8 diffs, all NFLX, every one a pure append (old text is a prefix of new; every added line a "•"/"◦" bullet or the "summary results" transition sentence). 276 quarters byte-identical. 3 new tests; all 3 fail on stashed old code. The repair script's `norm` gained paren-spacing normalization (strip space after "(" / before ")") — the stored rows were flattened with spaced footnote markers ("( Adolescence 1 )") vs the current attached form ("(Adolescence1)"), and without it the preservation gate mistook a flattening artifact for dropped content (NFLX 2025-03-31); same class as the existing letter-digit join, repair-script-only, extractor untouched.

**Related:** Closes the batch-169 "◦" sub-bullet truncation follow-up (6 blocked repairs unblocked; the batch-169 NFLX 2026-09-30 row re-repaired). Still open: ABNB prose-interleaved tables; CART "And …" sentence; ARCC table-section tail + MCO section-pick body divergence; AFRM/LIN image-only blanks; EOG supplement-only blanks; SKYD pre-merger blanks; COIN Q3 2026 NULL; COIN Q1/Q2 FY2026 deck rows; pending-filing cohort at 22; Wikipedia 403s; the batch-121 transient-window limitation. **New:** the append-then-break closing-line wart as a possible cohort-wide review item; the NFLX 2024-09-30 source-label mislabel as the attested case for a source-label correction decision.

## 2026-11-01 — Batch 171: stale "Q1" source labels repaired; backfill self-heals stale labels (14 rows)

**What:** `_correct_stale_source_label` (`services/financials.py`) + wiring into `_enrich_press_release_highlights`, and a gated repair of 14 stored `press_release_source` labels ("Q1 FYyyyy" → "Q3 FYyyyy", XOM "Q1 FY2026" → "Q2 FY2026"; PLUG/XOM/MET/UNH/HD/AMZN/NET/NFLX/SOFI/KO/ABBV/MS/PEP/BRK.B). Highlights untouched — only the label string moves.

**Why:** The labels are pre-batch-52 fossils: the old `_assign_fy_and_quarter` counted forward from Q1 within each fiscal-year group, so a seed window opening mid-cycle labeled its oldest 10-Q "Q1". Batch 52's count-back fix corrected the algorithm (already pinned by test), but the backfill only fills blank fields, so a wrong-but-present label stayed wrong forever — the rows sat mislabeled for weeks. The correction fires only on a two-against-one vote: stored quarter ≠ live quarter, live quarter == the row's XBRL fiscal_period, fiscal years agree. That guard is load-bearing in both directions: it leaves the 88 FY-only offset rows alone (quarter agrees; the FY difference is the Feb-year-end convention question, not a mislabel) and it leaves the 0000922224 row alone (its fiscal_year 2021 cannot match any live quarter). The repair's same-exhibit gate (live highlights contain the stored text's first content paragraph) proved each live exhibit is the same release; the first version required a bullet-led paragraph and wrongly skipped 5 prose-form rows (HD/SOFI/KO/PEP/BRK.B) — the headline alone proves the exhibit once discovery matched the period by report date.

**Deliberately not done:** The highlights were NOT replaced — the live text for AMZN Q3 FY2024 is 837 chars against 2117 stored (the live extraction drops its bullets), which is a separate extractor question; replacing highlights would have mixed a label repair with an extraction change, so the label repair went ahead on the headline match and the AMZN bullet drop is carried as the next defect hunt. The FY-offset convention (88 rows where the source FY differs from the DB fiscal_year) was NOT "fixed" — for Feb-year-end companies the source label's FY is the company's true FY and the DB column uses calendar-year-of-period-end; that is a column-convention question, not a label error. The self-heal is opportunistic, not complete: it only fires when the backfill already re-fetches a period (a row with a blank disclosure field) — the 7 fully-filled fossil rows were fixed by the repair script, and the code prevents recurrence rather than replacing the script.

**Blast-radius method:** None on extraction — the change touches only the service layer; the extractor and discovery labeling are untouched. The repair itself is the live verification (every label re-derived from live EDGAR discovery + exhibit re-fetch). 4 new tests; the module fails collection on stashed old code. The repair script dogfoods the shipped helper (`_correct_stale_source_label` imported from the service module), so the gate logic is the same code the product runs.

**Related:** Closes the batch-169/170 NFLX 2024-09-30 source-label follow-up and the "stored labels are never re-validated" gap. Still open: ABNB prose-interleaved tables; CART "And …" sentence; ARCC table-section tail + MCO section-pick body divergence; AFRM/LIN image-only blanks; EOG supplement-only blanks; SKYD pre-merger blanks; COIN Q3 2026 NULL; COIN Q1/Q2 FY2026 deck rows; pending-filing cohort at 22; Wikipedia 403s; the batch-121 transient-window limitation. **New:** AMZN Q3 FY2024 live bullet drop (837 vs 2117 chars) is the attested next defect hunt; the 0000922224 2024-06-30 fiscal_year-2021 row is the attested case for a row-fiscal-fields audit.

## 2026-11-01 — Batch 172: Business Wire datelines recognized; AMZN live extraction restored

**What:** `_DATELINE_LEAD_RE` (`data/filings.py`) gains a Business Wire branch, and `_leading_bullet_block` skips bare period-header lines before any bullet is collected (reusing `_is_lede_period_header`). AMZN Q3 FY2024 live goes 837 → 2635 chars (headline + all 13 metric bullets, no dateline).

**Why:** The batch-171 attested defect had two stacked causes in the same path. First, Amazon's dateline has no "CITY, ST" comma — "SEATTLE—(BUSINESS WIRE) October 31, 2024—..." — so the dateline regex (batch 118's "location + dash" shape, batch 155's mixed-case state) never matched it; the leading block returned None and the lede fallback spent a budget line on the dateline, serving 5 of 13 bullets. The same wire form appears at FICO, Dollar General, Palantir, and DoorDash with different separators and an optional date, so the branch covers all five attested shapes with the literal "(BUSINESS WIRE)" tag as the anchor. Second, Q4 releases put a bare "Fourth Quarter 2024" label between the dateline and the bullets, which also broke the block (the lede already drops it). The stored 2117-char business bullets are a Sep-29 seed-time artifact — the old bare `\bhighlights?\b` section regex matched the "Some other highlights..." intro sentence — and the live result now follows the batch-102 financial priority instead: the metric block wins over the later business section (Q1 2024's old 3999-char capped business-section result becomes the 13-bullet metric block, same as Halliburton). Stored rows are untouched — the backfill only fills blanks — so the fix governs future seeds and blank-row backfills, not the pages as they render today.

**Deliberately not done:** A lede-loop dateline skip was implemented, blasted, and REVERTED. The 73-diff blast showed the freed budget line is usually filled by junk the existing skips don't recognize: footnote definitions ("1. Non-GAAP measure...", "1 Refer to the tables..."), a contact role line ("Chief Financial Officer"), bare table headings ("… GAAP Revenue"), and a table data row (DASH). Skipping the dateline in the lede is correct in principle, but it needs those tail guards first — extending four more regexes in one batch would have multiplied the blast surface. The 2026 Amazon key-figures callout (prose lines before the dateline) and Halliburton's "CITY – Month DD, YYYY –" dateline form are likewise carried, not fixed: the first is a new-format design question, the second is pre-existing and stable across all 11 HAL quarters.

**Blast-radius method:** Old-vs-new live extraction over 436 quarters across 35 tickers (the wire cohort + prior-batch regression cohorts; HTML cached, byte-identical input): 10 diffs, all AMZN, all improvements; the other 34 tickers byte-identical. The 3 new-only files are discovery flakiness (old run logged NO EXHIBIT URL during SEC 503s). 6 new tests; 5 fail on stashed old code. Full suite 1257 green.

**Related:** Closes the batch-171 AMZN live bullet drop. Still open: the lede-dateline skip (needs footnote/contact/table-heading tail guards); AMZN 2026 callout format; HAL "CITY – date –" dateline form; the "Some other highlights..." sentence-form section intro (no attested need without a leading block); the 0000922224 fiscal_year-2021 row; the standing ABNB/CART/ARCC/MCO/AFRM/LIN/EOG/SKYD/COIN/pending-filing/Wikipedia/batch-121/CCI/STZ items and the batch-157 gate skips.

## 2026-11-01 — Batch 173: lede tail guards; the dateline skip is dead

**What:** Tail guards at all three collection paths (`data/filings.py`): a footnote-pointer opener (`_FOOTNOTE_POINTER_RE`, incl. a parenthesized "(See ...)" alternative), a bare-numbered footnote opener (`_is_bare_numbered_footnote`, terminated + 500-char cap + capital after the number), a bare quote-attribution role-line skip (`_is_bare_role_line`), a symbol-legend dash form in `_LEDE_SYMBOL_LEGEND_RE`, a "Key Points:" heading skip, and the pointer/bare-number branches in the salvage-path sentence filter. 28 stored rows repaired live (`scripts/batch173_repair.py`).

**Why:** Batch 172's reverted dateline skip left a prerequisite: the freed budget line fills with footnote definitions, role lines, table headings, and data rows. This batch adds the guards instead of the skip — and the blast killed the skip itself: a whole-line dateline skip over the full 1,699-exhibit cache produced 130 diffs, every one a lead-sentence removal (AAPL's dateline sentence carries revenue $94.9B / EPS $0.97; 70 stored rows serve a dateline sentence as lede line 1). A bare-only skip would change 0 quarters; prefix-stripping is 130 rows of churn for cosmetic gain. The skip is dead — do not re-propose it.

**Design notes:** The guards must live at every collection path, not just the lede loop: Walmart's legend only surfaced through its Third-Quarter Highlights section (the section collector had the footnote-def skip from batch 166 but no legend/role skip), which the lede-path tests never exercised — caught by the live repair's residue scan, fixed, and covered by section-path tests. The bare-number cap was raised twice on blast evidence (300 → 400 → 500) as attested footnotes ran to 470 chars; the mega-lines it guards against are 3000+ (Ford) and unterminated (Affirm), so the margin is wide. The capital-after-the-number guard is load-bearing: without it the branch eats batch 164's wrapped bullet continuation ("1 million shares are included in the diluted count."). The repair's line-level preservation gate excludes exactly the defect classes (plus rule lines, call/webcast notice lines, and the extractor's own table-line predicate) — the batch-170 paragraph gate has no defect exclusion and tripped on DIS/MCO's own defect lines, which is why both gates exist. The gate's skips are all correct behavior: 13 targets have no stored row, and the 3 content skips (AXP notice form, CSX bullet gap, DASH letter opener) are pre-existing vintage gaps, not regressions.

**Blast-radius method:** Cache-only old-vs-new over 1,699 exhibits / 142 tickers (HTML cached at `~/workspace/goals/tickerlens-app-build/hidden_files/blast_html`, ~40s per run, no fetching — /tmp is a 512MB tmpfs and the discovery blast died on disk-full): 33 diffs, all improvements; 1,666 byte-identical. 11 new tests; full suite 1268 green.

**Related:** Closes the batch-172 tail-guard prerequisite. Still open: the 110-row / 151-line audit cohort (batch-174 follow-up — UAL, BLK, PHM, AWK, TT, TTWO, KKR, MRK, HIG, CEG, KMX + singles); the 500-char cap's two survivors (MCO 556-char disclaimer, AXP 671-char FX footnote); AXP's "will be held" conference-call notice form; the CSX/DASH vintage gaps; MCO's table-header lede line; DIS's "Financial Results..." heading lede line; the lede 6-line budget; AMZN 2026 callout format; HAL "CITY – date –" dateline form; the "Some other highlights..." intro; the 0000922224 fiscal_year-2021 row; the standing ABNB/CART/ARCC/MCO/AFRM/LIN/EOG/SKYD/COIN/pending-filing/Wikipedia/batch-121/CCI/STZ items and the batch-157 gate skips.

## 2026-11-01 — Batch 174: audit-cohort repair (42 rows); bare period fragments break the bullet block

**What:** Three waves against the batch-173 audit cohort (`data/filings.py`, `scripts/batch174_audit.py`, `scripts/batch174_repair.py`): (1) 23 clean live repairs under the batch-173 gates; (2) a repair-script-only gate extension — contact-block lines are defect lines and the stored headline is the first real stored paragraph — repairing 14 AWK/PHM rows whose stored headline was the contact block; (3) an extractor fix, `_is_bare_period_fragment`, breaking the leading-bullet block and the Highlights section collector on bare period-fragment lines ("Three Months", "Six Months", "Ended", "Year Ended", "Full Year"), repairing 5 BLK rows. 42 rows repaired in total; the cohort falls from 83 rows / 91 lines to 41 / 44.

**Why:** The batch-173 audit found the cohort but its repair covered only 44 targets; the remaining 83 rows split into three classes, each with its own unblock. The contact-block class needed no extractor change — the live extractor already skips the contact block, so only the repair gates were wrong (they compared against a residue headline and treated residue lines as content). The fragment class needed an extractor change: the lede loop already drops fragments via `_is_lede_table_line`, but the bullet block and section collector break only on strong heading shapes, and a fragment matches neither the period header (needs a year) nor the date header (needs "ended" or a month).

**Design notes:** The fragment predicate is deliberately a strict subset of `_is_lede_table_line`: 1–4 words, every word a period token, never bullet-led. A prose sentence carries a month name or digits ("Three months ended September 30, 2025 ..."), a bullet carries its marker, and a year-bearing header ("Full Year 2025 Actual") carries digits — none qualifies. The bullet-block break (not skip) is load-bearing: skipping the fragment would let the table's data rows in as continuations; breaking ends the block at the table's start, mirroring the existing break on table headings/units/period headers. The repair script's blast gate caught a stale-directory mistake during the batch: it compared new-extractor live text against the batch-173 blast dir and flagged all 5 BLK rows (live 26 chars shorter — exactly the fragment drop), which is why the script now points at the batch-174 blast dir. The 2 AWK skips are a real content question, not a gate artifact: live drops the stored subtitle lines ("AFFIRMS LONG-TERM GROWTH TARGETS") because the headline pass serves one title line only. The TTWO repaired rows still open with "(Investor Relations) (Corporate Press)" — pre-existing wart, live headline matches stored, not a regression.

**Blast-radius method:** Cache-only old-vs-new over 1,699 exhibits / 142 tickers (same cache as batch 173; `scripts/batch173_cache_blast.py` re-run as `/tmp/blast174c/{old,new}` via `git stash`): 5 diffs, all improvements, 1,694 byte-identical — BLK ×5, each exactly the fragment lines dropped, the SUMMARY title line kept. 5 new tests; full suite 1273 green.

**Related:** Closes 42 of the 83 audit-cohort rows. Still open: the 41-row / 44-line remainder (batch-175 follow-up — TT ×7 table rows + truncated headlines, KKR ×4 footnote-keyed headings, BLK ×3 dropped expense bullets, MRK ×3, AWK ×2 subtitle lines, CSX/DASH vintage gaps, CEG ×2, KMX ×2, UAL "Customer Experience" residue, AXP "will be held" notice form, plus singles); the 500-char cap's two survivors; MCO's table-header lede line; the BLK SUMMARY table-title line (separate class from the fragment); DIS's heading lede line; the lede 6-line budget; the standing ABNB/CART/ARCC/MCO/AFRM/LIN/EOG/SKYD/COIN/pending-filing/Wikipedia/batch-121/CCI/STZ items and the batch-157 gate skips.

## 2026-11-01 — Batch 175: Trane Wingdings "l" bullets; bare-dollar units line; `_is_lede_data_row` bullet exemption

**What:** Three extractor changes (`data/filings.py`) plus a TT-only live repair (`scripts/batch175_repair.py`, 7/7 rows): (1) `_BULLET_LEAD_RE` gains the Wingdings "l" bullet (`l\s`, space required); (2) `_TABLE_UNITS_RE` gains the bare-dollar units form (`^\$\s*,\s*(millions|thousands|billions)\b`); (3) `_is_lede_data_row` exempts bullet-led lines outright. Repair-script-only gate extensions: stored table-residue lines are defect lines, and a stored headline that is a strict prefix of the live headline is accepted.

**Why:** Trane's releases use a Wingdings "l" glyph for bullets and a paren-less "$, millions except EPS ..." units line — two shapes no predicate recognized, so the section collector ran through the summary table and served its data rows as highlights, and the figure-dense EPS bullet was misread as a data row. The `_is_lede_data_row` exemption is the principled fix: the predicate is a semantic claim ("a bullet-led line is never a table row"), and it was the only one of the three table predicates lacking it (`_is_lede_table_line` and `_looks_like_financial_table` already exempt bullets).

**Design notes:** The "l" bullet's space requirement is load-bearing: a lone "l" is not an English word, but a line starting with the letter "l" glued to a word ("lower revenue ...") is prose. The bare-dollar units alternative is anchored (`^`) while the paren branch is unanchored — a prose "$" line ("Reported revenues of $5.4 billion") never starts "$,". The exemption was kept whole rather than narrowed to exclude dash bullets: dash-led lines were already treated as bullets by the other two predicates, so narrowing would have created two definitions of "bullet" in the codebase. The blast proves the cost is confined to 4 exhibits, each reviewed: AJG's labels now render with their values (more readable), AWK's CEO subtitle is served (a carried defect, fixed), CARR trades a title-grade subtitle for the 6th bullet (a recorded wash — the 6-line lede budget working as designed). The subtitle mechanism is worth naming: `_is_lede_table_label` classified the subtitle as a table label only because the bullet below it was misread as a values row; fixing the predicate fixes the classification, and the budget does the rest. The repair's prefix-headline acceptance is TT-scoped by its targets (7 TT rows) — a stored headline that is a prefix of the live one is a truncated seed artifact, not a mismatch. TT 2024-12-31 was probed and is live == stored under the new extractor, so it stays out of the repair.

**Blast-radius method:** Cache-only old-vs-new over 1,699 exhibits / 142 tickers (`scripts/batch175_cache_blast.py`, same cache as batches 173–174, ~40s per run): 4 diffs, all reviewed above, 1,695 byte-identical. TT is not in the cache, so the TT fix itself is verified by direct live probes (Q3 FY2024) and the repair's own live re-fetch gates, not by the blast. 5 new tests; full suite 1278 green.

**Related:** Closes the batch-174 TT ×7 follow-up and the `_is_lede_data_row` bullet-exemption gap. Still open: the 34-row / 37-line audit remainder (KKR ×4, BLK ×3, MRK ×3, AWK ×2, CSX/DASH, CEG ×2, KMX ×2, UAL, AXP, singles); the 500-char cap's two survivors; MCO's table-header lede line; DIS's heading lede line; the lede 6-line budget (this batch's AWK/CARR trades are its visible cost); the standing ABNB/CART/ARCC/MCO/AFRM/LIN/EOG/SKYD/COIN/pending-filing/Wikipedia/batch-121/CCI/STZ items and the batch-157 gate skips.

## 2026-11-02 — Batch 177: lede heading / table-header lines (MCO + DIS carried items)

**What:** Three extractor changes (`data/filings.py`) plus a repair run (`scripts/batch177_repair.py`, 49/75 rows repaired): new `_is_lede_colon_heading` (colon-ended list-intro / section heading — the "the following:" / "as follows:" idiom, or a bare noun phrase ≤8 words with no verb; "said"/"says" vetoes quote attributions), new `_is_lede_table_header` (figure-less, capital-led metric-label line — repeated label word or Adjusted/After-Tax/Pre-Tax/Non-GAAP/GAAP qualifier; headline verb vetoes sub-headlines), and `_is_lede_heading_fragment` extended 4→8 words with a headline-verb + no-figures guard. Wired into the lede loop and, post-selection, into `_extract_highlights_section` (drop from the winning section only). Repair: MCO ×6, DIS ×8, DDOG ×8, BSX ×8, ETR ×8, GE ×7, APH ×3, APD ×1 — 49 rows, live==blast verified, DB-verified.

**Why:** Moody's Q4 2025 served its summary-table title ("FOURTH QUARTER AND FULL YEAR SUMMARY FINANCIALS") and column-header line ("MCO Diluted EPS MCO Adjusted Diluted EPS2") as lede lines above the CEO quote; Capital One served "Impact After-Tax Diluted EPS" below its adjusting-items intro; Disney served "Fiscal 2026 outlook:" / "Financial Results for the Quarter:" above its bullets. Each heading consumed one of the 6 lede lines and starved a real bullet (MCO Q4 lost its FY bullets; DIS Q3 lost the $9B buyback bullet). The probe showed the class is DB-wide (273 stored rows carry one of the three shapes), so the fix is predicates, not per-row edits.

**Design notes:** The colon predicate's idiom branch ("the following" + ≤3-word bare noun-phrase tail) is load-bearing: "The quarter included the following adjusting items:" fires, while "Our guidance assumes the following metrics will improve:" does not (the tail carries the verb "will"). The table-header predicate's repeated-word-or-qualifier test is what separates "MCO Diluted EPS MCO Adjusted Diluted EPS2" from a sub-headline — a sub-headline always carries a headline verb ("Q2 ORGANIC SALES AND ADJUSTED EPS EXCEED OUTLOOK", "COMPANY MAINTAINS FULL-YEAR REVENUE AND EPS GUIDANCE", "RESETTING DIVIDEND TO ACCELERATE DEBT REDUCTION"), and the verb veto is what keeps all of them. The caps extension keeps the ≤4-word behavior byte-identical (a year-carrying 4-word line like "FOURTH QUARTER 2025 RESULTS" is still a fragment under the old bound); the digit/verb guards apply only to the 5–8 word band. The section drop is post-selection BY DESIGN: the first attempt dropped headings in the per-section cleanup, which changed candidate financial density and flipped Akamai's competition — the Full-year section won over the lede's Revenue section and the Security/Delivery/Compute bullets were lost in 3 Q4 rows (10 bullets, caught by the blast's true-loss check). Reverted; the post-selection form leaves competition untouched and only strips the served text. The repair's three added residue guards (title line via `_is_trailing_heading`, $-row ≤120 chars, spaced-decimal "$ .44") are strict blocks: 14 of the 75 targets trip them (COF ×7 newly-served title lines, ADBE title lines, CHD/CMG pre-existing title lines, GEV GW data rows) and stay skipped rather than writing new residue.

**Blast-radius method:** Cache-only old-vs-new over 1,699 exhibits / 142 tickers (`scripts/batch175_cache_blast.py`, baseline `/tmp/blast177c/old`, new `/tmp/blast177c/new3`): 130 diffs, 1,569 byte-identical, 0 true content losses (every removed line is a defect line or re-appears re-wrapped), 98 gained bullets. The reverted pre-selection attempt (`/tmp/blast177c/new2`) had the same 130 diffs with 10 true losses, all Akamai. 10 new tests; full suite 1296 green. The pin is behavioral: on stashed old code the new helpers don't exist (collection error).

**Related:** Closes MCO's table-header lede line and DIS's heading lede line (batch-173/175/176 carried items) and the colon-heading class DB-wide. Still open: the batch-177 repair remainder (26 rows — COF ×7, GEV ×8, CMG ×5, AIG ×2, ADBE, APD, CHD, CPB) with per-row skip reasons in the repair run; the exposed table-row/title leak (ARCC ×6 recon rows above the 80-char $-figures cap, COF ×7 title lines, EL spaced-decimal rows, ARCC date fragment) — needs its own predicates before those rows are repaired; the 500-char cap's two survivors; the lede 6-line budget; the batch-176 31-row audit remainder; AFRM's page-header-prefix false positive; the standing ABNB/CART/ARCC/MCO/AFRM/LIN/EOG/SKYD/COIN/pending-filing/Wikipedia/batch-121/CCI/STZ items and the batch-157 gate skips.

## 2026-11-02 — Batch 176: paren-footnote prose continuations are not footnote definitions

**What:** One extractor change (`data/filings.py`) plus a repair run (`scripts/batch176_repair.py`, 1/31 rows repaired): new `_is_paren_prose_continuation`, wired into `_opens_footnote_def`'s paren branch only. A paren-led line is prose, not a footnote definition, when its text after the marker starts lowercase ("(MRC) protocol ...", "(QoQ) and ...") or its first sentence is a single 2+ letter alphabetic word with a new sentence after it ("(IV) KEYTRUDA. Merck also announced ..."). Repair: FIS 2025-12-31 only (984→1362); the other 30 targets skip on gates, each with a recorded reason.

**Why:** The paren footnote shape (`^\([a-z0-9]+\)`) is the loosest marker in the codebase — it matches any parenthesized token at line start, including an abbreviation a wrapped sentence breaks on. Arista's bullet, Plug's bullet and Merck's sentence all wrap exactly that way, and the section collector was dropping their tails as footnote definitions. The fresh-vs-stored diff proved it is a live regression, not stale data: ANET's stored row has the (MRC) tail while fresh extraction (pre-fix) cut it off.

**Design notes:** The previous line is deliberately not consulted. The tempting guard — "a footnote definition never follows an unterminated line" — is wrong: Lilly's genuine "(1) Tirzepatide is marketed ..." definition follows the revenue table's last row, which is unterminated, so that guard would keep real footnotes. Both guards therefore read only the post-marker text shape. The 2+ letter, all-alpha bound on the single-word rule is load-bearing: CEG's "(210) (0.67)" splits on its decimal point and "U.S. ..." splits after one letter, so neither fires and both stay footnote/table residue; a bare "(1)" has no text after the marker and never reaches the test.
