## 2026-11-08 — Batch 217: DAL 2024-12-31 wrapped narrative heading dropped from guidance; 1 stored row repaired

**Fix (`src/tickerlens/data/filings.py`):** DAL 2024-12-31's results-narrative heading is wrapped across two flattened lines ("Revenue Environment" / "and Outlook"), so batch 202's single-line carve-out never fired and the stored guidance ended on the two fragments. New `_wrapped_guidance_narrative_end` joins a candidate with its next non-admin line and tests the joined form against the two narrative patterns; the follower is then tested after the second fragment. The second fragment must look like a continuation (short, unterminated, lowercase/"&"/tail-word start), and for the end-open results-led regex the tail after Guidance/Outlook must be a heading tail — so a bare table label plus prose cannot join-match. Cache survey (1,699 exhibits): exactly 2 wrapped occurrences, both DAL; only 2024-12-31 is in the guidance collection path.

## 2026-11-09 — Batch 224: the noun/number-ending hole — next-line lookahead + termination lookahead

**Fix:** `_lede_next_line_continues(tail, nxt)` — a lowercase-led next line on an unterminated tail means the sentence wraps on; wired into the lede finish-item decision and the completion allowance at all three caps. `_lede_termination_ahead(collected, next_lines, limit=4)` — the hard caps (lede 8, section 16, block 18) yield when the sentence terminates within the next 4 raw lines, gated on the sentence still being unfinished. The unterminated-tail gate is load-bearing: AJG's lowercase-led pointer line after a closed quote is a new sentence's wrap tail, not a continuation — the first cut without the gate re-introduced batch 222's pointer regression and was caught by its pins.

**Blast-radius method:** Guidance + highlights old-vs-new over 1,699 exhibits (`scripts/batch222_guidance_blast.py`, `scripts/batch222_cache_blast.py`, `hidden_files/blast224/`): **0 guidance diffs, 21 highlights diffs**, all verified line-by-line. 19 terminate cleanly (the 9 target rows plus DG 2024-05-03/2024-08-02, CI 2023-09-30, AJG 2024-12-31, ETN 2023-12-31, GEV ×4, GPC 2026-09-30); ABNB 2024-06-30 flips section winners on density (intro line lost, Euro Cup sentence gained — net neutral, the rule working as designed); ABNB 2023-12-31 is partial (more prose, still mid-sentence — its sentence runs past the +4 window).

## 2026-11-10 — Batch 226: the table-residue tail class — row labels, units+period headers, caps footers, data rows, header orphans

**Fix:** `_clean_highlights_result`'s trailing trim gains five predicates plus a colon-dangling rule and a period-suffix strip on the heading test (`src/tickerlens/data/filings.py`): `_is_trailing_row_label` (verbless statement row labels — Berkshire/BlackRock/Best Buy; "share" added in the re-blast for "Net earnings per average equivalent Class A Share"), `_is_trailing_units_period_header` (units + period columns, Aon/CVS/MSCI; bare "In millions", "(Amounts …)", "(unaudited)" forms), `_is_trailing_caps_line` (AEP's "AMERICAN ELECTRIC POWER" page footer; headline-verb veto), `_is_trailing_table_data_row` (label + 2+ period values — Expedia/Colgate/Best Buy/AEP; refuses flattened-list bullets, requires 40% figure density + a label word + a range veto), `_is_trailing_table_header_orphan` (period/table headers, bare fragments, closed-vocabulary orphans, letter-less junk, ordinal fragments "Third Quarter"/"First Quarter" from the re-blast). The trim completes the cascade: a colon rule pops a short colon-ended line left dangling by residue pops ("EPS ($):", "Net earnings includes:" — gated on a residue pop, quote-verb vetoed), the heading test strips a trailing " - <period> <year>" suffix (Expedia's "… - Fourth Quarter 2023" title), and a trailing-local period header covers month/day-anchored headers — AEP's whole flattened earnings table strips to clean prose. `_is_lede_table_line` is deliberately NOT consulted in the final trim: its 262 stored-tail matches include genuine content (Cencora's "5% to 7% growth $285.0B", Centene's "GAAP diluted EPS $ 1.36", JPMorgan's truncated prose), and the first blast caught the Palantir false positive (its ◦ bullet read as a row via the 25%-density branch). The re-blast caught an `UnboundLocalError` in the new orphan predicate (`words` used before assignment); it crashed extraction for 123 exhibits in that blast run, and the blast script's "wrote 1699" line counts inputs, not files written (1576 landed). Fixed by moving the split above the branch; the re-blast is complete and the shapes that exposed it are pinned.

## 2026-11-10 — Batch 227: the wrap-fragment tail class — comma/semicolon/dangling-word cuts, truncate-at-terminator, colon-intro list labels, row-label qualifier strip

**Fix:** `_clean_highlights_result`'s trailing trim gains a wrap-fragment drop, a truncate-at-last-terminator, a colon-intro list-item-label rule, and a " - <qualifier>" strip in the row-label predicate (`src/tickerlens/data/filings.py`): `_is_trailing_wrap_fragment` (comma/semicolon end, dangling function word, open quote, short lowercase noun phrase continuing an already-unfinished line — General Mills' "market share trends", Nasdaq's "...(ARR)4 of", Kroger's comma tails), `_truncate_trailing_fragment` (Coinbase's "...on that transition. Our customers trust us...;" → "...on that transition."; Genuine Parts' "...comparable sales. Segment" → "...comparable sales." — abbreviation dots, headline-verb ";"s, imperative-led and figure-carrying fragments never truncate), `_is_trailing_list_item_label` (Berkshire's impairment label under "Net earnings includes:" — verbless, ≥8 words, figure-free, label noun; the colon rule + row-label predicate cascade-clear the block), `_is_trailing_row_label` now strips a short " - <qualifier>" (BlackRock's "...noncontrolling interests - Consolidated sponsored investment products"). Bullets without terminal periods are house style and are never touched; a trailing figure is the object of its "of", so figures are never stripped before the dangle test. The first blast caught two false positives — Cisco's "consecutive quarter" (wrapped bullet completion) and Estee Lauder's "Affirms ... 3% to 5%;" (a ";" separating headline items) — both fixed with gates (previous-line-unfinished gate; headline-verb ";" gate) and pinned as guards. The repair script's first cut had a loop-order bug (wrap-pop before truncate); fixed by mirroring the trim's order. Repair review caught a bad truncate (Linde's "...ex. FX, growth" → "...ex.") — "ex" is now an abbreviation. The first-cut tests caught the verbless test missing ordinary verbs ("reduced") — `_TRAILING_COMMON_VERBS` makes the guard real.

**Blast-radius method:** Guidance + highlights old-vs-new over 1,699 exhibits (`scripts/batch227_guidance_blast.py`, `scripts/batch227_cache_blast.py`, `hidden_files/blast227/`): **0 guidance diffs, 7 highlights diffs**, all verified line-by-line (BLK ×3; BRK.B 3-line cascade; COIN truncate; GIS fragment drop + cascade; GPC truncate). The probe (2,496 stored rows) found the complete-clause bucket (185) is mostly complete bullets, not defects — only genuine mid-sentence cuts (verified against the raw source lines after each live tail) are dropped.

## 2026-11-10 — Batch 228: the dated-dateline swallow in the leading-bullet block

**Fix:** a new `_DATED_DATELINE_LEAD_RE` (city + spelled-out month/day/year + dash) breaks `_leading_bullet_block` when a continuation line is a dated dateline sentence, and the same form joins the pre-bullet dateline skip (`src/tickerlens/data/filings.py`). `_DATELINE_LEAD_RE` never matched the dated form — its location segments allow no digits — so a dateline sentence after the leading bullets was collected as a bullet continuation, dragging the flattened earnings table behind it into the block (Centene: dateline paragraph + 7 table rows in all 8 quarters; Chevron's dateline paragraph as the block tail). ## 2026-11-10 — Batch 230: the fragmented "· <word>" bullet openers — Dollar General's table-cell wrap orphans each bullet's first word; 8 stored rows repaired

**Fix:** a new `_FRAGMENTED_BULLET_RE` (marker + one or two bare alphabetic words, no punctuation) plus `_join_fragmented_bullet_starts` (`src/tickerlens/data/filings.py`), applied post-collection to the winning Highlights section, the lede list, and the guidance section's assembled lines. Dollar General's releases typeset each bullet as a table row whose text cell wraps after the first word ("· Net" / "Sales Increased 3.4% to $10.8 Billion"), and the flattener preserves the break. The predicate is narrow by construction (a label line always carries punctuation — "• Same-restaurant sales:", "• Fiscal 2025:", "• Media: media@danaher.com" — and never matches), and the continuation must be a plain line: not another bullet (including a "◦" sub-bullet — Equifax's "• Revenues" heads one), not blank, not an all-caps heading, not a dateline, not admin/boilerplate, not a footnote-definition or table-units line. A bullet wrapping after a full clause is untouched. Live verification caught the same fragment in the guidance card, so the join covers the guidance path too.

**Blast-radius method:** Guidance + highlights old-vs-new over 1,699 exhibits (`scripts/batch230_guidance_blast.py`, `scripts/batch230_cache_blast.py`, `hidden_files/blast230/`): **7 guidance diffs, 2 highlights diffs**, all verified line-by-line (highlights DG 2026-05-01 + 2026-07-31 — all 6 bullets joined in each; guidance DG ×7 — the same wrapped bullets, including "○ The"/"○ Diluted" sub-bullets in 2024-08-02 and "· Share repurchases of up to $700 million" in 2026-07-31). The probe that scoped the batch (`scripts/batch230_probe.py`) established only 10 exhibits carry the true shape (DG ×8, ADSK ×1, DAL ×1); the other 84 marker + 1–2 word lines are labels, sub-bullet headers, or next-bullet lines that the guards correctly leave alone, and the older DG quarters' bullets lose the density competition to their prose sections, so the join is a no-op for them.

**Tests:** 14 in `tests/data/test_filings_batch230.py` (live pins on the real cached exhibits for highlights and guidance + unit pins for the predicate and the continuation guards). Full suite: **1952 passed**.

**Repair (same batch):** `scripts/batch230_repair.py` (gates A/A2/B/G/BG): **8 repaired, DB-verified** — highlights A2:2 (DG 2026-05-01, 2026-07-31 — new gate: stored equals blast-old except its headline is a strict suffix of the live headline, the seed-era wrapped-headline artifact that lost "Dollar"; the swap restores the full headline and the joined bullets), guidance G:6. Idempotent on re-run. DG_2024-08-02 has a guidance blast diff but no stored row (Q2 2024 outside the DB window) — live serves it on refresh.

## 2026-11-11 — Batch 232: the trailing dangling table/list intro — colon lines are dangling by construction, truncate-at-terminator mid-body, spaced "$" counts as a figure

**Fix:** a trailing colon-ended line is dangling by construction — whatever it introduces is not in the served text — so the batch-226 colon rule in `_clean_highlights_result` (`src/tickerlens/data/filings.py`) loses its residue-cascade gate and gains the long-intro tail phrases: new `_is_trailing_dangling_intro` pops a trailing line ending in ":" when it is short (≤6 words, bullet marker stripped — Berkshire's "Net earnings includes:", Disney's "• Fiscal 2025:") or ends on a dangling-intro tail phrase ("as follows", "adjusted for", "driven by", "includes/include", "consist of", "comprised/composed of", "set forth/summarized/described/shown/listed below", "follows", "below" — Fastenal's "...were as follows:", FedEx's "...have been adjusted for:", Gilead's bullet "...primarily driven by:"). The quote-verb veto is kept ("...commented on the results:" stays). The gate existed because a colon line in an untouched result was assumed to be a quote intro or a live list intro; both are now handled directly (quote verb veto; a list intro with no items after it is residue). Once the intro pops, the row-label predicate pops the line above it on the next iteration — the Berkshire cascade the old gate deadlocked (the intro needed a prior pop, the label needed the intro gone first). The colon lines were invisible to the other trailing rules because `_is_sentence_terminated` counts ":" as terminated, so the wrap-fragment and truncate rules never fired on them. A line whose post-terminator fragment is a dangling intro is truncated at the terminator instead of popped (new `_truncate_trailing_dangling_intro`, plus the case added to `_truncate_trailing_fragment`'s unfinished test): the truncate runs at the tail and, as a new mid-body pass over the body lines, anywhere a freed lede budget strands the shape ahead of a later paragraph (AutoZone's dateline, Bristol Myers' in-line-products sentence, Centene's summary sentence, Fastenal's product/end-market paragraphs). Only the dangling-intro test runs mid-body — a comma/semicolon fragment there continues on the next line. Separately, `_is_lede_data_row` counts a standalone "$" token as a figure for density: Fastenal's SG&A row reads 4/11 = 36% without it (its 6-word label dilutes the figures under the 40% bar) and 5/11 = 45% with it; the shared `_LEDE_FIGURE_RE` is untouched, and the `dollar_figs` count already accepted spaced "$".

**Blast-radius method:** Highlights + guidance old-vs-new over 1,699 exhibits (`scripts/batch232_cache_blast.py`, `scripts/batch232_guidance_blast.py`, `hidden_files/blast232/`): **34 highlights diffs, 0 guidance diffs**, all verified line-by-line (AZO ×12 dateline truncations; BMY ×1; BRK.B ×7 label+intro cascades; CNC ×1; DIS ×1; FAST ×8 DSR truncations + the 2025-03-31 SG&A row and the freed end-market paragraph; FDX ×3; GILD ×1). The spaced-$ count flipped no prose line to a data row anywhere in the cache.

## 2026-11-11 — Batch 233: the split guidance table — APP ×7 + DD ×4 NULL-guidance rows filled; post-collection `_clean_guidance_result` fuses split rows and drops footnote furniture

**Probe (`scripts/batch233_probe.py`, `scripts/batch233_survey.py`, `scripts/batch233_guidance_blast.py`, `scripts/batch233_guidance_diff.py`):** APP ×7 + DD ×4 NULL-guidance rows were blocked by batch-231 gate G (table fraction ≥ 0.2): their guidance section is a flattened split table — a bare label line, then 1–2 figure-only value lines — plus footnote-definition furniture. DD's section also carries prose CFO quotes after the table (which restate the full-year numbers); APP has no quotes. APP 2024-09-30 has no guidance section at all (pointer release to a shareholder letter, 17 lines) → stays NULL. Survey over 1,699 cached exhibits: 884 non-None guidance, 328 table-heavy/mixed, ≥63 with split label/value rows (crude detector undercounts — DD missed because labels carry "(2)" digits). DB has 1,176 NULL-guidance rows total; CSCO/DAL/FICO/AKAM/APH/ADSK/CRM/EMR/GPC/DLR/BIIB/AAL/ADBE are already FILLED (their stored rows keep old split text; the extractor fix improves live serving on refresh — re-sync of filled rows is carried forward, not this batch's repair).

**Fix (`src/tickerlens/data/filings.py`, +~500 lines):** new post-collection clean step `_clean_guidance_result`, hooked into `extract_management_guidance` just before the `"\n\n".join(...)` (collector untouched). It fuses split rows to `Label: v1 / v2`, or `Label: low – high` when the table's columns are exactly one Low/High pair and the row has exactly 2 figures in one value unit; merges dash-led value lines into the previous unit (`$3.2B` + `- $3.3B` = one range); drops footnote-definition blocks (shared opener via `_footnote_def_block_len` with an optional `opens` parameter, plus a guidance-local relaxed opener for glued keys like `2Includes...` and unterminated bare-numbered wraps like `3 We have not...` — only after a table value/fused row has been seen; drops only if the block terminates within 10 lines or runs to the end of collected lines); drops pure rule lines (`____...`); splits a units line carrying its Low/High headers. It keeps heading, units line, period sub-headings, prose, quotes; normalizes fused rows to `Label: values` when in table context. It refuses ambiguous shapes (leave as-is): wrapped labels (Emerson `Underlying Sales`/`Growth`, split ranges), 3+ value units, wordy values (`Greater than 10%`, `10% average` — Delta stays unfused), period sub-headings kept as section structure. A trailing qualifier moves off the label onto its values (`GAAP diluted EPS >` + `$5.94` → `GAAP diluted EPS: > $5.94`; `... share At least` → `... share: At least $37.20`; `... Up to` → `...: Up to $3.0 billion`; `... Step-up ---` → `... Step-up: --- $0.38` — a standalone dash token is the first column's missing-value marker); a bare month word is never a label (Elevance's `December 31,` date headers stay as-is); a label never opens with a qualifier and always carries an alphabetic word; a fused row's values must carry a real figure (bare `(N)` keys and bare 4-digit years filtered). `_footnote_def_block_len` gained an optional `opens` parameter (default `_opens_footnote_def`); highlights/lede behavior byte-identical.

**Blast defects caught and fixed by the verification loop:** Cigna's comma-separated footnote keys read as figures (`Adjusted Income from Operations, Pre-Tax: 1,2`, `Medical Care Ratio: 2,5`) — fixed by strict thousands grouping in `_GUIDANCE_FIGURE_RE` plus a comma-key guard in `_guidance_value_figures` (a bare digit adjacent to `,\d` is one half of a key, not a figure); qualifier labels (`at least: $7,000`) and a bare `+` label; a prose sentence chopped at its year (`The Cigna Group's: 2025`) — fixed by the verb/qualifier/alpha guards plus the year filter; Centene's dropped `Full Year 2024` period line (the header-token drop now only drops column headers and bare quarter tokens) and its `EPS >: $5.94` qualifier; Elevance's date fragments (`December: 31,`) and qualifier-tailed labels (`... Approximately: ($0.89)`); Emerson's missing-value dashes (`Step-up ---: $0.38`); Moody's `Up to: $3.0 billion`. Two real predicate bugs the unit tests caught: `_guidance_value_figures` matched the halves of `1,2` separately (the strict grouping only stops the whole key matching), and the `\d{1,4}` figure-regex change (needed so a bare year matches as ONE figure and the year filter fires) made a lone `2024` a value line — Emerson's pending header never formed and 4 EMR exhibits unfused until the lone-4-digit guard was added to `_is_guidance_value_line`.

**Blast-radius method:** guidance old-vs-new over 1,699 cached exhibits (`scripts/batch233_guidance_blast.py`, `hidden_files/blast233_guidance_old.json`, `blast233_guidance_new.json`, `hidden_files/blast233_diff.txt`): **226 exhibits changed, 0 gained/lost guidance**, all verified line-by-line (AAL, ADBE, ADSK ×12, AKAM ×12, AMT ×12, APH, APP ×7, BMY ×7, CARR, CCI ×12, CI ×5, CLX ×4, CNC ×7, COIN, CPB, CRM ×10, CSCO ×12, DAL ×10, DD ×11, DG, DGX ×3, DLR ×9, EBAY ×8, ELV ×12, EMR ×12, FICO ×12, FIS ×3, FSLR ×12, GE, GILD ×2, GPC ×3, MCO ×2, META, SKYD, WELL ×11).

## 2026-11-11 — Batch 234: the split-guidance re-sync — stored rows overwritten with the batch-233 fused extraction under a figure-preserving gate

**What:** 111 stored `management_guidance` rows were re-synced to the batch-233 fused extraction (the fuser had only improved live serving until now); the gate is figures(new) == figures(stored) as multisets + no residue in the new text + the stored-vs-new diff must be the batch-233 transform itself (stored == pre-233 blast extraction, or line-by-line explainable: removed lines are rule lines / footnote openers / Low-High-only headers / lines covered by a fused added line).

**Why:** batch 233 changed the extractor but stored rows are only written at seed/fill time, so every company page kept serving the old split text (`Revenue $15.4 billion - $15.6 billion`, `Low End High End` headers, `____` rule lines) until a refresh re-extracted it. A re-sync is safe exactly when the transform is figure-preserving: fusing and footnote/rule-line drops add and remove no figures, so multiset equality plus a no-residue check plus the transform-identity check rules out overwriting a row with a different vintage or section. The 18 diff-figures rows (AMT ×3, where the new extraction adds a whole table the stored row lacks) and the 26 new-residue rows (CRM/DLR/CCI/ELV unfused shapes) are therefore left alone for per-row review and a future fuser-extender batch, not force-synced.

## 2026-11-11 — Batch 235: the fuser-extender — 26 new-residue guidance rows from batch 234, plus 5 more; 66 stored rows re-synced under a two-gate repair

**What:** the batch-233 fuser's residue — 31 stored guidance rows whose new extraction still carried unfused split rows or footnote-definition openers (BMY prior/updated headers, CCI `Site rental billings(c)`, CRM two-range value lines, DAL footnote defs, DD three value lines, DLR three identical value lines, ELV glued footnote digits + bare-number defs, EMR wrapped labels, FSLR `Operating Income (4)`, GE four value lines + interleaved FCF rows). Blast over 1,699 exhibits: 103 changed, 0 figures added, only 5 exhibits lost figures (all footnote keys). Repair re-synced 66 stored rows: gate A (fuse-only, figures equal as multisets) 60 rows + gate B (footnote-drop, figures(new) ⊆ figures(stored) with every removed figure sitting in a dropped footnote/chrome line) 6 rows. Probe re-run after apply: residue rows 31→4.

**Why:** batch 234 re-synced what the batch-233 fuser could already produce, and explicitly deferred the rows the fuser couldn't yet handle. The extender closes that cohort the same way: the transform must be provable before a stored row is overwritten — fusing adds no figures, footnote/chrome drops remove only keys, so the two gates (multiset equality for fuses, subset-plus-chrome-membership for footnote drops) keep the repair from adopting a different vintage or section. The gate's conservatism is deliberate: BMY 2025-03-31/06-30/09-30 stay stored-old because their dropped figures sit in wrapped footnote-def continuation lines the gate doesn't yet recognize as chrome — the live extraction is already clean there, and the stored row is a display lag, not a data error. The `(Reaffirmed)` catch shows the review loop working: the repair dry-run's new-residue skip for BMY 2026-03-31 exposed a live-extraction gap (bare `(Prior)` + `(Reaffirmed)` group not dropped), which the fix then closed before any row was written.

## 2026-11-11 — Batch 236: the wrapped footnote-def continuation gate — 8 stored guidance rows re-synced; unexplained-diff cohort closed

**What:** the 3 unexplained-diff rows from batch 234 (CHD, ELV, EMR) plus the BMY ×3 gate-B-skipped rows from batch 235, and the 2 CARR fuse-only rows batch 235 missed. No extractor change — the live extraction was already clean; only the repair gate needed extending. A footnote definition that wraps across two stored lines leaves its continuation line unexplained (`exchange rates as of April 23, 2025.`, `include AspenTech.`, `year adjustment amount.`), so the gate now recognizes a def-continuation: previous stored line is a footnote-def line ending mid-sentence, the line starts lowercase, and it is not a table row. CHD's dangling `will…` tail is accepted by a new tail-truncation clause (last line, ends with `…`, no figures, not a table row). Gate B's chrome-figure accounting is tightened to removed lines only. Repair re-synced 8 rows (gate A 3 + gate B 5), DB-verified 8/8 byte-identical to the batch-235 extraction, idempotent on re-run; 14 new tests (6 unit + 8 live pins on the frozen blast files). Full suite 2059 passed; 12/12 live routes 200, 10/10 content checks.

**Why:** the gate's job is to prove a stored row may be overwritten, and a wrapped footnote def is provable exactly the same way an unwrapped one is: the opener is already recognized chrome, the continuation carries only the def's own words and figures, and the chain terminates at the sentence's period — so the removed figures (`2025`, `23`, `2`, `3`) are all def furniture, not guidance data. The tightened gate-B accounting closes a real hole in batch 235's version: counting chrome figures over all stored lines could mask a removed figure that also appears in a kept chrome line. The CARR miss is a process note, not a gate failure — batch 235's repair ran against a blast file regenerated afterwards, so the two rows were never evaluated; the batch-236 dry-run caught them because it re-scans every stored row rather than trusting the previous batch's census.

**Related:** Still open: the 27 diff-figures rows (mostly different-vintage sections or lede/anchor changes — AAL's dropped press-release header lines need their own gate); the 4 remaining residue rows (EL/ELV `(Unaudited)` labels — probe false positive; GE interleaved FCF row); SKYD 2026-06-30 NULL guidance (fragmentary date-header table); EMR's 4-word wrapped tail; plus the standing list from batch 234 (345 SKIP-loss rows; 82 first-diff + 10 dateline-mismatch rows; ABNB 2024-12-31; ADBE 2025-11-28 + 2026-05-29; INTC ×6; V 2025-09-30; DG 2024-11-01; JPM's "n "-prefixed bullets; BRK.B Q3 FY2026 mid-body table residue; KHC date-pair header; DLR TOC-only live extraction; ABNB 2023-12-31; CCI 2026-09-30 guidance furniture, LUV tail, SPG/MU/BMY tables, DIS footnote shape, batch-184 list, C slide-deck exhibit, batch-157 gate skips, DASH truncated para; GILD's guidance tail ends on a "Key Updates" heading; AppLovin's "Summary:2" heading wart; wordy value lines as a future fuser class; still-truncated stored highlights rows).

## 2026-11-11 — Batch 237: the header / footnote-drop / tail-furniture re-sync — 13 stored guidance rows re-synced under a new gate D

**What:** the batch-234 diff-figures cohort (27 rows) is now 14. Gate D re-syncs a stored `management_guidance` row when figures(new) ⊆ figures(stored), every removed figure sits in a dropped chrome line or a tail-furniture line (counted over removed lines only), and the stored-vs-new diff is explainable line by line. The 13 re-synced rows: AAL ×3 (release header triple), DAL (footnote-def continuations), ADSK + CHD ×2 + DPZ + GIS ×2 + META + MU + V (press-release furniture tails). The gate extensions live in `scripts/batch236_repair.py` (shared gate code) with the census in `scripts/batch237_repair.py`; the extractor is untouched.

**Why:** batch 236 closed the unexplained-diff cohort and left the diff-figures rows as "different-vintage sections or footnote-def prose drops". The probe showed most of them are not different vintages at all: the new extraction drops press-release furniture the stored row kept (header lines, webcast notices, company descriptions, safe harbors, Reg G definitions, product highlights, corporate-news sections), and the removed figures are all dates, years, times, footnote keys, or product specs — never guidance values. The gate proves that per row: the tail-furniture clause requires the removed run to be a true suffix after a furniture opener, with no $ or % figure and no value line anywhere after the opener (one such line voids the whole tail), and the figure accounting requires every removed figure to sit in a chrome or tail line. The one row where the live extraction is actually wrong — FedEx's `(ETR) ~23%` guidance row, dropped because the footnote opener matches its parenthesized label — is blocked by a new labeled-value guard: a line opening with a non-numeric parenthesized label followed immediately by a value figure is never footnote chrome. Without that guard the gate would have re-synced FDX and deleted a real guidance figure from the stored row, hiding the extractor defect; with it, the defect stays visible and is the next batch's extractor fix (with a blast to bound the opener change).

## 2026-11-12 — Batch 238: the labeled-value footnote false positive — FDX `(ETR)` rows restored under a new gate E

**What:** batch 237's carried-forward extractor defect is fixed in the extractor itself: `_opens_footnote_def` no longer treats a labeled-value line — a parenthesized all-caps abbreviation label followed immediately by a value figure — as a footnote definition. The blast over all 1,699 cached exhibits bounds the change to exactly two guidance diffs (FDX 2026-05-31 and 2026-09-30, each gaining only its `(ETR)` row) and zero highlights diffs. Both stored FDX rows are re-synced: 2026-05-31 under gate D, 2026-09-30 under the new gate E.

**Why:** the footnote opener's paren shape (`_LEDE_FOOTNOTE_RE`) is the loosest marker in the codebase — it matches any parenthesized token — and FedEx's guidance table splits its Effective Tax Rate row as a label line plus an `(ETR) ~23% ...` value line, which is exactly that shape. The batch-176 prose-continuation guard doesn't save it (the rest starts with `~`, not lowercase), so both the collector and the clean step dropped a real guidance figure. The guard is placed in the shared opener rather than the guidance-local one because both drop sites funnel through it, and the blast proves the shared change touches nothing else: mixed-case labels, numeric keys, and single-letter keys are all excluded by construction, and the highlights blast is byte-identical. Gate E is the narrow form of the "new guidance found" gate batch 237 deferred: it re-syncs only when the diff is a pure add, every unexplained hunk is an added labeled-value line, and every added figure sits in one — the added content is exactly the rows the old opener provably dropped, in the same section and table, so it cannot adopt a different vintage. The broad form (AMT/SPG whole-table adds) still needs section verification and stays open.

## 2026-11-12 — Batch 239: the split-currency guidance table + the broad "new guidance found" gate — 15 stored guidance rows re-synced under new gates F and H

**What:** the broad "new guidance found" gate batch 237/238 deferred is now built, plus the extractor defect that made SPG's rows stubs in the first place. Simon's guidance tables split currency and amount into separate cells (`$` / `6.67`), and the collector's admin skip dropped the bare decimal as a "99.1" version fragment — so the per-share ranges never reached the fuser. The fix guards the admin skip (a bare decimal after a lone `$` or a table label is a value, never a version fragment) and merges the split cells before fusing; a lone "End" also drops with its Low/High header now that rows fuse. Gate F re-syncs a row when every removed line is explainable chrome/furniture/re-render and every added line is guidance-shaped, anchored by a shared fused value row (or the figure-less-stub variant); gate H re-syncs a pure remove when the removed lines are the trailing historical-results table. Repair re-synced 15 rows (AMT ×3, CLX, SPG ×7, MCO ×2 under F; GEV ×2 under H), DB-verified 15/15 byte-identical, idempotent; the blast is 8 pure-add guidance diffs and 0 highlights diffs, and both the diff-figures-added and unexplained-diff cohorts are now at zero.

**Why:** the version-fragment alternative in `_ADMIN_LINE_RE` is a document-head rule (`EXHIBIT` / `99.1`), and it has no business firing inside a guidance table — but the guard had to be predecessor-shaped rather than value-shaped, because Elevance's membership table chains bare decimals after plain value lines and keeping those spends the 30-line collection budget and drops the table's tail (caught by the batch-233 pin, which is why the value-line predecessor was reverted the same run). Gate F's section anchor is what makes whole-table adds provable: the new text must share a fused value row with the stored text (same table, same vintage), so the added tables are the section's own continuation, not a different quarter's guidance — and the stub variant covers the rows whose stored text is too thin to anchor (Simon's reconciliation stubs). Gate H is the mirror image: the stored row's trailing table is provably historical (results title + GAAP Metrics + period header + prior-year columns), so dropping it loses no guidance value. The `_figures` counter's `$0.03B` artifact (the figure regex's `\s*` spans blank lines and grabs the next line's initial) is a probe-accounting quirk only — the repair verifies byte-identity against the extraction, so no gate decision rests on it.

**Related:** Still open: the 5 new-residue rows (EL/ELV `(Unaudited)` labels, GE interleaved FCF row, SKYD fragmentary table); EMR's 4-word wrapped tail; plus the standing list from batch 234.

**What:** batch 239's 5 new-residue rows plus EMR's wrapped tail, closed in one batch. Two extractor fixes: the guidance-local footnote opener now matches GE's letter-dash footnote keys (`(a –` / `(b –` — no closing paren), so both definitions drop; and the wrapped-label fragment cap goes from 2 to 4 words, so Emerson's `Acquisition / Divestiture Fees and Related Costs` joins. Three probe-side `_residue` exemptions, all mirroring a decision the extractor already makes: a bare `(Unaudited)`/`(Audited)` line followed by a table-shaped line is a table label, not a footnote opener; a label+value pair the fuser's marked-prev guard refuses (GE's interleaved FCF/conversion rows) is faithful, not residue; a label followed by a 5+-unit value run (SKYD's six-value row) is unfusable by design, not residue. Repair re-synced 7 rows (gate A 6 — EL, ELV ×2, EMR 2024-09-30, GE 2025-09-30, SKYD; gate F 1 — GE 2025-06-30), DB-verified 7/7 byte-identical, idempotent; the blast is 3 intended guidance diffs and 0 highlights diffs, and the new-residue cohort is now at zero.

**Why:** the residue check's job is to catch new text the extractor should have cleaned, and these five rows were the check disagreeing with the extractor rather than the extractor being wrong: the cleaner already kept the `(Unaudited)` labels (their blocks never drop — the next line is a table line, not a plausible definition continuation), the fuser already refused the GE interleave and the SKYD 6-unit row by design (fusing either would misattribute values), so flagging them blocked re-syncs the gates would otherwise have passed. The exemptions mirror the extractor's own guards instead of inventing new ones — the interleaved exemption copies the fuse loop's refuse condition verbatim, and the 5+-unit exemption copies its unit-counting — so probe and extractor cannot drift apart. The two real defects got extractor fixes, not exemptions: a footnote definition that survives collection is an extractor gap (the letter-dash key matched no opener anywhere), and a wrapped label the fuser refuses on an arbitrary wrap point is a cap the blast can bound (3 diffs, all intended, 0 highlights). GE 2025-09-30 passing gate A rather than gate D is the same principle working as designed: the stricter gate (figures equal + explainable diff) already proves the row, because the dropped footnote carries no figures. The `(a –` misread (first regex required a closing paren that the source never has) is why the live pins run before the blast — the pin caught the no-op before any census trusted it.

## 2026-11-12 — Batch 241: the highlights sweep, gate S3 — 55 stored highlights rows re-synced; SKIP-loss cohort 345→192

**What:** the batch 231/232 stale-row sweep's 345 SKIP-loss rows, decomposed by probe into a 103-row census correction and a 55-row gated repair. The `norm()` comparison now canonicalizes all bullet glyphs, trademark spacing, and footnote-key spacing (`$1.64, 1 up` / `basis. 1`), so 103 rows classify as already-new with no write. Gate S3 extends gate S's residue with four chrome classes — dateline sentence (extractor's own predicates plus the two-comma dated form the extractor's dated regex misses), period subheading, table-section header (guarded by the next-line-is-table condition), section subheading (guarded by the next-line-is-kept condition) — plus a dateline-presence exemption that fires only when live carries no dateline at all, with the check itself using the same dateline notion on both sides under norm comparison. Repair re-synced 55 rows (AAL ×6, ABT, ALL ×8, AVGO ×8, AZO, BBY, CARR, CHTR ×8, CL ×5, CVS, DIS ×2, EL ×2, FAST ×4, FCX, FTNT, GILD ×5), DB-verified 55/55 byte-identical, idempotent; SKIP-loss is now 192 and dateline-mismatch is at zero.

**Why:** the loss cohort was three different things wearing one label, and each needed its own treatment rather than a looser gate. The spacing-only rows were a comparison bug, not stale data — the seed-era extractor emitted bullets without the space, and `norm()` only knew about `•`. The chrome rows were the gate disagreeing with the extractor: the live extraction already drops dateline sentences, period subheadings, and table headers by design, so recognizing them as droppable in the gate is the same proof as the batch-233/234 guidance gates, just for highlights. The two guards on the heading rules (next-line-is-table, next-line-is-kept) are what keep the gate from eating real content: Air Products' `Creating shareholder value` looks exactly like CVS's `CEO Commentary` until you check what follows it. And the dateline check had to be fixed, not just extended — collecting datelines with one regex while excusing them with another let a different-exhibit row through, which the new negative pin now locks in. The still-blocked 192 stay blocked for the right reason: Visa's prose and Amazon's business bullets are content the live extraction does not carry, and no gate can prove an overwrite safe when the content genuinely differs — that is a section-selection design decision for a later batch, not a gate extension.

## 2026-11-12 — Batch 242: the highlights sweep, gate S4 — 52 stored highlights rows re-synced; first-diff cohort 82→16

**What:** the 82 SKIP-first-diff rows from the batch 231/232 stale-row sweep, decomposed by probe into stored-leading chrome (bare date / salutation / contact block / masthead), headline wrap/prefix, single-line flattening, and spacing-only — and repaired under gate S4, which replaces gate S3's entry condition with a chrome strip + headline alignment (JOIN / PREFIX / OFFSET) + the Corteva single-line split, while every other gate-S3 condition stays unchanged. `norm()` gains three per-line spacing rules (space before comma/period, spaced ordinals, footnote key spaced off a word or "%"). Repair re-synced 52 rows (AGNC ×6, AMT ×7, AWK, BKNG, CTVA ×8, DD, DGX ×2, DKS ×4, DRI ×2, ECL ×8, EL, ET ×2, GD ×8, NFLX), DB-verified 52/52 byte-identical, idempotent; first-diff is now 16, SKIP-loss 204, and AXP_2025-12-31 flips to already-new under the new norm rules.

**Why:** the first-diff label was four different things wearing one name, and each needed its own entry treatment rather than a looser gate — the same lesson as batch 241's loss decomposition. The chrome rows were the gate disagreeing with the extractor (the live extraction never carries a release-date line, a contact block, or a masthead, so a stored row that opens on one is not a different exhibit); the wrap/prefix rows were the gate's suffix-only first-line relation missing the other direction (a stored headline truncated at a clause boundary is a prefix of the live headline, and a two-line wrap joins to it); the CTVA rows were a seed-era flattening the gate compared line-by-line against a multi-line live text. The ordering inside the alignment is load-bearing: JOIN must be tried before PREFIX because a wrapped headline is also a prefix, and only JOIN consumes the continuation line — PREFIX alone would leave it for the loss check to (correctly) reject, which is how the DGX rows would have stayed blocked. The loss check behind the new entry is what keeps the still-blocked rows blocked: a different exhibit's bullets carry different figures (AGNC's two Q4 rows), and a dropped quote / repurchase bullet / strategic bullet is a real loss, so the entry can be generous about shapes without ever adopting wrong content. The "%"-preceded footnote key ("12.5% 3") was the same class as the word-preceded one — the probe's char-level diff found it, and the rule's preceding-character class grew by one character rather than by a new rule. The one genuinely new live-table refusal (DRI 2026-08-30, 7/13 live lines residue) is the gate working as designed: the live extraction for that quarter carries the flattened same-restaurant-sales table, so swapping to it would serve a table, not highlights.

## 2026-11-12 — Batch 243: the orphaned AMT contact role line — telephone lines count as contact evidence in the block-skip lookahead

**What:** the batch-242 wart is fixed at the extractor: American Tower's contact block (`Contact: Adam Smith` / `Senior Vice President, Investor Relations and FP&A` / `Telephone: (617) 375-7500`) no longer strands the role line as the headline of every AMT quarter. The `_skip_contact_block` lookahead now treats a line carrying phone/contact evidence as a following line even when it matches `_ADMIN_LINE_RE`. Full-cache blast: 12 diffs, all AMT, all intended (role line out, real headline in, one extra tail bullet each). Repair re-synced 8 stored rows under gate S4 (DB-verified 8/8 byte-identical, idempotent); first-diff is now 15, loss 205. The first-diff probe closes the cohort: the remaining 15 rows are all section/exhibit selection (AGNC full-year vs quarter figures, APO table vs lede, COIN slide junk, CSCO body prose vs bullets, NFLX content bullets vs letter bullets) — no gate can prove those swaps safe.

**Why:** the bug was two individually-correct rules colliding. The telephone alternative in `_ADMIN_LINE_RE` is correct on its own (a bare telephone line is admin, never headline material), and the batch-151 admin-skipping lookahead is correct on its own (a "FOR IMMEDIATE RELEASE" line between a contact name and its phone must not consume a lookahead slot). But the lookahead's job is to find contact evidence, and the telephone line IS the evidence — skipping it as admin removed the only signal that the content-shaped role line above it was contact detail. The fix restores the lookahead's intent with the narrowest possible change: evidence lines are never admin-skipped, non-evidence admin lines still are. The blast proves the scope: 1699 exhibits, 12 diffs, one ticker, one shape. And the gate did the rest of the proving — AMT 2024-09-30 moved from first-diff to loss instead of being repaired, because its stored India-sale prose is real content the live extraction drops; the loss check is what keeps a good extractor fix from becoming a bad overwrite.

## 2026-11-13 — Batch 244: quarter-scoped Highlights sections beat full-year ones; gate S5 closes the first-diff cohort

**What:** the highlights sweep's first-diff cohort (15 rows) is closed — 20 stored rows re-synced in total (the 15 plus 5 FY-stored rows the extractor fix exposed). Two changes: (1) the extractor prefers a quarter-scoped Highlights section over a full-year-scoped one when the quarter candidate financially qualifies — the anchored-section refactor carries each section's heading alongside its content, and the scope rule (quarter word beats full-year word; combined headings count as quarter) drops full-year candidates only in that case, so FY-only releases are untouched; (2) gate S5, the wrong-section gate — s5-table (APO's flattened deck tables), s5-narrative (CSCO's detail prose), s5-disclaimer (COIN's forward-looking-statements boilerplate), s5-wrong-section (NFLX's content-slate section + footnote furniture), and s5-fy-stored (CART/ANET/EBAY/FTNT rows whose stored text IS the doc's full-year section, verified against the doc's own anchored sections, not a figure heuristic). Full-cache blast for the extractor fix: 5 diffs, all intended (AGNC ×2, CART ×2, ANET ×1); repair DB-verified 20/20 byte-identical, idempotent; first-diff 15→0, SKIP-loss 206→203; full suite 2194 passed.

**Why:** the first-diff label was hiding two different mistakes. The AGNC/CART/ANET rows were an extractor ranking bug: a Q4 release carries two financial Highlights sections and the density ranking picked the fuller one — the full-year section — because it has more figure lines, even though the row is a quarter and the quarter's figures are the answer. Density is the right tie-break between sections of the same scope, but scope has to come first: a quarterly row should never show annual figures when the quarterly section exists and qualifies. The other rows were a gate blind spot, not an extractor bug: the stored text was never highlights at all (a deck table, detail prose, a disclaimer, a different letter section), so no chrome-strip or headline-alignment could ever make the first lines agree — the gate needed a positive shape match for the stored text plus the same clean-live checks, with the loss check deliberately set aside because the dropped lines are, by construction, not highlights content. The s5-fy-stored class is the proof standard for that trade: it does not guess from figures, it checks the stored text against the document's own full-year section. And the batch-197 CART test update is the honest part of the change — the old test pinned a 12-line window that straddled the FY/Q4 boundary and served both sets of figures; serving both was a rescue artifact from when the alternative was nothing, not a decision that annual figures belong on a quarterly row.

## 2026-11-13 — Batch 245: bare "Fiscal YYYY" is a full-year scope word; the loss cohort is a coverage question, not a gate problem

**What:** the 203-row SKIP-loss cohort decomposed (probe, not a gate): ~912 lost lines across 7 shapes, but only 4 rows are chrome-only (APLD's non-GAAP-definition boilerplate) — 199 rows lose genuine content, mostly Amazon's business bullets, executive quotes, and letter prose the live extraction never carries. The one concrete extractor defect the decomposition surfaced: Autodesk's annual section is headed "Fiscal 2025 Financial Highlights", a bare "Fiscal YYYY" the batch-244 scope regex never matched, so the denser full-year section won over the quarter section and the Q4 row served annual figures. The fix adds `\bfiscal\s+\d{4}\b` to the full-year scope regex (quarter check still first; bare year alone still "other"); the full-cache blast moves exactly one row (ADSK Q4 FY2025, SKIP-loss → in-sync), full suite 2198 passed, live-verified on the Q4 detail page (Q4 figures served, 0 FY figures).

**Why:** the decomposition changed what the loss cohort is. Batches 241–244 treated loss as "stored chrome the gate doesn't recognize yet", and each gate (S3/S4/S5) was a new chrome class — that program is done: the remaining 202 rows lose content, not chrome, so no further gate extension can prove those swaps safe. The next step is a design decision, not a gate: whether the highlights extraction should serve the executive quote that follows the lede (ADI's 7 quote-only rows are the cleanest test case — live is otherwise byte-identical to stored) and whether it should also capture a release's business-bullets section (Amazon's 8 rows). Both would change what the app serves on many pages, so they need a blast-sized justification of their own, not a quiet gate. The scope fix itself is the batch-244 principle applied to a heading form the regex didn't know: scope before density, and the blast (1 diff, intended, no mirror-image rows) is the proof the form is unambiguous in practice. The ADSK non-Q4 rows also surfaced a second real predicate gap — the all-caps-month dateline ("SAN FRANCISCO, MAY 22, 2025 -- ...") matches no dateline predicate — but widening it alone repairs 0 rows (every carrier also loses quotes), so it rides with the quote decision instead of shipping as a no-op fix.

## 2026-11-13 — Batch 246: the executive quote after the dateline is served — 85 stored highlights rows re-synced; SKIP-loss 202→189

**What:** the batch-245 design question, decided by blast rather than by stored-row precedent. The highlights extractor now serves the executive quote that immediately follows the dateline: `_leading_bullet_block` pulls the quote run (consecutive executive-quote lines, at most 3) after the dateline sentence that closes the bullet item, then ends the block. The quote predicate is strict — quote-first (`“...,” said X, CEO`) or attribution-first (`CFO X added, “...”`), the quote mark opens within 140 chars and closes, so Ford's flattened mega-lines (a quoted `“adjusted”` buried deep inside) never qualify. The dateline test is local to the pull-in (`_is_quote_pull_dateline`): the shared dateline regexes miss four real forms — glued dashes (ADI), all-caps months (Autodesk), parenthesized dates (Deckers), two-comma city/state/date (DGX/CVS) — and widening the shared regexes would be wrong, because the block breaks on a dated dateline mid-block and a matching dateline would drop out of the block instead of riding in as a continuation. Full-cache blast: 85 rows change (72 in-sync rows gain their quote, 13 loss rows re-sync), every added line a genuine executive quote (89 quote lines total; probe3 verifies 83/85 rows quote-only, the 2 DGX exceptions being the headline-join artifact the S4 gate already handles), 0 rows lose content. Repair re-synced the 85 rows under the existing gate S4 (DB-verified, idempotent — dry re-run finds 0 S4 rows); post-repair census is B 1001, SKIP-loss 189. Full suite 2205 passed; 12/12 live routes 200 with quote content checks on ADI/DGX/AVGO detail pages.

**Why:** the seed rows could not settle this — they are split (ADI/Deckers/DGX carry the quote, Broadcom/Dollar Tree/Elevance/GD do not), so "stored says so" pointed both ways and the decision had to be made on product grounds with a blast-sized justification. The quote is the release's own summary of the quarter and the most quotable line a retail investor reads; serving it is strictly more informative, it is doc-verified rather than derived, and the blast bounds it (85 rows, 1–2 lines each, no other diffs in the whole cache). The S4 gate blesses the swap in the right direction: its loss check is one-directional (no stored line lost, no table added), and the added lines are the doc's own quote lines, so the repair is a content addition the extractor proves, not a chrome excuse. The 3-line cap and the immediacy requirement (quote must directly follow the dateline) are what keep the rule from becoming a general "serve the quote anywhere" change: Deere's quote sits one prose line past the dateline and stays out, Palantir's sits before a Highlights section and stays out (section path wins), Ford's sits inside flattened mega-lines and stays out by design. The dateline forms stay local to the pull-in for the same reason the all-caps-month gap was left alone in batch 245: the shared predicates are load-bearing in the headline loop and the block's break logic, and a form that matches there changes what the block collects, not just what it recognizes.

## 2026-11-13 — Batch 247: gate S6, the doc-aware trailing-furniture gate — ADSK ×6 re-synced; SKIP-loss 189→183

**What:** the batch-246 carry-forward, closed in one batch. Two changes: (1) a norm rule — spaces around `--` canonicalize only when a 4-digit year precedes the dash, so the seed-era `2025 -- Autodesk` dateline equals the live `2025-- Autodesk` one; (2) gate S6, the trailing-furniture gate — a stored row may end on a bare period label (`First Quarter Fiscal 2026`) plus the results-table units header, provided the run verifies against the source document (consecutive doc lines after live's last line, table data immediately after). The six ADSK rows re-synced under it (DB-verified 6/6 byte-identical, idempotent); the norm blast flips zero rows.

**Why:** the two blockers needed different treatments. The dash spacing is a comparison artifact, not a content difference — but the first attempt (a general ` -- ` collapse) broke two batch-244 tests, because the S5 narrative classifier keys on the `Label -- prose` marker. The year anchor is what makes the rule safe: a label-dash line carries no digits before its dash, so the anchored form can never touch one — the full suite caught the over-broad form before it shipped, which is the test suite doing its job. The trailing run needed a gate rather than a residue extension because the units header is already S3 residue (a table line) while the label is not, so the tail is a mix — and the doc verification is what makes the gate honest rather than shape-only: a `First Quarter Fiscal 2026` heading over prose live drops would not sit directly before table data, so it could never pass. The first S6 draft failed 5 of 6 rows in dry run for exactly this mix reason (it demanded the unexplained lines be the whole tail); the rescue now takes the maximal not-in-live suffix and checks each line as furniture-or-residue, which is the same proof stated correctly.

## 2026-11-13 — Batch 248: gate S7, the spacing-only swap — 15 stored highlights rows re-synced; SKIP-loss 183→168

**What:** the batch-247 carry-forward, closed in one batch. The APLD rows were not boilerplate-only — the stored boilerplate line is present in live too, and the rows differ from live by spacing only (spaces inside the curly quotes, `Re cent`, `M acquarie`). Gate S7 is gate S4 with a spacing-equivalence rescue in the loss check, applied symmetrically: `spacing_equivalent(a, b)` requires the same non-space character sequence AND the same digit runs, with the difference confined to spaces. The 15 re-synced rows: APLD ×6, AXP 2026-06-30 (`JD Powe r`), CRM 2024-10-31 (`Y /Y`, `an d`), CRWD ×2 (`Equivalen ts`, `com pared`), DE 2026-08-02 (trailing quote space), EQIX 2025-06-30 (`IBX® )`), ET ×3 (`wer e`, `Strategi c Highlights`). DB-verified 15/15 byte-identical under norm, idempotent; full suite 2221 passed.

**Why:** the probe changed what the APLD rows were. Batch 245 bucketed their lost lines as boilerplate, but the char-level diff shows the boilerplate line itself is in live — the loss was the comparison, not the content: seed-era stray spaces inside words and quotes that `norm()` never canonicalized. A `norm()` rule could have fixed the quote spaces, but the split words cannot be fixed in a per-line comparison — `Re cent` vs `Recent` needs the live line as the reference, and a blind "join a short fragment to the next word" rule would merge real words (`a test` → `atest`). The gate is the right instrument because it is pairwise: it only ever equates a stored line with the live line it actually corresponds to, and the two conditions are the whole safety argument — identical non-space characters means no word or figure changed, identical digit runs means no space can ever merge two numbers (`10 0` vs `100` is refused even though the characters match). The symmetric coverage check is the other half: without it, live could add a bullet under the spacing cover, and the pre-existing S4 post-checks would not catch a prose addition. The `a ward` vs `award` boundary is the honest limit of a spacing-only comparison and is stated as such in the tests — it is not a figure, and the gate's other conditions are what keep such a line from blessing a swap on its own. DE 2026-08-02 closing here also retires the batch-245/246 "prose between dateline and quote" worry for that row: the row re-syncs, so the quote is served and nothing else is lost.

## 2026-11-13 — Batch 249: the pre-section executive quote — 24 stored highlights rows re-synced; SKIP-loss 168→165

**What:** the highlights extractor now serves the executive quote that sits immediately before a Highlights section heading, when the quote run is anchored by a dateline. Palantir's releases put the CEO's quote between the dateline and the `Q# YYYY Highlights` heading, so the section path wins and the quote is never collected — the batch-246 pull-in only fires inside the leading-bullet block, which these releases never open. `_prepend_pre_section_quote` walks back over the consecutive quote run, requires a dateline before it, and serves the first 3 lines in document order; it runs post-selection, so the density competition and the trim judge the section alone. The blast moves exactly 24 rows (ADBE ×6, ANET ×2, CRWD ×4, CSX, EBAY ×3, PLTR ×8), all re-synced under gate S4, DB-verified 24/24 byte-identical, idempotent; probe 3 proves every added line is a quote line except on the 3 truncated PLTR seed rows, where the section's remaining bullets ride along (stored carried only 2 of 12). Full suite 2229 passed.

**Why:** the batch-246 decision (serve the quote — it is the release's own summary) already covered the quote-after-dateline shape; this is the same content class in the one position the pull-in cannot reach. The dateline anchor is what makes the rule safe rather than a general "serve quotes anywhere" change: the probe-2 blast surface is 122 exhibits with a quote before an anchor, but the anchor splits them cleanly — the dateline-preceded cohort is served, and the bullet-preceded (Amazon), footnote-preceded (Chevron), attribution-preceded (Equinix), and undated-dateline (Biogen, Datadog) shapes stay out by construction. The cap-ordering fix is the same lesson as batch 247's S6 rescue: the first implementation capped the backwards walk and stranded the dateline check on the uncollected fourth line, so a four-line run prepended nothing — capping after reversal serves the first three in document order, the ones right after the dateline, exactly as batch 246 does. And the anchored-sections signature change is why the full suite runs before any repair: the triples broke the batch-244 repair script's 2-tuple unpacks, and its 3 tests caught it.

## 2026-11-14 — Batch 250: gate S8, the trailing-guidance swap — the doc check, not the shape, is the proof

**What:** the three BIIB loss rows are re-synced under a new gate S8 (`scripts/batch250_repair.py`). Each stored row equals the live extraction exactly, plus a trailing run that opens the release's guidance section — `Full Year YYYY Financial Guidance`, the intro sentence, and the first rows of the guidance table. The gate requires: an exact stored-prefix match, a 1–5 line tail opening on a full-line guidance heading, no bullet in the tail, clean live by the S5 checks, and doc verification — the tail lines appear consecutively in the source document right after live's last line (bare page-number lines skipped), with a guidance-table continuation line immediately after. Dry run re-syncs exactly the 3 BIIB rows; repair DB-verified 3/3 byte-identical, idempotent; SKIP-loss 172→169; full suite 2242 passed.

**Why:** the probe changed what the BIIB rows were before any gate was written. Batch 245 bucketed their lost lines as content, but the char-level decomposition shows the stored rows are live plus the next section's opening — a seed-era over-inclusion, not highlights live drops. The alternative (an extractor change to serve the guidance head) was rejected on the batch-222 trim's own terms: the trim deliberately ends highlights at the guidance section, and serving the guidance head would duplicate guidance into highlights for every ticker with a guidance section. The gate is the right instrument because it is pairwise and doc-verified: the shape checks alone (a guidance heading as the first tail line) would bless any stored row whose tail happened to start with one, but the consecutive-doc-run plus table-continuation check proves the tail IS the guidance section's opening in this document — a guidance heading over prose live drops could never pass, because the line after it would be prose. The page-number skip is the same lesson as batch 247's S6 rescue: the first draft of the verification demanded the tail immediately follow live's last line, and Biogen's exhibit pagination (`3` between the last bullet and the heading on two of the three rows) would have failed it — the skip is narrow (bare 1–3 digit lines only) and the continuation check still anchors the run to the table. The truncated-prefix rule (Rule B) dying at 0 rows is the honest part of the probe: Ford's stored table line is longer than live's, and its footnote bullet differs in its lead-in, so no prefix rule could ever prove those swaps safe — the F rows stay blocked until their two sub-defects (the S5-table phrase gap, live's own table-line carry) are addressed directly.

## 2026-11-14 — Batch 251: gate S9, the summary-heading swap — 4 stored highlights rows re-synced; SKIP-loss 169→165

**What:** the batch-250 carry-forward, decomposed by a fresh census rather than by the named list alone. All 7 CHD rows share one shape — stored carries the release's two summary-table column headings ("YYYY <Quarter> Results" + "YYYY Full Year Results|Outlook") and only the first 2 bullets, live drops the headings and serves 4 bullets — and the shape probe finds exactly 4 rows where the lost lines are only those two headings. Gate S9 (`scripts/batch251_repair.py`) blesses the swap when the lost lines are exactly one quarter heading + one full-year heading, the added lines are 1–4 bullets at live's tail, stored-minus-headings equals live-minus-added in order, live is clean by the S5 checks, and the doc check proves the headings sit consecutively immediately before the first stored bullet with the added bullets following the last stored bullet consecutively. The 4 rows re-synced under it (DB-verified 4/4 byte-identical, idempotent); post-repair census is B 1032, SKIP-loss 165. Full suite 2255 passed; 12/12 live routes 200 with bullet content checks on all four re-synced CHD detail pages.

**Why:** the probe changed what the CHD rows were before any gate was written. Batch 245 bucketed their lost lines as table furniture, which is true of the headings — but the same probe shows the other three CHD rows lose a genuine sub-headline each, so the cohort is not uniform and the gate must be written for the 4-row subset, not the 7-row ticker. The doc check is what makes the gate honest rather than shape-only: the headings are proven to be the summary table's column headings sitting directly above the bullet run, and the added bullets are proven to be the run's continuation — a period heading over prose live drops could never pass, because the lines around it would not be the run's bullets. The alternative (an extractor change to serve all 10 summary bullets) was rejected for this batch on the budget's own terms: live stops at 4 bullets because the 6-line lede budget counts the headline + 2 sub-headlines first, and widening that budget is a cache-wide change that needs its own blast — the gate certifies the swap to what live produces today, and the remaining 6 bullets are carried as a live-side budget item, not silently blessed. The 3 blocked CHD rows are the honest limit of the same reasoning: the ≤4-word `_is_lede_heading_fragment` rule eats short all-caps sub-headlines ("ANNOUNCES 4% DIVIDEND INCREASE", "TOUCHLAND ACQUISITION COMPLETED") even though they carry real news, and the 2026-OUTLOOK line's drop is a second mechanism the probe has not yet attributed — a swap on those rows would lose news, so they stay blocked until the fragment rule learns a sub-headline guard. The trace work that found the fragment rule also settled the mechanism question for the headings: `_is_lede_title_line` drops them (heading-shaped, capital-led, ending in a section word, no headline verb), which is correct behavior for table headings and is why no extractor change ships here.

## 2026-11-14 — Batch 252: the sub-headline guard — short sub-headlines survive the lede filters; SKIP-loss 165→162

**What:** an extractor fix, not a gate (`src/tickerlens/data/filings.py`): the ≤4-word `_is_lede_heading_fragment` rule now spares a line carrying a $/% figure, or — at ≤3 words — a completed-event statement (COMPLETED, added to `_LEDE_HEADLINE_VERB_RE`); and a new `_is_lede_percent_range_statement` exempts %-range guidance statements from `_is_lede_data_row` and serves them budget-free in the lede loop (the %-range mirror of the batch-183 $-range exemption). The full-cache blast moves exactly the three CHD rows (loss → S9) plus four pure-addition rows (BBY/DKS/DUK/EL, in-sync → S4) and moves no row to loss; the repair re-syncs all 7 (DB-verified 7/7 byte-identical, idempotent); post-repair census is B 1035, SKIP-loss 162. Full suite 2264 passed; 13/13 live routes 200 with content checks on all seven re-synced detail pages.

**Why:** the batch-251 blockers were both extractor defects, and the fix had to be earned against the blast, not assumed. The first attempt — the 5–8-word band's verb guard applied flat at ≤4 words — regressed three cohorts in the blast, and each misfire taught a guard that is now in the shipped code: (1) a 4-word verb line reads as a heading ("AFFIRMS LONG-TERM TARGETS", "Q1 EPS EXCEEDS OUTLOOK", "UPDATES FULL YEAR OUTLOOK"), so the verb guard stops at completed events (COMPLETED) — a heading names a topic, it never states that an event is complete — and at ≤3 words; (2) a bullet is never a range statement (Edison's "● Reiterates ... 5%-7% ..." bullet was served budget-free and let the dateline paragraph in, moving EIX ×8 to loss); (3) a negative value's minus is a sign, not a range connector (Fastenal's "2.02 -0.6 %" rows rode the lede as statements). The %-range exemption's ≥4-word bar is the same argument the $-range exemption made in batch 183: a table row's label is short ("Net Sales Growth (0.5%) - 0.5% 1.5% - 3.5%" has 3 words), a guidance statement's subject is not — and the density rule's figure counting is what made the exemption necessary at all, since a hyphenated range counts as two figures. The two older tests that pinned the old behavior were updated, not deleted: the EL semicolon test now pins the line present and uncut (it is no longer last because the guidance line is served), and the S9 refusal test now rides on the still-dropped AFFIRMS line — both updates name batch 252 in their comments. The detail-page `quarter` param takes a label ("Q4 FY2025"), not a date; the first live-verification round "failed" on date values that silently default to the latest quarter — a wrong URL, recorded here so the next batch does not re-litigate it.

## 2026-11-14 — Batch 253: the black-square bullet + gate S10 — DLR ×7 re-synced; SKIP-loss 162→155

**What:** an extractor fix plus a new gate. (1) `src/tickerlens/data/filings.py`: "◾" (U+25FE) joins `_BULLET_LEAD_RE` — Digital Realty renders its Highlights bullets with it, and the glyph was missing from the class, so two $+%-carrying ◾ bullets plus the two $+%-carrying "Financial Results" narrative lines tripped the data_rows >= 4 threshold in `_looks_like_financial_table`, the genuine 7-bullet "Highlights" section was rejected as a financial table, and the lede fallback served the supplement TOC (DLR 2026-03-31: live 220 chars of TOC junk vs 1638 stored). (2) `scripts/batch253_repair.py`: gate S10, S4 with the S7 spacing-equivalence rescue in the loss check. The fix restores the section, but live differs from the seed-era stored rows by normalization drift (spaced curly possessive "Digital Realty ’ s", a lone zero-width-space line) plus one added genuine narrative line — S7 refuses the addition, S4 refuses the drift, S10 accepts the combination. Full-cache blast moves exactly the 7 DLR rows (loss → s10) and no row to loss; the repair re-syncs all 7 (DB-verified, idempotent); post-repair census is B 1042, SKIP-loss 155. Full suite 2272 passed; 13/13 live routes 200 with content checks on all seven re-synced detail pages.

**Why:** the defect had to be attributed to the section rejection, not the TOC skip. The first suspect was `_skip_toc` — it never fires on DLR's first TOC heading because "Exhibit 99.1" matches `_ADMIN_LINE_RE` and breaks the scan before any page number is collected — but patching that direction returns None (the headline pass lands in the supplement's "Corporate Profile" prose and the release is lost entirely), while the stored rows prove the section path produces the right output once the section qualifies. The section rejection is the batch-154 defect class with a new glyph, and the fix is the same shape as batch 154's: the bullet guard must see the markers the exhibits use, or figure-dense bullet lists read as tables. The gate follows the same logic: S10's loss side is proven by spacing-equivalence (identical non-space characters, identical digit runs — a figure change like $707M → $708M is refused, pinned by test), and its addition side is S4's existing policing (an added table row is refused, pinned by test). The older batch-225 test that pinned the TOC-junk wart was updated, not deleted: it now pins the real section served. The "Table of Contents" headline wart stays on all 8 DLR rows — live and stored agree on it, and fixing the headline pick for supplement packets is a separate batch, not smuggled into this one.

## 2026-11-14 — Batch 254: gate S11, the section-heading-label swap — DHR ×7 re-synced; SKIP-loss 155→148

**What:** a new gate, no extractor change. `scripts/batch254_repair.py`: gate S11, S4 with the section-heading-label rescue in the loss check. The DHR ×7 rows — batch 253's named next-cleanest cohort — share one uniform shape: stored is tagline, headline, dateline, the bare "Key <Quarter> <Year> Results" heading, 3 bullets; live is tagline, headline, dateline, the same 3 bullets, + 1 genuine content line (the CEO-quote paragraph in 4 rows, the first full-year bullet in 2024-12-31, a 4th Key Results bullet in 2026-03-27 / 2026-06-26). The heading pattern is exact — `^Key (First|Second|Third|Fourth) Quarter \d{4} Results$` — a label with no figures. Full-cache blast moves exactly the 7 DHR rows (loss → s11) and no row to loss; the repair re-syncs all 7 (DB-verified 9/9 DHR rows byte-identical to live, idempotent); post-repair census is B 1049, SKIP-loss 148. Full suite 2280 passed; live detail pages for three re-synced quarters verified (quote / full-year bullet / cash-flow bullet present, heading absent).

**Why:** the heading is chrome, not content, and the extractor already treats it that way everywhere else. It repeats the quarter the headline already names, carries no figure, and the section path strips section headings for every other company — keeping it would be a DHR-only exception rather than a fix. The swap is a 1-for-1 trade of a redundant label for a real content line, so it is strictly more informative, and the gate's exact pattern is what keeps it narrow: a heading carrying a figure does not match and still refuses as loss (pinned by test), the same way batch 247's year-anchored dash rule kept a general collapse from touching the S5 narrative classifier. The addition side needs no new policing — S4's fraction / added-table / tail / fragment checks already bound it, and the blast proves the bound (non-DHR census byte-identical). The alternative — teaching the extractor to keep the heading — would reintroduce a label the section path deliberately drops, for one ticker.

## 2026-11-14 — Batch 255: gate S12, the stored-truncation swap — CEG ×3 + EQIX ×2 re-synced; SKIP-loss 148→143

**What:** a new gate plus a two-rule norm fix, no extractor change. `scripts/batch255_repair.py`: gate S12, S4 with the stored-truncation rescue in the loss check — a stored line live does not carry exactly is excused only when it ends with "…" (the seed-era truncation marker), the text before it is ≥80 chars, and it is a strict prefix of a live line that is strictly longer. The cohort: CEG 2025-06-30 / 2025-12-31 / 2026-06-30 each lose exactly one line (the "Nuclear Operations" bullet / capacity-factor line cut at "…") while live carries the same line to its end plus one genuine "Natural Gas, Oil, and Renewables Operations" bullet; EQIX 2024-12-31 / 2025-09-30 are the same shape, once the norm fix glues the spaced "TM" trademark marker ("Exchange TM" → "ExchangeTM", "Intelligence TM" → "IntelligenceTM") and the space before a closing bracket ("(IBX® )" → "(IBX®)"). The norm-only census is byte-identical to before (the rules move no row on their own); the full-cache blast moves exactly the 5 rows (loss → s12) and no row to loss. The repair re-syncs all 5 (DB-verified 5/5 byte-identical to live, idempotent); post-repair census is B 1054, SKIP-loss 143. Full suite 2290 passed; live detail pages for all five re-synced quarters verified (full lines + added bullets present).

**Why:** the "…" is the seeder's own truncation marker, so the stored line is a prefix of the live line by construction, never a different sentence — the swap trades a cut-off line for its own full text plus one genuine bullet, so it is strictly more informative, and the prefix test is exact, not fuzzy: a live continuation that changes a figure (45,170→45,171) is not a prefix and still refuses (pinned by test). The 80-char floor is what keeps headings and labels out of the rescue — the smallest rescued base is 117 chars — the same way batch 247's year anchor kept a general collapse from touching the S5 narrative classifier. The norm rules are the same spacing-only family as batch 242's (® spacing, space-before-comma/period): every non-space character is preserved, so two lines that differ in content still differ after norm, and the capitalized-preceding-word anchor on the TM rule is load-bearing — a standalone word "TM" ("TM Forum", "TM mode") is never preceded by a capitalized mark word, so it can never be glued (corpus-checked across all 2,526 stored rows + the full blast cache; every " TM" is a trademark marker). The DASH 2025-06-30 control case is the proof the gate stays narrow: its "In Q2 2025, Total Orders…" line IS a truncated-prefix shape, but its other lost line runs the reverse direction (live's line is a prefix of stored's — live drops the cohort-definition tail), so the row still refuses as loss. F ×6 is the same reverse direction throughout (char-level diff: live's mega-line is a strict prefix of stored's, 1,722 of 3,870 chars), and the stored tail holds real segment narrative and outlook — a prefix rule in that direction would bless genuine loss, so F stays loss. The alternative — teaching the extractor to serve truncated lines in full — is already what the extractor does; the stored rows are seed-era artifacts, so a gate + re-sync is the fix, not an extractor change.

## 2026-11-15 — Batch 256: post-selection sub-section bullet-list completion — 34 stored highlights rows re-synced; SKIP-loss 143→142

**What:** an extractor fix, no new gate. `src/tickerlens/data/filings.py`: the winning Highlights section's stranded sub-section bullet list is completed post-selection — the collector records the tail (`_collect_sublist_tail`: consecutive bullet lines past the 12-line cap, at most 4, skipping admin/page-footer lines, stopping at the first non-bullet) and returns it as a 4th tuple element, and `_extract_highlights_section` appends the winner's tail after the batch-177 drops, bounded at 16 total lines, before the join/trim steps. The cohort: BKR 2024-12-31 (the Q4 section serves 6 Q4 bullets + the "Full-year highlights" label + 5 of 7 FY bullets; the cap cuts the cash-flows $3,332M and returns-to-shareholders $1,320M bullets) plus 33 more rows whose winners gain genuine bullets the same way (DDOG ×2: Forbes/Bits AI/Archive Search/Internal Developer Portal bullets; ET ×5 + ET 2026-03-31 via S10). The blast moves 33 rows in-sync→s4 and 1 row loss→s10, and 0 rows to loss; the repair re-syncs all 34 (DB-verified 34/34 byte-identical to live, idempotent); post-repair census is B 1055, SKIP-loss 142. Full suite 2295 passed; live detail pages for the re-synced quarters verified (DDOG/ET content checks present).

**Why:** the cap strand is the same wart the batch-222/224 completion allowance already fixes for sentences, in list form: a sub-heading collected as a label line inside the section means the list after it is the section's own content, and serving 5 of its 7 items is a truncation, not a bound. The form matters more than the fix: two earlier forms were tried and both regressed the blast, and each regression taught a constraint now in the shipped code. (1) The broad in-collector continuation (any bullet past the cap) moved in-sync 1054→970: AMD's plain 12-bullet list pulled its 13th bullet — an "including:" opener stranded without its sub-bullets — and a later section grew past the quarter section and flipped the density ranking (AGNC ×7). The sub-heading guard (the tail after the last Highlights sub-heading must be all bullets) fixed AMD/AGNC but (2) the still-in-collector form flipped Welltower 2024-12-31: its Q4 section grew from 11 to 13 figure lines and passed its Annual section, so the winner changed even though the guard held. The final form follows the batch-177 principle the code itself states — nothing about a candidate changes before selection, because the ranking judges the candidates as collected. The tail is recorded where the cap stopped (only the collector knows that exactly) and applied to the winner only, after selection, so the ranking is computed on the unchanged capped sections and cannot flip; Welltower stays in-sync, and the only rows that move are the winner's own pure additions, which gate S4 already polices. The 16-line total bound is the same bound the sentence allowance already uses, and the tail appends before the join/trim steps so a tail ending on a dangling opener is trimmed by the same rule that drops GEV's stranded quote. The alternative — raising the section cap — was rejected for the same reason the 12-line cap exists: it bounds runaway collection, and the sub-list completion is the targeted repair for the one shape where the cap demonstrably truncates (a headed sub-list already being served), not a general widening.
