## 2026-11-15 — Batch 257: AMT 3-quarter backfill + census fast-path fix — in-sync 1056→1059; SKIP-loss 142 unchanged

**What:** a data backfill plus a probe-methodology fix, no extractor change. (1) `scripts/batch257_seed.py`: AMT re-seeded with `periods=12` through the production path (`fetch_and_persist` XBRL upsert + `_enrich_press_release_highlights` for the blank rows). AMT's stored window started at 2024-09-30 because it was seeded with the default `periods=8` ending 2026-06-30; the three missing quarters (2023-12-31, 2024-03-31, 2024-06-30) now land with financials and 7-bullet highlights, all in-sync with the cached exhibits, and the 9 pre-existing rows are unchanged. (2) `scripts/batch257_probe.py`: the census fast path (`norm(stored) == norm(live)` → in-sync) moves inside `classify()`, so the gate chain never runs on an identical pair. Post-seed census is B 1059, SKIP-loss 142. Full suite 2299 passed; live detail pages for all three new quarters verified (Q4 FY2023 "2,787 million" ×3, Q1 FY2024 "2,834 million" ×3, Q2 FY2024 "900.3" ×3).

**Why:** the gap was a window artifact, not an extraction defect — the exhibits were cached and extract cleanly, so no gate or extractor change could have filled it; only a wider seed window could. The 12-quarter window is the minimal one covering the gap (latest 2026-09-30 back to 2023-12-31), and the re-upsert is provably safe: the before/after snapshot shows the 9 existing rows byte-identical on revenue, net income, EPS and assets. The probe fix matters because the phantom regression it caused is exactly the kind of false signal that would have sent a future batch chasing a defect that does not exist: S4's policing checks are properties of the live text (a lowercase second line, a contact line that reads as a table), not of a stored-vs-live difference, so they must never fire on an identical pair. Putting the fast path inside `classify()` rather than in the caller is what makes it testable — the two fast-path tests fail under the old gate-chain-only classify, which is the proof the pin holds. AMT 2024-09-30 stays loss deliberately: its stored row is truncated mid-quote with no "…" marker, so S12's truncation-prefix rescue does not apply, and live's additions include quote prose — blessing that would trade a truncation swap for quote loss, the same design question that keeps BKR 2024-12-31 and BNY ×8 open.

## 2026-11-15 — Batch 258: DLR "Table of Contents" headline wart fixed — real headlines served, 8 rows re-synced

**What:** an extractor fix plus a targeted re-sync. (1) `_skip_toc` in `src/tickerlens/data/filings.py` now consumes an exhibit-marker line ("Exhibit 99.1" / "EXHIBIT-99.1" / the "ECHIBIT" typo / ticker-prefixed) as TOC material instead of breaking the scan on it — the marker is packet front-matter, and it was the single line that kept the DLR skip from ever running. (2) The headline loop gains an `after_toc` guarded scan: after a TOC skip, long lines and non-headline-shaped lines are skipped rather than breaking with headline=None, and only a headline-verb + results-topic line is picked, so the supplement's decoy titles ("Key Quarterly Financial Data", the "Unaudited, Dollars ..." units line, the "DLR" / "Series J Preferred Stock:" stock listing) are passed over; the single-word-fragment join gets the same requirement. (3) The dateline branch skips instead of breaking in the after-TOC state, so a dateline-shaped table row in a supplement-only packet (EOG) cannot become the break point that serves a table section as highlights. (4) `scripts/batch258_repair.py` re-syncs the 8 stored DLR rows under a strict condition (stored first line exactly "Table of Contents" + live body byte-identical); the batch-225 test that pinned the wart now expects the real headline. Full-cache blast moves exactly the 12 DLR exhibits; full suite 2305 passed; post-repair census B 1059, SKIP-loss 142 unchanged.

**Why:** the wart was a false headline — worse than no headline, since the highlights card titled eight quarters of real results "Table of Contents". The fix had to serve the real headline, not just delete the false one: the first attempt (marker consumption alone) left headline=None and the rows bullet-only. The guarded scan is the minimal way to reach the release section through supplement front matter — verb+topic is the established headline shape in this codebase (batch 148's narrative-tail rule), and it is exactly what separates "Digital Realty Reports Second Quarter 2025 Results" from every decoy ahead of it. The dateline guard exists because the scan otherwise wanders supplement-only packets until a false dateline trips it; EOG proved the hazard in the same blast (three exhibits flipped from honestly-None to a wrong table-section headline before the guard, byte-identical after it). The repair's strict body-identity condition is what makes an 8-row overwrite safe without running the gate chain: nothing but the headline line changes, verified per row.

## 2026-11-15 — Batch 259: lede bullet-run extension to the 12-line cap; S9 tail bound recalibrated 4→10; 121 stored rows re-synced; CHD backfilled to 12 quarters

**What:** an extractor fix plus a gate recalibration, a targeted re-sync and a seed. (1) The lede loop in `src/tickerlens/data/filings.py` now completes a bullet-list lede to the 12-line section cap: while the next line is a bullet and the last collected line is one too, collection continues past the 6-line budget; a non-bullet past the budget still falls through to the finish logic, so prose ledes keep the hard cap. A colon-ended bullet is refused as a sub-list opener (its ◦ items are not recognized bullets; collecting it let the overrun path pull the sub-list plus the next section heading). (2) Gate S9's tail bound moves 4→10 in `scripts/batch251_repair.py` — the old bound was calibrated to the 6-line budget and refused exactly the re-syncs the extension enables. (3) `scripts/batch259_repair.py` re-syncs the 121 S4 rows (batch-256 pattern, idempotent). (4) `scripts/batch259_seed.py` backfills CHD's 3 pre-window quarters through the production path (9 pre-existing rows byte-identical on financials). Full-cache blast moves 190 exhibits, all pure bullet additions (1278 lines, 0 removals); full suite 2314 passed; final census B 1059, SKIP-loss 145 (the +3 are EIX ×2 and BRO 2026-09-30, gate-policing artifacts diagnosed in `scripts/batch259_loss.py`, not extractor defects).

**Why:** the 6-line lede budget was designed for prose ledes (dateline + quote), but a bullet-list lede is the release's own summary — cutting CHD's 10-bullet list at 4 lost the whole FY-outlook group. The extension is shaped like the section cap (12 lines) rather than a new budget, so the bound the codebase already trusts does the work; the colon guard exists because the first blast showed the overrun path turning an opener into a heading leak (EL's "BEAUTY GAINS AND OPERATIONAL HIGHLIGHTS3"), and the guard is the minimal fix — refusing the opener ends the lede at the last complete bullet instead of teaching the overrun path about sub-lists. The S9 recalibration follows the same logic as the extractor change: a gate bound calibrated to an extractor budget must move when the budget moves, or the gate silently dies for the cohort it was built for (the CHD rows). The repair's 121 rows are all S4-blessed pure additions, and the census proves the blast moved nothing else (124 rows moved, 121 to S4, 3 to loss, 0 elsewhere).

## 2026-11-15 — Batch 260: S4 policing fixes — leading-chrome strip on the live side; shared-second-line fragment exemption

**What:** two fixes in `sweep_gate_s4` (`scripts/batch242_repair.py`), both closing the 3 rows batch 259 left in loss. (1) The added-table scan strips leading chrome from the live lines too — the gate already stripped it from stored, so EIX's leading contact line "Investor Relations: Sam Ramraj, (626) 302-2540" counted as live-only table material because its phone number matches `_is_lede_data_row`; the added EPS bullet itself is `hard_table`-False, correcting batch 259's misattribution to the bullet's parenthesized $ range. The strip is front-only, so a contact line added mid-text still trips added-table (pinned by test). (2) The fragment refusal is exempted when live's second line equals stored's second line post-alignment — BRO's lowercase "including total revenues ..." is a headline continuation both sides share, not a live-only fragment; a genuinely new lowercase second line still refuses (pinned by test). `scripts/batch260_repair.py` re-syncs the 3 rows (BRO 2026-09-30, EIX ×2), DB-verified byte-identical to live, idempotent; census B 1060→1063, total skips 145→142. Full suite 2319 passed.

**Why:** both refusals were properties of the text, not of the addition — the gate was policing its own chrome handling and its own headline shape. The fixes are symmetric with what the gate already does (strip chrome from stored; align the headline) rather than new exemptions: the added-table scan now compares like with like, and the fragment check now asks whether live actually starts somewhere stored does not. The full-census simulation proves the narrowness — fix A moves exactly EIX ×2, fix B exactly BRO, nothing else — and DIS ×4 stay refused because their added ◦ sub-bullets are genuinely table-shaped, which is the control that keeps the chrome fix honest. The batch-257 fast-path pins are updated rather than deleted: S4 no longer refuses those identical-pair shapes at all, and `classify()` still says in-sync, so the fast path remains as the safety net for the next policing shape.
